## 🆕🔴🔴 THE EXECUTION RISK ARRIVES FROM INSIDE: MUSK REPLACED THE DATA-CENTRE LEADERSHIP AFTER CIVIL-ENGINEERING AND RELIABILITY PROBLEMS AT THE TENNESSEE AND MISSISSIPPI SITES — AND THE STATED CAUSE IS THE BUILD SPEED THAT IS THIS PAGE'S CENTRAL BULL CASE (2026-09-01)

**(The Information · Grace Kay, EXCLUSIVE, 2026-09-01, 11:45 and 13:08 ET — two sends of the same story)**

- 🔴🔴 **THE REPORT, VERBATIM: *"**Elon Musk has SHAKEN UP the SpaceX team building the company's data centers in recent weeks, REPLACING SEVERAL LEADERS with executives from SpaceX's ROCKET AND SATELLITE INTERNET businesses**, people familiar with the matter said. The data center reshuffle follows some **CIVIL ENGINEERING CONCERNS and CONSISTENT RELIABILITY ISSUES at SpaceXAI's existing data center facilities in TENNESSEE and MISSISSIPPI**, two people said. During its early AI infrastructure build-out, **SpaceXAI had RACED to put those data centers together quickly, leaving facilities RUNNING FOR MONTHS WITHOUT BACKUP COOLING AND POWER SYSTEMS in place**, four people said. **That's increased the RISK OF OUTAGES**, the people said."*** The Information's own takeaway box: *"**SpaceX replaced data center leaders amid reliability concerns. The AI unit has TOUTED ITS CONSTRUCTION SPEED AS A MAJOR ADVANTAGE. AI revenue surged to $2.6 billion in Q2 2026 from compute rentals.**"*
- ➤ 🔴🔴🔴 **WHY THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT NEGATIVE DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE SINCE THE MAIDEN PRINT, AND IT IS NOT ABOUT ONE OUTAGE. The entire 10GW-by-YE27 argument on this page rests on a build-speed advantage — *"closer to 10GW than 5GW by YE27"* against 1.4GW online at end-April. This report says the speed was achieved in part by **commissioning facilities without backup cooling and power**, that it produced *"consistent reliability issues"* at two named sites, and that the response was to **replace the leaders and staff the function from the rocket and Starlink organisations.** That is a direct challenge to the *quality* of the GW number, not the quantity — and it lands while the company is selling multi-year compute contracts to [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[GOOG]], counterparties for whom uptime is the product.**
- ⚠️ **WHAT IT DOES *NOT* SAY, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERREAD: no capacity figure is retracted, no customer is named as affected, no SLA breach or credit is reported, and the reshuffle is framed as remediation. Sourced entirely to unnamed people familiar (two-to-four per claim); SpaceX is not quoted. **Not a company disclosure.**
- 🔴 **THE THINGS TO WATCH THAT WOULD CONFIRM OR KILL IT: (a) whether the **1.4GW → 2GW+ YE26** and **5-10GW CY27** markers move at the next print; (b) whether any offtaker discloses availability credits — recall [[CRDO]]'s covering analyst cites *"SpaceX disclosing plans to scale compute capacity from **2GW+ in CY26 to 5-10GW in CY27**"* as an unmodelled revenue wildcard for its own coverage, so a slip propagates; (c) whether the **<1yr payback** claim survives capex for the backup systems that were skipped.**

## 🆕🔴 THE SAME WEEK, THREE HOUSES PUBLISHED ON SPCX AND ALL THREE ARE CONSTRUCTIVE — INCLUDING THE ONE THAT DEFENDS *HOWMET* AGAINST SPACEX (2026-08-31 / 09-01)

- **MS · Adam Jonas / William Tackett — OVERWEIGHT, PT $300** (*"IDEA: SpaceX: The Rowdy Robot: What's the Catch? Bots on the Bayou"*, 2026-08-31, 7pp): *"Adam Jonas and William Tackett discuss the **overlooked significance of an eventual STARSHIP CATCH** and how the **newly announced LOUISIANA STARBASE makes their forecast for LAUNCH PAD ASSUMPTIONS appear so CONSERVATIVE.**"* ⚠️ **The note as delivered is a summary plus a video link — no revised numbers, and the PT is unchanged at $300. Logged for the rating/PT mark and the two named drivers.**
- 🔴 **Bernstein · Doug Harned — SPCX UPDATE, and the framing is that Starship, not AI, is the near-term driver** (via TMT spec sales · Tyler Seidman, 2026-09-01): *"Harned updates his thoughts after August developments… **STARSHIP RAMP IS THE MOST IMPORTANT N[EAR]-TERM DRIVER.** Doug highlights **six topics from August** for SpaceX and their implications: **1) Starbase Louisiana announcement. 2) Upcoming launches heading toward reuse. 3) FOUNDRY TO PRODUCE IGT BLADES AND VANES. 4) Grok, Cursor and [[OPENAI]]. 5) NEW TERAFAB SITE. 6) Airline Starlink dominance continues.**"* ⚠️⚠️ **NOTE THE DIRECT CONFLICT WITH 22V, WHICH IS ALREADY ON THIS PAGE: Bernstein calls it a foundry for **"IGT"** blades and vanes; 22V argued specifically that the fleet SpaceX actually operates (GE TM2500 GEN8 units, the mobile packaging of the LM2500, descended from the CF6 aircraft engine) points to a *"measured start in **AERODERIVATIVE** blades and vanes rather than a full-scale Industrial Grade Turbine threat initially."* **Same week, same event, opposite class of turbine. This is the single most load-bearing unresolved technical question in the foundry thread, because the addressable market, the yield problem and the incumbent damage all differ by class.** BOTH LOGGED, NEITHER ADOPTED.**
- ✅ **AND THE HOWMET DEFENCE — WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT IS THE BEAR CASE ON SPCX'S FOUNDRY, WRITTEN BY A BULL ON SPCX (same analyst, same morning): *"Doug Harned thinks weakness on SPCX competitive concerns is **OVERDONE… Elon has TALKED ABOUT THIS FOR 6+ MONTHS as he's looking to VERTICALLY INTEGRATE, NOT BECOME A DIRECT COMPETITOR.** Doug sees **little risk to Howmet** given its dominant market position, long-term agreements, and strong demand visibility through the end of the decade. While SpaceX is likely to pursue vertical integration to meet its own power needs, **Doug remains SKEPTICAL IT CAN SCALE PRODUCTION QUICKLY ENOUGH to impact Howmet**, making the stock's recent weakness a buying opportunity."*** ➤ **So Bernstein is OW SPCX and simultaneously says SPCX cannot scale the foundry fast enough to matter to the incumbents. Those are compatible — but only if the 18-month acceleration claim is wrong, which is exactly what Jefferies' ~4+ year decomposition and MS's own 45-60+ month build estimate also imply. **Three houses now bracket Musk's 18 months at 4-5 years.**
- **A NAMED, DATED FORUM TO ADJUDICATE IT: **Jefferies "JEF Access: Gas Turbine Landscape Debate on SPCX/HWM/GEV and More" — Tuesday 2026-09-08, 3:00pm ET**, a Zoom webinar bringing together Jefferies' Power/Utilities lead (Julien Dumoulin-Smith), Aerospace & Defense lead (Sheila Kahyaoglu) and Paul Zimbardo *"to assess the broad implications of SpaceX expansion into casting of blades and related equipment for natural gas turbines… **We intend to host a SURVEY during the debate and recap the results together live**."* ⚠️ *"This session will NOT be recorded"* — attendance-only. **Also diarised: MS's *"SPCX Morgan Stanley Lunch: How to Play the Space Economy ft Adam Jonas, Sean Diffley, and William Tackett"* — 2026-09-10, NYC, 12:00 ET.**

## 🆕🔴 TERAFAB IS NOW A NAMED DRIVER OF THE *INDUSTRY* WFE FORECAST — AN OUTSIDE-IN CONFIRMATION THAT THE FAB IS BEING TAKEN SERIOUSLY (2026-09-01)

- **UBS · Timothy Arcuri raised his 2027/2028 WFE forecasts from $198bn/$248bn to **$225bn/$275bn**, with 2029 biased above $300bn — and named SpaceX among the four largest contributors to the 2028 step-up: *"For 2028… **foundry and logic spending becom[es] the dominant driver, accounting for roughly 75% of the projected $50 BILLION year-over-year increase. Major contributors include [[TSM]], [[INTC]], Samsung Foundry, and notably SPACEX'S NEW TERAFAB, which could emerge as a MAJOR SOURCE OF INDUSTRY SPENDING over the next several years.**"* (relayed by UBS sector sales · Robert Ruple, 2026-09-01).
- ➤ 🔴 **THIS IS THE FIRST TIME A SELL-SIDE *INDUSTRY* FORECAST ON THIS WIKI HAS BUILT SPACEX INTO THE DENOMINATOR. It is an independent, quantitative vote of confidence in the Terafab — from an analyst with no SPCX equity coverage and no incentive to flatter it — and it sits alongside Bernstein listing the *"new Terafab site"* among its six August topics. ⚠️ **UBS attaches no dollar figure to the SpaceX share of the $50bn, and "over the next several years" is not a 2028 commitment. Do not convert this into a capex line.** Cross-filed [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[ASML]], `themes/semicap-wfe.md`.**
- **The capacity markers this page carries, restated together for the record because two independent third parties now cite them back: **AI revenue +247% y/y with $2.6bn in Q2 2026 from compute rentals; 1.4GW online at end-April; 2GW+ by YE26; *"closer to 10GW than 5GW"* by YE27; power/cooling ambitions of 15-20GW; Colossus/Colossus 2 driving $1.6bn of incremental cloud revenue with a further $6.7bn contracted in early 3Q ([[GOOG]], [[ANTHROPIC]]); <1yr payback** (Jefferies · Sheila Kahyaoglu, 2026-08-31; JPM · Joe Cardoso, 2026-09-01). ⚠️ **The 15-20GW figure is rendered as *"by YE26"* in the Jefferies relay, which cannot be reconciled with 2GW+ at YE26 in the same paragraph — almost certainly a relay error for a later year. FLAGGED AND NOT USED.**
- ⚠️ **THE [[ANTHROPIC]] CONTRACT FIGURE IS ALSO UNRESOLVED ACROSS SOURCES: GS puts Anthropic's SpaceX commitment at **$45bn**; The Information says *"earlier this year, it agreed to pay SpaceX **$40 billion** for compute **through 2029**"*. Both logged; do not average. Re-mark from SpaceX's own disclosure.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 THE BLADES & VANES FOUNDRY IS CONFIRMED BY MUSK HIMSELF — AND HIS 18-MONTH ACCELERATION CLAIM SITS AGAINST MORGAN STANLEY'S OWN 45-60+ MONTH BUILD ESTIMATE (2026-08-29 / 08-31)

**This resolves the reverse-engineered thesis in `## THE POWER BOTTLENECK GETS A NAME` above. MS · Adam Jonas built that entire 08-16 note out of four job postings and an FTC early-termination notice, with the explicit caveat that *"SpaceX has announced nothing."* Musk has now confirmed the substance publicly. The construction of the thesis was right; the TIMELINE is where the disagreement now lives.**

- 🔴 **THE REPORT (The Information · Ann Davis Vaughan, 2026-08-29): *"SpaceX has taken steps to develop a **foundry in Bastrop, Texas**, about 30 miles from Austin, **to make vanes and blades for large gas turbines**, according to job listings and other indicators."* Net-new detail beyond the four April postings MS used: SpaceX is also *"looking for a programmer to assist in the milling of parts in **high-temperature superalloys** at SpaceX's new **industrial gas turbine (IGT) blades and vanes manufacturing line in Bastrop**"*, and a *"**senior tooling engineer to design castings**."* Framing: *"Clues are emerging that Elon Musk intends to **bypass the power supply chain for AI data centers in a way others assumed was impossible**… It's a move no one else has been bold enough to attempt."* **SpaceX did not return requests for comment.**
- 🔴🔴 **MUSK CONFIRMED ON X (2026-08-30, relayed 2026-08-31): *"By doing **in-house casting [of vanes and blades] at SpaceX, we can accelerate nat gas turbines coming online by up to 18 months**, which is a **profound game-changer**."*** He separately restated the constraint — ***"The limiting factor for nat gas turbine production is casting the blades & vanes"*** (@The_AI_Investor relaying Musk, corpus, 2026-08-29) — and said explicitly that **natural gas will be needed to *"supplement and bootstrap solar"* for several years** (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-31). ➜ **The "three massively backlogged casting companies" argument this page already carries from Musk's February Dwarkesh/Collison interview is now the stated operating rationale, from the CEO, on the record.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE TIMELINE DISAGREEMENT, AND IT IS NOW A HARDER NUMBER THAN THE ONE THIS PAGE CARRIED: the MS TMT desk notes that **HWM and DPC both traded off ~5% pre-market on the report, *"but casting facilities take 45-60+ MONTHS to complete"*** (Morgan Stanley TMT desk, 2026-08-31). **That is a material tightening of MS Research's own 08-16 framing — *"it may be 2-3+ years before the first volume production for SpaceX"*, with *"minimum greenfield-to-first-qualified-production… two years; full run-rate three or more."* 45-60+ months is 3.75-5+ years.** ➜ **THE TWO NUMBERS ON THIS PAGE ARE NOT COMPARABLE AND MUST NOT BE NETTED: Musk's **18 months** is a claimed ACCELERATION OF TURBINE DELIVERY once castings are available; MS's **45-60+ months** is TIME TO BUILD THE FOUNDRY THAT MAKES THEM. Read sequentially they put the payoff at ~2030-31 at the earliest — which is AFTER the 10GW-by-YE27 ambition this page is built around, and consistent with MS Research modelling only **5GW by YE27**. Prior MS value moved to `## Changelog`.**
- ✅ **THE DUAL-USE ARGUMENT IS RESTATED BY JONAS AND IT REMAINS THE STRONGEST PART OF THE CASE: Jonas *"believes SpaceX is standing up a Texas turbine-component foundry for **both its data center needs AND its Raptor turbopumps**"* — *"**Raptor turbopump castings and data center turbine airfoils draw on the same alloys, furnaces and trained workforce, letting a single foundry spread fi[xed costs]**"* (via The Information + MS desk, 2026-08-29/31). **Consistent with the 08-16 "one foundry, two customers" framing; nothing new but nothing withdrawn.**
- ⚠️ **THE EXPERT DISAGREEMENT IS NAMED ON BOTH SIDES AND IS UNRESOLVED: *"Debates continued to rage between traditional energy experts who asserted that **powering hyperscale data centers off grid is far harder than it looks** and AI bulls like **Dylan Patel of SemiAnalysis, who stated his belief that Musk could pull it off.** '**This is because you have no idea how to build infrastructure**,' **Jigar Shah** — the clean-energy entrepreneur who ran the Biden energy loan office — shot back at Patel on X."* **Logged as a genuine unresolved dispute between named practitioners, not as colour.** **Market context from the same piece, and it is the bear datapoint: *"**SpaceX shares plunged after the [2Q] call on fears about what it would all cost.**"* Musk *"said earlier this month that he had **secured [[NVDA]] chips for these future sites**."*
- 🔴 **THE POLICY/EMISSIONS LEG, WHICH IS THE UNDERRATED RISK AND IS QUANTIFIED FOR THE FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE: BloombergNEF *"has estimated that if planned off-grid gas plants for data centers come to fruition, they would **boost U.S. carbon dioxide emissions 20%**."* That estimate **EXCLUDES** the foundry effort but **INCLUDES 2 GW of off-grid gas that SpaceX's xAI unit has said will be running by the end of the year** at its Memphis-area data centres. *"Musk's potential to unlock even more fossil fuel power for AI data centers would represent a setback to low-carbon energy advocates who had hoped the supply crunch for turbines would encourage renewables."*** ➜ **A 20% national CO2 increase is a political number before it is an environmental one. The leading indicator: NY Governor Hochul is *"explicitly pro-AI but taking a harder line on the physical and social externalities… especially hyperscale data centers"* — framed as *"force companies to internal[ise]"* rather than *"stop AI"* (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-31).**
- **THE SUPPLY-CHAIN READ-THROUGH LIST, from the market rather than a house: on Musk's own naming of **transformers, wiring, liquid cooling, massive chillers and complex networking** as the AI-compute point of failure — *"harder than finding power"* — the corpus names **AAON, [[NVT]], MOD, IESC, MTRS, JCI, HPS.A, PRY.MI** and, on networking, **[[CRDO]] / [[AAOI]] / [[LITE]] / [[COHR]]** (@aleabitoreddit, corpus, 2026-08-30). ⚠️ **An independent/retail read-through list, NOT a broker basket — logged for orientation only.** **Consistent with MS Research's 08-16 conclusion that this is a cost/schedule play and not a merchant-supply business: *"we would NOT expect SpaceX to be a material supplier of blades & vanes… OUTSIDE OF SPACEX USE"* — so the read-through to [[GEV]] and the power chain stays on the DEMAND side.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 THE FOUNDRY TIMELINE GETS A THIRD, INDEPENDENT MARK — JEFFERIES AT "~4+ YEARS" WITH A DECOMPOSITION — AND 22V SUPPLIES THE VARIABLE EVERY OTHER NOTE OMITTED: *WHICH CLASS OF TURBINE* (2026-08-31)

**The section above left the page with two non-comparable numbers (Musk's 18-month acceleration vs the MS desk's 45-60+ month build). Two houses published the same afternoon and BOTH advance it: Jefferies converges on the MS build estimate from an independent supply chain, and 22V Research reframes the whole question around aeroderivative-vs-IGT, which changes what "breaking the casting moat" would even mean.**

- 🔴🔴 **JEFFERIES IS THE THIRD MARK AND IT LANDS ON TOP OF MORGAN STANLEY'S — WITH A BUILD, WHICH IS WHAT THE PAGE LACKED (Jefferies · Sheila Kahyaoglu, "Musk 'Confirms' SPCX's In-House Turbine Blade Foundry; **+4 Yrs Is Our Timeline**", 2026-08-31, relayed by Julien Dumoulin-Smith).** *"In our view, the short-end of potential **time-to-market is likely ~4 years**, as DPC has highlighted standing up a new facility requires **up to 12 mos of customer negotiations, up to 24 mos to industrialize, and up to another 24 mos to ramp production & improve manufacturing yields**."* ➜ **~4 years is the SHORT end and it is built from an incumbent's own stated stage-gates, which is why it is more useful than a range: MS's 45-60+ months (3.75-5+ yrs) and Jefferies' 48+ months now agree from two independent channels. The 18-month figure remains a claim about TURBINE DELIVERY once castings exist, not about the foundry.** ⚠️ **One asymmetry Jefferies does not price and 22V does (below): the 12-month customer-negotiation stage does not apply to CAPTIVE production.** **Jefferies on why yields are the hard part: *"Yields should not be under-estimated, where HWM's Whitehall site **does not even allow customer tours and all paper has to be shredded**."*** **Site facts, net-new: Bastrop *"opened in 2023 and produces tens of thousands of Starlink kits per day and manufactures all current-gen Starlink Standard and Performance terminals"*, and **SpaceX reportedly acquired ~830 acres near the existing site between March & June**.**
- **THE OLIGOPOLY IS NOW SIZED AND SPLIT, AND TWO HOUSES INDEPENDENTLY AGREE ON THE EXPOSURE MATH.** Jefferies: Engine Products *"could be as large as **$10BB in 2025 revenues**, having grown at a **High-Teens CAGR since 2021** & including **only four competitors: Howmet Aerospace ($4BB), Precision Castparts (~$3BB+), Consolidated Precision Products ($1.5-2.0BB), and Doncasters ($0.7BB)**."* **Exposure: Jefferies puts DPC's IGT exposure at 42% of 2025 revenues vs HWM's 11% — 22V independently gets the same split (*"IGT is ~42% of Doncasters' $837M 2025 revenue"*; HWM gas-turbine incl. oil & gas *"~11% of 2025 revenue (~$0.9B of $8.3B), against a commercial-aerospace franchise near ~52% of sales"*).** **Two products: turbine airfoils (blades/vanes — highest temperatures and loads, nickel-based superalloys, life-limited, aftermarket replacement) and structural castings (stationary, engine-life).**
- 🔴🔴 **22V IS THE ANALYTICAL ADVANCE OF THE DAY: THE FLEET SPCX ACTUALLY OWNS IS *AERODERIVATIVE*, NOT IGT — SO THE FIRST STEP IS SPARES FOR ITSELF, NOT AN ASSAULT ON THE CASTING OLIGOPOLY (22V Research · Dauvin Peterson, "SPCX Strategy — Considerations on the Turbine Component Story", 2026-08-31).** *"Musk purchased **APR Energy (SPCX is the purchaser per Musk) in May**, which had **100 MW+ deployed against ~1.1 GW of owned capacity** (an **850 MW base plus eight turbines added in January 2026**), built on **GE TM2500 GEN8 units of up to ~35 MW each**. Notably, the **TM2500 is the mobile packaging of GE's LM2500, which descends from the CF6 AIRCRAFT ENGINE** — so the parts a SpaceX foundry would most logically cast for this fleet are **AERO-HERITAGE AIRFOILS, NOT IGT PARTS**."* ➜ ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS THE VARIABLE THE MS AND JEFFERIES NOTES BOTH LEAVE OUT, AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS. Bull: a captive aeroderivative spares line is far easier and faster than an IGT merchant business — it sidesteps Jefferies' 12-month customer-negotiation gate and 22V's *"multi-year OEM qualification of rotor-critical parts, which **SpaceX can sidestep for captive use by SETTING ITS OWN ACCEPTANCE STANDARDS**."* Bear: it is also far SMALLER, and none of the read-through to [[GEV]], Siemens Energy or FTAI follows from it — *"SpaceX does not currently operate this class of turbine at their data centers."* The market traded the IGT story; 22V says the evidence supports the aero story.**
- 🔴 **THE STEP FROM AERO TO IGT IS A PROCESS WALL, NOT A SCALE-UP: *"Single-crystal yield falls off SHARPLY as the casting grows and IGT ceramic cores are more prone to SHIFT OR CRACK under a bigger, longer pour."*** **The moat 22V says cannot be bought quickly, in three parts: *"**single-crystal casting yield**, which depends on decades of trade-secret process know-how to hold first-pass yield at commercially viable levels; **in-house ceramic core production**, which the incumbents deliberately own rather than outsource (Doncasters frames its edge as 'owning our own supply chain'); and **multi-year OEM qualification** of rotor-critical parts."*
- 🔴 **"THE 'CORE' OF THE ISSUE" — THE SHARPEST TECHNICAL POINT ANY SOURCE HAS MADE ON THIS THESIS, AND IT DIRECTLY QUALIFIES THE DUAL-USE ARGUMENT THIS PAGE HAS BEEN CARRYING FROM JONAS: *"SpaceX's capabilities overlap with turbine casting on metal alloys via Raptor engine development, but blades and vanes require **CERAMIC CORES** as well. **Raptor gives real transfer on the METAL — superalloy melt, single-crystal solidification, the furnaces, the trained workforce — but LITTLE on the CORED-AIRFOIL specialization.** Turbine blades and vanes run for thousands of hours and require intricate internal cooling passages formed by a ceramic core (with a separate ceramic shell forming the outer airfoil shape). **Core-making is a specialty process, and the incumbents own it in-house as part of the moat.**"*** ➜ **The "one foundry, two customers" synergy is REAL on the metallurgy and ABSENT on the cores. That is the single most load-bearing qualification on the dual-use case this page carries, and it should be read alongside it rather than instead of it.** **If SPCX outsources the core, 22V names the beneficiary: *"Morgan Advanced Materials (MGAM LN), whose **Certech** unit is the leading merchant ceramic-core supplier for both aero and IGT airfoils."***
- 🔴 **THE ONLY HARD ECONOMICS ON THE VERTICAL-INTEGRATION CASE COME FROM THE CORPUS, NOT FROM A HOUSE — AND THEY ARE A BUILD-COST NUMBER: relaying The Information, *"自建500兆瓦機組估約10億美元，外購則約20億美元"* — **building a 500MW unit in-house is estimated at ~US$1bn versus ~US$2bn if purchased externally** (@QQ_Timmy, corpus, 2026-08-30). ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD (this wiki's recurring failure mode): that is US$2.0/W of BUILD COST vs US$4.0/W purchased — CAPEX per watt, NOT revenue per watt. It must never be netted against the *"over US$40bn per GW for Vera Rubin vs US$25bn for Grace Blackwell"* figure on [[NVDA]]/[[MU]], which is REVENUE per GW. Different quantities entirely; chaining them produces nonsense.** ➜ **Taken at face value it says self-build halves genset capex — a ~50% saving on the power layer, which is the first quantified prize behind the whole foundry effort. Single, second-hand source; treat as indicative.**
- **WHY 22V THINKS THE TAPE OVERREACTED: *"Today's sell-off across select power capital-goods names… **may be overdone**… Connecting the dots — current fleet, Musk's own framing, the time to stand up single-crystal casting, and the depth of the incumbent casters' integrated moat — would point to a **smaller impact and initiative than related equities reflected**."* Names hit: **GEV, ENR GR, Doncasters, HWM, FTAI**. On FTAI specifically: *"FTAI's negative reaction is less clear as the company is **engine constrained** and would not have any obvious negative impact from SpaceX in this case."*** **Tape marks: [[GEV]] −1.5% *"on news Elon was going to begin producing turbines"* (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-31); Barron's ran *"GE Vernova Stock Drops. Is SpaceX Coming for Them Too?"* (@barronsonline, corpus, 2026-08-31); HWM and DPC both −~5% pre-market on the 08-29 report (MS TMT desk).** ⚠️ **Musk's own stated long-term orientation is 22V's structural argument against an IGT step-out: *"toward solar and battery installations and ultimately ORBITAL DATA CENTERS. While natural gas is fueling the rapid scaling, a step out into heavy-duty IGT manufacturing looks unlikely as a strategic priority."***
- **THE 2Q OPERATING NUMBERS JEFFERIES RESTATES, FOR THE RECORD:** *"SPCX AI revenues increased **+247% y-o-y**, with **1.4GW online**, including **2GW+ YE26**, and **closer to 10GW than 5GW by YE27**. Power/cooling ambitions target **15-20GW**. Colossus/Colossus 2 drove **$1.6BB incremental cloud revs**; **additional $6.7BB contracted in early 3Q ([[GOOG]], [[ANTHROPIC]])**."* ⚠️ **The $6.7bn of early-3Q contracted cloud revenue with Google and Anthropic named is the datapoint to carry forward — it is the demand-side counterpart to [[AVGO]]'s ~$9.3bn of C2026 Anthropic TPU shipments and the FT's *">$150bn of AI chips"* Google-to-Anthropic programme, and it puts SPCX inside the same financing web rather than beside it.**
- 🆕 **MS PUBLISHES A SEPARATE STARSHIP/ROBOTICS PIECE THE SAME EVENING — OW, PT $300 UNCHANGED (Morgan Stanley · Adam Jonas & William Tackett, "The Rowdy Robot: What's the Catch? Bots on the Bayou", 7pp + video, 2026-08-31): the *"overlooked significance of an eventual Starship CATCH"* and how *"the newly announced LOUISIANA STARBASE makes their forecast for launch pad assumptions appear so conservative."*** **No estimate or PT change; logged for the Louisiana Starbase disclosure and because it is the second consecutive MS note arguing the launch-cadence assumptions on this page are too low.**


## 🆕🔴🔴 BERNSTEIN PUTS THE FIRST BOTTOM-UP COST RANGE ON THE DIRECT-TO-DEVICE TERRESTRIAL BUILD: US$50-130bn INCLUDING SPECTRUM, US$15-80bn EXCLUDING, ~30k-120k MACRO SITES (2026-08-31)

**Bernstein Research (~40pp), relayed by Bernstein TMT spec sales · Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-31.**

- **THE METHOD IS WHY THE RANGE IS WORTH CARRYING: *"a unique feature from this analysis is that **the coverage requirement build starts with WHERE AMERICANS LIVE, rather than the usual site count × cost per site formula**, which provides more granular detail around the nationwide coverage potential."* The note unpacks *"coverage requirements, capacity requirements (inclusive of **femtocells**), spectrum holdings and benchmarking, capex and opex on a **per-site AND network-wide basis**, and **model validation against known build benchmarks**."***
- 🔴 **THE BINDING CONSTRAINT BERNSTEIN IDENTIFIES IS SPECTRUM, NOT CAPEX: *"SpaceX currently holds **~65MHz** (scenarios here have it reaching up to **~120MHz**) which is **small relative to existing players who have 300-400MHz of low- and mid-band spectrum**."* On capacity: *"With a small subset of subs early on, **capacity isn't a concern**, though **subs concentrate (like the US population) and incremental capacity will be needed**."***
- ⚠️⚠️ **USE THE EXCLUDING-SPECTRUM FIGURE FOR ANY CAPEX COMPARISON. The US$50-130bn headline INCLUDES a spectrum-acquisition assumption, which is a licence-auction outcome rather than a construction cost; **US$15-80bn** is the build. Do not chain the two, and do not net either against the compute-capex numbers elsewhere on this page.**
- ➜ **SET AGAINST THE PRIZE, AS SIZED THE SAME WEEKEND: Atreides' Gavin Baker puts **Starlink mobile at a US$800-900bn revenue opportunity inside a broader ~US$2 trillion mobile + broadband market** (podcast, via MS TMT desk 2026-08-31). ## Snapshot
Space Exploration Technologies Corp. (SpaceX, **Nasdaq: SPCX**, Texas-incorporated) is Elon Musk's vertically-integrated space-and-AI conglomerate. Post the **February 2026 acquisition of xAI**, it reports in **three segments** (424B4 2026-06-12):
- **Connectivity** — Starlink LEO broadband + direct-to-cell: **~10.3M Starlink subscribers** across 164 countries; **~7.4M monthly unique devices** for direct-to-cell/messaging across ~30 countries.
- **Launch** — Falcon 9 / Falcon Heavy / Dragon (operational, reusable) + **Starship** (Super Heavy + upper stage, in development); the launch backbone that uniquely enables the orbital-compute ambition.
- **AI** (ex-xAI) — **Grok** frontier models, **X** (social/advertising), and **AI compute** — the COLOSSUS / COLOSSUS II training clusters.

**FY2025 total revenue $17.2bn** (FY24 $12.5bn, FY23 $9.3bn); pro-forma (incl. xAI) **net loss attributable to common shareholders ~$(4.9)bn** in FY2025, driven by AI-segment compute scaling (424B4). It sits at the intersection of three of this wiki's biggest themes — AI compute/datacenter power, satellite connectivity, and reusable launch.

## The IPO (424B4, 2026-06-12 · Reg. No. 333-296070)
- **555,555,555 Class A shares at $135.00** → **~$75.0bn gross** (the largest IPO on record); underwriting discount $0.90/sh (~$500M); net proceeds ~$74.5bn before expenses. Over-allotment: up to 83,333,333 additional shares.
- **Listing:** Nasdaq + **Nasdaq Texas** under **SPCX**.
- **Dual-class control:** Class A = 1 vote, Class B = 10 votes. **Musk holds ~82.4% of voting power** post-IPO → SPCX is a **"controlled company"** (relies on Nasdaq governance exemptions). Musk's Class B vests against extreme milestones — market-cap tranches **plus a permanent Mars colony (≥1M people)** and **non-Earth-based datacenters delivering 100 Tbps** (424B4).
- **Shares outstanding post-IPO:** ~7.38bn Class A + ~5.70bn Class B ≈ **~13.1bn total** → implied equity value very roughly **~$1.7–1.8tn** at the $135 IPO price (computed; SPCX did not print an explicit market cap — treat as ESTIMATE).
- **Use of proceeds:** fund growth — **expansion of AI compute infrastructure**, launch infrastructure/vehicles, and scale/capacity (424B4).

## AI compute — the neocloud / datacenter story (the reason this name is here)
**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-28 (ingest; source ~late Aug) — THE ORBITAL-COMPUTE COST BRIDGE, AND IT REDUCES TO ONE VARIABLE: *STARSHIP REUSABILITY*. THE CLAIM IS THAT ~$15BN OF A ~$50BN/GW DATA CENTRE IS POWER/COOLING/LABOUR YOU SIMPLY DO NOT NEED IN SPACE — AND THAT $15BN IS THE INFLATIONARY PART ON EARTH**

_(Source: **a16z podcast — "The Positive-Sum Future of AI with Gavin Baker"**. Guest **Gavin Baker** (Atreides Management — already a tracked source on this wiki), host **David** (a16z, per the sign-off and the "across the a16z portfolio" question). Source date not stated; the conversation references July/August acceleration, [[ANTHROPIC]]'s pre-IPO quiet period and Starbase Louisiana ⇒ **~late Aug 2026**; ingested 2026-08-28. ⚠️ Machine transcript, garbled on nouns — "Grockbot"=Grokbot, "Fable 5.1", "Astra", "Kimmy"=Kimi, "Neatron"=Nemotron, "Dorcash"=Dwarkesh, "Satcha"=Satya, "Daario"=Dario, "Eric Fishery"≈Erik Fisher, "Cheryl Samberg"=Sheryl Sandberg, "Lowden County"=Loudoun County.)_

🔴🔴 **READ-DISCIPLINE: BOTH VOICES ARE LONG, AND THIS IS THE SECOND CONSECUTIVE a16z-HOSTED PODCAST IN THIS INGEST BATCH.** Baker runs a fund long much of what is discussed; a16z is simultaneously **raising the hardware/infrastructure "Machine Age" fund** logged elsewhere in this batch. The framing question is literally *"can you tell me one quantitative data point in your business that's getting worse?"* — a question designed to find confirmation. **Value is in the MECHANISMS and DEAL STRUCTURES (which are specific, novel and checkable); discount the demand-durability conclusions accordingly.** No ratings, no PTs, no estimates. Every round-number "let's just say" is flagged below rather than absorbed.

- 🔴🔴 **THE BRIDGE, AS STATED, AND IT IS THE CLEAREST VERSION OF THIS ARGUMENT THIS PAGE CARRIES.** Take **~$50bn per GW**; *"let's just say $35 of that is"* the compute itself — **the same in orbit, "and maybe it grows a little because it's going into space."** The remaining **~$15bn is power, cooling, labour "and all sorts of things that you don't need in space, because you have the solar panel and the big radiator."** ➤ **The asymmetry that makes it work: that $15bn is exactly the INFLATIONARY component on Earth — electricians (see the ~2% DC-certified constraint on [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md)), materials, copper — while the offsetting orbital cost is LAUNCH, which is deflationary. *"With Starship reusability, that goes to under a billion. So the economics just instantly flip."*** ⚠️ **All three figures are "let's just say" round numbers. Do not lift them. What survives is the STRUCTURE: terrestrial DC cost is levered to inflating labour/materials, orbital DC cost is levered to a falling launch cost — so the crossover is a function of reusability, not of chips.** ➤ **THE QUESTION HE SAYS TO ASK: *"the question people should be asking about orbital compute is Starship reusability."* That is the single variable to underwrite.**
- **THE BOUNDED CLAIM, WHICH IS THE CREDIBLE PART: "you're always going to train on Earth."** *"There will always be advantages to having GPUs right next to each other… speed-of-light limitations are a real thing. Latency matters. So data centers on Earth, they're not going anywhere — I think they're going to continue to be very, very valuable. But an increasing fraction of the world's compute is going to be in orbit."* ➤ **Orbital is positioned as SWING/INFERENCE capacity, not as a replacement — which is a materially more defensible claim than the maximalist version and is consistent with the "first AI satellite" dating already on this page.** Physical form, to kill the strawman: **not a giant structure — *"the size of an airplane,"* a rack of ~72 chips with solar wings, in a **sun-synchronous orbit so the radiator sits permanently in shadow** (that is the cooling mechanism), and described as *"dramatically simpler"* than a Starlink satellite, which needs phased arrays and to manoeuvre.**
- 🔴 **THE HARDWARE DATE: "Elon said that he and Jensen have co-designed a Rubin rack, and it's going to launch in the fourth quarter of '27"** — with Baker's own haircut: *"let's just say he's off by two quarters — I mean, that's 2028."* ➤ **Cross-ref the [[NVDA]] Vera-CPU-customer item already on this page; a co-designed Rubin rack is a deeper relationship than a purchase.** ⚠️ Second-hand attribution to Musk.
- **LAUNCH CADENCE AS THE ENABLER, and the China distinction that matters.** **Starbase Louisiana is described as infrastructure for *"thousands of launches a year"*, at *"two a day per pad"* — which Baker calls **a conservative assumption** — with additional sites expected internationally. On the competitive read: **China catching a rocket is *"very different"* from Starship's catch → move → stack → refuel → relaunch loop**; he notes the Chinese approach used a jury-rigged wire system *"actually suggested on the SpaceX subreddit"* more than a decade ago. ➤ **The distinction is reusability CADENCE, not recovery capability — which is the same variable the orbital-compute economics hinge on.**
- **NON-COMPUTE OPTIONALITY, for completeness: Starlink mobile framed as ~$800-900bn of addressable revenue, taking mobile + broadband to "close to $2 trillion of a market,"** plus a rapidly growing AI/AR base and the cloud business. ➤ His bear-case rebuttal is that **even in a world where SpaceX "fumbles the ball with their first-party AI applications, they do still have a massive infrastructure business."** ⚠️ Unsourced TAM. **And the genuinely speculative tail, flagged as such: asteroid mining** (Psyche said to contain more gold/silver/platinum than exists in Earth's crust; capture into geosynchronous orbit, Optimus robots doing the work) and **Bezos's "Earth zoned residential" framing with heavy industry off-world.** **Not investable on any current horizon; logged because it is the stated 10-20 year vision, not a forecast.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — SPACEX IS NAMED AS AN EARLY CUSTOMER FOR NVIDIA'S *VERA CPU* RACKS, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT PURCHASE FROM THE GPU RACKS THIS PAGE HAS BEEN TRACKING.** *"**SpaceX and AI cloud company [[NBIS]] are set to be early customers of Nvidia's Vera central processing units and fast inference-focused Groq LPX racks**… **SpaceX plans to use Vera CPU racks, which can contain up to 256 Vera CPUs and are meant for longer-running AGENTIC AI**"* (The Information · Phoebe Liu, quoting NVIDIA, 2026-08-25). NVDA declined to share timing, pricing or scale. ⚠️ **Context from the same article that caps the read: "Nvidia has shared much less information about the CPU and LPX racks since March, with **some server makers saying earlier this month that they hadn't yet received pricing information or purchase orders**."** 🔴 **Consistent with — and additive to — the "we're exclusive to Nvidia" primary this page already carries: SpaceX is now buying across NVDA's product line rather than only the flagship GPU rack.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 — ORBITAL COMPUTE GETS A DATE.** *"**Elon Musk says SPCX's first AI satellites powered by NVDA chips will initially launch in 4Q27 and hit 'significant scale' in 2028**"* (Bloomberg, via MS TMT · Thomas Wigg, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ *Founder statement relayed by a desk; no unit, power or capex figure attached.*

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — MORGAN STANLEY SIZES WHAT SPACEX'S COMPUTE AMBITION WOULD TAKE OUT OF THE GLOBAL SUPPLY POOL.** *"Power is increasingly the physical bottleneck: MS's **~19M 2027 CoWoS-implied GPU/ASIC units equate to roughly 38GW at 2kW/chip. An incremental 8GW of SpaceX compute capacity alone would correspond to ~4M Rubin GPUs, or ~20% of [[TSM]]'s potential 2027 AI accelerator output**"* (MS · Charlie Chan, via MS TMT · Thomas Wigg, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **An analyst construction — CoWoS-implied units converted to GW at an assumed 2kW/chip, not a TSMC or SpaceX disclosure. But it is the clearest available statement of the crowding-out question this page has been asking: SpaceX's stated ambition is not a rounding error against 2027 leading-edge accelerator supply.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 — TWO SECONDARY ITEMS.** **(1)** JPM published *"SpaceX: Latest Thoughts on Key AI Innovations; Cursor Acquisition, Frontier Models, & Enterprise Opportunity"* (JPM · Douglas Anmuth, 2026-08-25) — ⚠️ *distributed to the desk but the body was not read in this run; flagged for pickup.* **(2)** *"The US lender told bankers ahead of **SpaceX's blockbuster IPO in June** that it would **borrow against the stock holdings** of Elon Musk's rocket and AI company **earlier than its standard policy**"* (FT, corpus, 2026-08-25) — ⚠️ *headline only.* **Tape: SPCX +2% on 08-25, "as bulls hope for the most important KPI tomorrow: a complimentary Jensen reference to Elon"** (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25).

- **COLOSSUS (Memphis, TN) + COLOSSUS II (Memphis + Southaven, MS)** — described as the **largest AI training data center clusters on Earth**; collectively **~1.0 GW of compute power** today (424B4). SpaceX claims it was **first to deploy a coherent gigawatt-scale AI training cluster (2026)**; built COLOSSUS's first cluster in **122 days**, COLOSSUS II's in **91 days**, at per-MW construction cost "considerably below industry benchmarks."
- **~325,000 NVIDIA GPUs** across COLOSSUS / COLOSSUS II (disclosed via the Cursor compute agreement), backed by hyperscale CPUs, exabyte storage and high-speed interconnect.
- **GPU sourcing:** all GPUs procured **purchase-order basis, no long-term supplier contracts** — flagged as a supply-concentration risk (depends on a concentrated set of advanced fabs).
- **Terafab** — chip-manufacturing initiative **with Tesla and Intel** (framework announced Mar 2026); long-term goal **~1 terawatt of compute hardware per year** → vertical integration into chip design/manufacturing.
  - **NEW — SpaceX-Tesla merger speculation (The Information · Grace Kay, 2026-07-01):** Growing expectation Musk will propose a formal **SpaceX-Tesla merger**; Terafab (Tesla's $55bn semiconductor manufacturing project) framed as a **Tesla-SpaceX collaboration** rather than an arm's-length JV. Org-chart analysis: the two firms are **"already operating as one entity in key ways"**; **SpaceX employees make up most of Terafab's leadership**; shared executives include **Charlie Kuehmann** (materials engineering), and Tesla AI/software execs **Ashok Elluswamy** and **Silvio Brugada** have crossed over into SpaceX's **xAI** unit. Terafab has also hired several **ex-Intel** employees, including its first major outside hire, **Gary Jiang** (18-year Intel veteran, former Intel Arizona factory manager) as foundry manager. See [TSLA.md](TSLA.md) for the Tesla-side framing of this same reporting.
- **Cursor (Anysphere) — Apr 2026 compute + option agreement:** SPCX provides Cursor GPU-cluster capacity and gets a **right (not obligation) to acquire Cursor** at a predetermined price; tied to an implied Cursor equity value of ~$60bn (424B4, "Recent Developments"). Drives inference demand + developer-interaction data.
- **Orbital compute ambition:** stated goal to **launch ~100 GW of compute to space per year** via satellites carrying >100 kW compute/metric ton — "AI compute satellites" / "non-Earth-based datacenters." This is the moonshot the launch business is meant to enable (and is explicitly flagged as unproven/uncertain timeline).
- **NEW — xAI reportedly reselling compute externally, dialing back internal AI tools (Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli, 2026-07-01):** xAI reportedly **dialing back expectations for internal AI tools** (Grok, Cursor) and has **started selling compute capacity to external customers, including Google and Anthropic** — Crisafulli draws a direct parallel to **Meta's new "Meta Compute" external-cloud pivot**, announced the same day. This is a notable shift from the IPO-prospectus framing of COLOSSUS/COLOSSUS II capacity as being built to scale SpaceX's own AI ambitions (Grok training, Cursor option, orbital-compute roadmap) — now reads more like a neocloud/compute-reseller pivot, at least at the margin.
- **JPM (Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-06-30):** reports **xAI sold its Colossus 1 compute capacity to Anthropic for $1.25bn/month**, covering **220,000+ NVIDIA GPUs (H100, H200, GB200)**; note flags **Anthropic's ARR ceiling as reportedly compute-constrained** — i.e., Anthropic's willingness to pay up for guaranteed capacity is itself a signal of compute scarcity. This $1.25bn/month figure is a HARD external-revenue data point for the AI segment that is not in the 424B4 (which pre-dates the deal) — flag as the single largest disclosed external compute-sale so far.
- **JPM initiation — the third-party COMPUTE-services angle, quantified (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07):** JPM's key AI-segment call is that neocloud monetization of Colossus capacity is priced at a **materially higher $/W than internal Grok monetization**, and that SpaceX is signing at premiums to the market. Concrete marks: SpaceX struck its **first neocloud deal with Anthropic (May-26)**, a **second with Google (June-26, Blackwell capacity at the Colossus II site)**, and a **third with Reflection AI (late-June)**. JPM estimates **Anthropic renting at a blended ~$29/W (Hopper + Blackwell), Google paying ~$50/W for GB300, Reflection AI likely ~$50/W for GB300** — vs. an **industry-average neocloud rate of ~$10-20/W**, i.e. impressive premiums it reads as a signal of the lack of spare capacity + the quality of the Colossus clusters. By contrast JPM models **internal Grok monetization at only ~$3-4/W near-term** as SpaceX closes the model-quality gap with Anthropic/OpenAI, using **~$15/W as a base rate for future terrestrial neocloud deals**. Grok revenue modeled **$4.3B (2026) → $7.9B (2027) → $69.9B (2028)**, the step-up driven by better enterprise monetization (partly the Cursor integration). Segment split example: of ~$X terrestrial compute, ~850 MW allocated to third-party neocloud (**~$33/W realized**) vs. internal Grok (**~$5/W**) — the neocloud-vs-Grok $/W spread is the crux of the AI-segment upside. Orbital neocloud modeled cheaper at **$10/W (2029) → $9/W thereafter** given far lower orbital deployment cost.
- **JPM AI-DC power/cooling model read-through:** JPM's initiation folds SpaceX terrestrial compute (~8x to ~8 GW by end-2028) and orbital compute (→75 GW by 2031) into the same accelerator/rack power framework the wiki tracks on [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md); Nvidia GPUs (COLOSSUS/COLOSSUS II run ~325k NVIDIA GPUs today) are the compute substrate, with Microsoft Fairwater Atlanta among the largest AI facilities JPM benchmarks against (light read-through — see [NVDA.md](NVDA.md), [MSFT.md](MSFT.md)).
- **FUNDA "Deep|SPCX: xAI 8GW Buildout Mapping" (channel-check deep dive, 2026-07-28) — the first gate-by-gate supply-side stress test of the 8GW target: the compute is ordered; power and permits decide how much gets plugged in.** Demand side "locked" per FUNDA's channels: existing **~1.5GW** (facility basis) + **13k GB300 racks** through 2026 (≈2.5GW; orders raised several times late-May→early-Jul, ~3–5k 1H vs 8–10k 2H, tail into 1Q27) + **15k Rubin racks in 2027 (≈4GW+**, double-confirmed — company-sourced + independent channel; "the market has not yet formed a consensus number"**) = ~8GW**, already slotted into tier-1 OEM schedules. **But none of six independent supply-side sources underwrites 8GW — they converge on 3.5–5GW**, with the entire gap at two gates: **power generation and environmental permitting** (chips, T&D and capital each verified non-bottlenecks; a transformer supplier: "5–6GW of new xAI capacity in 2027 is realistic" electrically, with mainland-Chinese vendors — Sanbian "the most deeply involved with xAI," Jinpan, Sieyuan, TBEA, China XD — backstopping any shortfall even after tariffs). **Scenario ladder (nameplate, YE2027, FUNDA estimates): Bear 4GW ~25% / Base 5GW ~50% / Bull 8GW installed ≈5.5–6GW usable ~15–20% / Tail <3GW ~5–10%; P(8GW installed) ≈ 12–17%.** The generation fix is Chinese and in motion: the **$1.465B Jereh × FTAI Aviation JV turbine order** (>1GW ≈ 40–45 Mod-1 mobile aeroderivative gensets on second-hand CFM56 cores, 24–25MW/unit, staged delivery by Nov-2027, 5-yr master agreement, per-unit downside capped at 10%; the order ≈61% of Jereh Group's audited 2025 revenue) — key terms corroborated by public filings; customer triangulated to the xAI/SPCX complex via the disclosure exemption + APR Energy + the "Fortress bought APR (38 turbines, ~$375M) and flipped it to the Musk complex for **$1.9B**" channel intel. **Progress marker: turbine bridge power is running ~2 quarters behind plan.** Third leg: a supplier has reserved a **1.5GW solar+storage program** (1.1–1.2GW solar + Megapack-type). **The binary risk is regulatory: every turbine at Colossus 1 operates unpermitted**; GW-scale self-generation needs a PSD permit (1–1.5yr review + 1.5yr build — "compliant operation at that scale is not achievable before 2028" unless essentially in hand); Jereh's mobile + water-injection-NOx spec is read by FUNDA as **purpose-built for the regulatory seam** (temporary permits, feasible in TN/TX); the endgame is **workaround (fines / retroactive permitting / relocation) or shutdown** — and a shutdown impairs the turbine-backed share of *existing* capacity too (Clean Air Act suit filed Apr-2026; Southaven litigation names subsidiary **MZX Tech**; NAACP + residents suing). Existing base, two-ledgers discipline (don't mix GW bases): **Colossus 2 at ~946MW is the largest single-site AI DC in the world (Epoch, 2026-07-27)**; the two operating sites ≈ **1.29GW IT × PUE 1.15–1.2 ≈ ~1.5GW facility**; the Memphis-area generation pool (APR's 38 turbines) is **1.14GW ISO nameplate but only ~750MW usable in summer**; YE26 ≈ 1.8–2GW facility (B3 ~500MW partially overlaps C2's 946MW — FUNDA deliberately takes the conservative range). GB300 mechanics: 2026 landing capacity tops out ~900MW; a million-GPU cluster needs **~3.5 quarters to reach 80% utilization**; VR200 module ramp 1–2 months behind plan concentrating in 4Q26; **most of the 2026-delivered 13k racks release compute only in 2027** — the micro-mechanics of the "back-loaded compute output" call. **Two datapoints that press on marks already on this page:** (a) the three external compute leases carry **90-day termination clauses and on-demand pricing** per FUNDA — a materially weaker contractual read than Bernstein's "multi-decade clauses (Anthropic and WULF cited)" (07-11); both kept, flagged, unreconciled; (b) FUNDA states **Cursor was "acquired by SPCX in an all-stock deal"** (running at C2 as an internal workload, Epoch-tagged "confident") — stated as fact where this page carries the acquisition as "looming" (The Information, 07-15); logged as FUNDA's characterization pending company confirmation. Capital: **$85.7B raised in the June IPO** — which reconciles the FT's previously-unfolded "$86bn raised" (07-19 changelog) with the prospectus's $75.0B base deal **if the 83.3M-share over-allotment was exercised in full (75.0 + 83.3M × $135 ≈ $86.3B gross — computed, not company-confirmed)** — plus **$25B of debt at end-June**; capex **FY2025 $20.7B (+86%) → 1Q26 $10.1B (+144%)**, AI share 61% / 76%. **The Aug-4 tension to watch: 13k GB300 racks × $4–4.5M ≈ $52–58B of server spend vs AI capex annualizing ~$31B → vendor financing / leasing structures, or a steep 2H capex ramp.** (FUNDA, 2026-07-28)

## Reconciling the Jeff Pu datapoint with the filing
- **Jeff Pu (@sssjeffpu, 2026-06-29):** SpaceX neocloud **1 GW (2026) → 4.5 GW (2027) → 9.5 GW (2028)**; **~2.2M / 2.5M Blackwell-equivalent GPUs in 2027 / 2028**; **new Buy on [CRDO](CRDO.md)** off the connectivity read-through.
- **Maps onto disclosure:** Pu's **1 GW 2026 = the company's ~1.0 GW COLOSSUS+II today** (HARD anchor). The **4.5→9.5 GW** ramp and the **2.2M/2.5M GPU** figures are **Pu's estimates**, not company guidance — but they're directionally consistent with SPCX's stated aggressive scaling and the IPO's "expand AI compute" use-of-proceeds. The ~325k GPUs disclosed today → ~2.2M by 2027 implies a ~7x ramp (Pu's model).
- **Read-throughs if the ramp is real:** [NVDA](NVDA.md) (Blackwell/Rubin units), [CRDO](CRDO.md)/[ALAB](ALAB.md)/[MRVL](MRVL.md) (AEC/optical/retimer content per GPU), [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) (1→9.5 GW firm-power step-change). Pu's CRDO Buy is the explicit connectivity play on this.

## New speculative threads (2026-06-30 / 07-01)
- **AI handset / phone (Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli, 2026-06-30 & 07-01):** 2026-06-30 note poses "Is SpaceX going to sell a phone?" as speculation; 2026-07-01 follow-up cites **WSJ reporting that SpaceX has shown investors a prototype AI handset device** running on a **proprietary OS** and a **Qualcomm chip**. Crisafulli is **skeptical SpaceX can scale to compete with leading consumer device platforms**; separately reiterates that **Starlink is seen remaining complementary to AT&T/Verizon/T-Mobile** (direct-to-cell partnerships) **rather than a replacement** for terrestrial carriers.
- **T-Mobile US (TMUS) / Deutsche Telekom angle (Jefferies · Jeffrey Favuzza, citing Handelsblatt, 2026-07-01):** Deutsche Telekom's CEO is reportedly **pushing ahead with a merger plan for T-Mobile US**; an analyst cited by Handelsblatt speculates **SpaceX could target T-Mobile US** as part of the dynamics driving the holding-company restructuring talk. Unconfirmed, third-hand (Jefferies citing a German paper citing an unnamed analyst) — treat as a rumor thread, not a HARD data point. If real, it would be a second major inorganic angle (alongside the Tesla merger speculation) pointing toward SpaceX aggressively expanding its terrestrial telecom/consumer footprint beyond Starlink direct-to-cell.
- **Trading activity (Jefferies Tech · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-07-01):** SPCX pre-market +0.6% on July 1 but on **very heavy volume — 3,257,479 shares (~$573.1mn notional)** — the **largest pre-market dollar-notional mover in Jefferies' tech basket** that day, consistent with the cluster of speculative headlines (Tesla merger talk, phone prototype, xAI compute-selling, TMUS rumor) hitting the stock simultaneously.

## The $100 debate — what the market is implying (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-07-24)
**"SpaceX Approaches $100: What's in the Price?" — OW, PT $300 unchanged.** With shares "at less than $120/share today" and many investors expecting **$100 to be in play on the first waves of lockup expiry**, Jonas reverse-engineers what $100 would actually mean. Against MS base-case **FY28 estimates of $157bn revenue** (including **$16.5/Watt on avg 6 GW** and **35mn avg consumer subs at $46/month ARPU**) **and $69bn EBIT**:
- **$100 = 18x EV/EBIT (FY28)** — roughly the median of comps, but **with 3x the revenue CAGR (76% vs 26% for 2025-2030)**. It would also be **16x FY28 P/E**, "more than a 50% discount to WMT (33x) and a 40% discount to TJX (26.5x)" — a deliberately jarring comparison. 18x EV/EBIT would rank **#81 in the S&P 500 ex-financials/real-estate**, between Boeing (21x), Waste Management (19x), CSX (18x) and Garmin (18x).
- **The sum-of-parts is the actual argument: at $100 the AI business is free or worse than free.** Putting **40x EV/EBITDA on Space + Connectivity** (slightly *below* the 46x space-comp median, for a market leader with a multi-year moat) implies **zero value for AI**. At **50x** — slightly above the median — it implies **negative ~$300bn for AI**. MS's own $300 PT rests on a **15-year DCF implying 56x EV/EBITDA on Space + Connectivity = ~$136/share**; on that multiple, **$100/share implies zero AI value AND FY28 Space+Connectivity EBITDA ~30% below MS estimates**.
- **Where sentiment actually sits.** Jonas is candid that the disconnect is positioning, not fundamentals: "the frequency of investor conversations overall has been **less intense than we would have expected**… despite no major fundamental changes to the story over the past few weeks, **bears appear to have become gradually more convicted, while bulls appear more resigned to wait for more obvious positive catalysts**." **"Most investors we speak with significantly discount Grok & Cursor and view the bulk of the AI monetization opportunity as neocloud,"** and **many ascribe zero or even negative value to AI** given high capex relative to Space & Connectivity, uncertain economics, and the **share of management time** devoted to it.
- **Acknowledged near-term risks:** further pressure from **impending lockup releases ("supply is undefeated?")** and an **earnings print that may leave many key investor questions unanswered.**
- **The bull framing:** at current levels "investors are paying a reasonable multiple for the core Space + Connectivity business while receiving limited-to-no value for the AI opportunity," against MS's estimated **~$100bn of 2028 AI revenue on 6 GW average compute** — i.e. a **discounted Space+Connectivity business** (>3/4 of global maneuverable satellites and mass launched to orbit, growing ~50% this year at 40% EBITDA margins) **with all AI optionality, orbital and terrestrial, near free.**

Context for the drawdown: **"SpaceX has erased $1 trillion in market value over the past month"** (@Hedgeye, 2026-07-22). Cross-read to [[MEDIATEK]] — Jefferies sales flags a **second hyperscaler ASIC project in final design with industry speculation pointing toward xAI/SpaceX** (2026-07-23), unmodelled optionality on both pages. See also the Terafab thread on [[INTC]] and [[ASML]].

## 2Q earnings preview — “Approaching Max Q?” (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-07-28)
**Overweight, PT $300 unchanged.** Jonas's follow-up to the $100 note, published the day of the print, and the metaphor is the argument: **in rocketry “Max Q” is the point of maximum dynamic pressure on the airframe during launch — and MS frames SPCX shares as approaching exactly that.** With **shares down nearly 50% from their highs and ~20% below the IPO price**, he argues the near-term is governed by mechanics rather than fundamentals.
- **The technical overhang is the whole story: “We'll see >930mn shares on Aug 6th (approximately $100bn worth), the first of 8 tranches that will see nearly 4bn shares unlocked through January of 2027.”** That is the specific, dated supply schedule behind the “supply is undefeated?” risk this page already carried in general terms.
- **Explicit expectation-setting for the print itself:** *“We anticipate 2Q results to neither significantly change near-term consensus expectations or the long term story, leaving the share price largely at the mercy of technical forces (lockup expiry), investor positioning (cautious) and macroeconomic factors.”* Metrics he flags as mattering — revenue, EBITDA, Starlink subs, ARPU, AI pricing, Cursor ARR, FY capex guide — are named and then subordinated to the technicals.
- **Valuation restated at the lower price: at $100, SPCX would trade 18x EV/EBIT (FY28) and 16x FY28 P/E — “more than a 50% discount to WMT (33x FY28 P/E) and a 40% discount to TJX (26.5x)” — with zero-to-negative implied AI value and Space & Connectivity results materially below MS estimates.** Unchanged from the 07-24 framing; what changed is that the price has moved toward it.
- **The conclusion is a window, not a call on the quarter: “A window has opened for longer term investors.”**

**Market context around the print (Twitter corpus, 2026-07-27→28):** **@Hedgeye: “SpaceX is down -50% from its all-time high”**; **@CNBC: “SpaceX has now lost the equivalent of a full Tesla in market capitalization.”** **@DougKass discloses a short from $213**, noting the **IPO price was $135, the shares peaked above $225 and are currently ~$111**, and criticising sell-side/TV promotion of the name. **HSBC published a mapping of the lockup expirations** — “when the shares could hit the market” (@zerohedge, 2026-07-27). On the other side, **@CathieDWood on the same session: “Starship's successful splashdown was perhaps game-changing news for SpaceX; yet the stock dropped further below its IPO price today. The equity market is climbing a ‘wall of worry'. Bull markets do not end in this way.”** Also logged: **CME Group launched single-stock futures on 50+ US names including SpaceX** (@Hedgeye, 2026-07-27) — cash-settled, leveraged, ~23-hour trading, no borrow fees, which materially changes how the lockup overhang can be expressed/hedged.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 — MORGAN STANLEY REFRAMES THE UNLOCK OVERHANG, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS THE WHOLE NOTE: "Roughly 3.3bn SpaceX shares will get unlocked between now and end of year. 'Supply is undefeated'… assuming you get the supply, right? What if only 30% of these shares are actually sold? What if nearly all of those shares sold are then bought by PASSIVE INDEX FUNDS?"** (MS · Adam Jonas / William Tackett, Overweight, PT $300, 2026-08-19 — "SpaceX: Investor Feedback Following 1st Unlock".)
- 🔴 **Read what the note actually is: it is investor FEEDBACK following the first unlock, and Jonas's contribution is to attack the consensus supply-overhang premise on two counts — the SELL-THROUGH RATE (he floats 30%) and the ABSORBER (passive index demand). Neither is asserted as a forecast; both are posed as questions. The page should carry it as a framing challenge with an explicit sell-through sensitivity, not as a supply estimate.** The falsifier is observable: actual sell-through versus the 3.3bn unlocked, and passive-fund 13F/ETF flow data.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 — THE COMPETITIVE MOAT ON REUSABILITY IS NO LONGER TWO COMPANIES WIDE: China's LandSpace successfully landed the booster of its Zhuque-3 rocket in Gansu province on its SECOND attempt, joining SpaceX and Blue Origin as the only private companies to have recovered an orbital-class booster.** (Reuters, 2026-08-19; MS · Adam Jonas the same day — "LandSpace Lands a Rocket… from Space… 2 China Recoveries in 6 Weeks.")
- 🔴 **Jonas's own headline is the datapoint that matters more than the single landing: TWO Chinese orbital-class booster recoveries in SIX WEEKS. A capability that took SpaceX years to make routine is now being demonstrated on a compressed cadence by a state-adjacent competitor set. For a name valued on launch-cost dominance and constellation economics, the erosion of reusability as a unique moat is the single most consequential structural item of the day — and it is being logged by the same analyst who is Overweight with a $300 PT.**
- **Also on 08-19, an unhelpful item and a speculative one:** new NASA lunar-orbiter images show a **fresh 60-foot-wide crater formed by a wayward SpaceX rocket that struck the lunar surface earlier this month** (CNN); and Barron's ran "**SpaceX Rocket Moon Impact Pictures Show Space Clutter May Be the Next Stock Play**." The first is a reputational/regulatory item, not a financial one; the second is thematic press.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-17 — DB PUTS THE FIRST FULL ESTIMATE REVISION THROUGH THE *CLOSED* CURSOR DEAL, AND THE SHAPE IS THE STORY: REVENUE AND EBITDA UP SHARPLY WHILE GROSS MARGIN COMES **DOWN** IN ALL THREE YEARS (Deutsche Bank · Edison Yu, with Bryan Kraft / Melissa Weathers / Roshan Ranjit / Laura Li, "Double clicking on Cursor", 2026-08-17; **BUY REITERATED, PT $235**, price at 2026-08-14 **$140.00**, 52-week range **$108.27–$201.80**; SPCX.OQ / SPCX US).**
- 🔴 **THE FULL REVISION TABLE (Prior ➜ Revised) — carried in full because it is the load-bearing content of the note, and this page had no DB estimate set on it before today:**

| Metric | 2025A | 2026E | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| Revenue ($m) | 18,674 | 46,041 ➜ **46,439** (+0.9%) | 97,105 ➜ **115,308** (+18.7%) | 148,081 ➜ **198,056** (+33.7%) |
| Gross margin | 49.4% | 61.5% ➜ **59.0%** (−250bp) | 68.1% ➜ **63.3%** (−480bp) | 68.9% ➜ **66.6%** (−230bp) |
| EBITDA ($m) | 6,584 | 23,592 ➜ **24,494** (+3.8%) | 63,330 ➜ **78,369** (+23.7%) | 109,852 ➜ **142,287** (+29.5%) |
| EPS (non-GAAP) | ($0.78) | $0.96 ➜ **$1.06** (+10.9%) | $2.35 ➜ **$3.44** | $3.30 ➜ **$5.62** |
| Capex ($m) | 20,737 | 66,950 (**unchanged**) | 172,534 ➜ **185,034** | 175,341 ➜ **190,341** |

- **The drivers, exactly as DB states them:** the **earlier-than-expected close of the Cursor deal**, a **LOWER gross-profit contribution from Cursor**, and — separately, i.e. not a Cursor effect — **raised neocloud estimates "based on more favorable pricing dynamics next year."** Net conclusion: *"this takes up our 2026-27E revenue and EBITDA forecasts to now well above consensus."*
- 🔴 **READ THE SHAPE, NOT THE HEADLINE: revenue and EBITDA go up sharply while GROSS MARGIN IS CUT IN ALL THREE YEARS. That is a lower-margin application layer being bolted onto a higher-margin compute base** — the first estimate set on this page that models the Cursor deal as **margin-dilutive** rather than as pure un-priced optionality (contrast the MS 08-10/08-13 framing, which is entirely about the residual value the market assigns Cursor, not about what it does to blended GM).
- ⚠️ **Reconciled against the BBG snapshot at the top of this page (asof 2026-08-17).** Revenue: DB 2026E **$46.4bn vs cons $43.7bn**, 2027E **$115.3bn vs $103.8bn**. EBITDA: **$24.5bn vs $20.1bn** and **$78.4bn vs $66.7bn** — so DB's "well above consensus" claim checks out on both lines. GM 2027E **63.3% vs consensus 63.8%** is effectively in line. ⚠️ **EPS IS NOT COMPARABLE AS PRINTED: DB's $1.06 / $3.44 is NON-GAAP against BBG's −$0.94 / $1.66 — log it as a basis difference to resolve at the next print, NOT as a ~$2 divergence.** ⚠️ **Capex: DB 2026E $66,950m is a THIRD basis on this page, above the sum-of-quarters mark (~$57.4bn) this page instructs you to use and far above the defective CY-sum snapshot ($47.4bn); DB 2027E $185,034m vs the snapshot's $181.4bn. Do not blend the three.**
- 🔴 **COMPUTE — and it corroborates the print rather than adding to it: "SpaceX exited 2Q26 with 1.4 GW of capacity with plans to deploy >2 GW by year-end"** (DB, 2026-08-17). Identical to the Q2 disclosure already on this page (1.4 GW exit-Q2 ➜ >2 GW exit-2026), i.e. a second house reading the same ladder — **not** an independent upgrade, and notably DB does **not** underwrite the ~10 GW exit-2027 figure in this note.
- **The deal, as closed:** the **$60bn all-stock acquisition of Cursor has closed**. Structure detail this page did not carry: **the legal entity remains Anysphere; the trade name AND the SpaceXAI subsidiary are both "Cursor"; HQ stays San Francisco.** DB's three arguments for the deal: **(1)** instant installed presence across **blue-chip enterprises already using Cursor at large scale**; **(2)** a proprietary **data flywheel from >1m real-world users** whose coding interactions are high-quality **RL data**; **(3)** vertical integration of the **application/harness layer (Cursor) with a frontier model (Grok) and large compute**.
- 🔴 **THE CANDID BEAR-TO-BULL BRIDGE, which is the most usable paragraph in the note because it concedes the bear case in the sell-side's own words:** Cursor's historic binding constraint was compute, while SpaceX *"had spent billions on compute and still lagged Claude/GPT on capability, and perhaps more importantly, **Grok never established a real presence in the enterprise engineering workflow**."* What Cursor supplies that money cannot: **the sequential traces of professional coding agents, which web scraping cannot provide.** On the org, and this is the part to keep: **the original xAI leadership team has essentially all departed**, and DB believes **Cursor's management fills that void** — citing the **Grok 4.6 / Composer 2.5** improvements and the earlier moves of Cursor product-engineering leaders **Andrew Milich and Jason Ginsberg** to xAI.
- **What $60bn bought, on DB's numbers: Cursor at ~$1bn ARR with about 300 people**, and opex estimated at only **~$200–300m** (opex is *"the inverse of COGS: extremely light"* — ~$500k/employee on a higher headcount). Funding trail: **Series A Aug-24 $60m at $400m · B Dec-24 $105m at $2.5–2.6bn · C Jun-25 $900m at $9.9bn · D Nov-25 $2.3bn at $29.3bn** — so **the all-stock price is ~2x the November-2025 round**. Company history: Anysphere incorporated 2022 in SF by four MIT classmates (Michael Truell, Sualeh Asif, Aman Sanger, Arvid Lunnemark) on a ~$400k pre-seed; Cursor shipped 2023.
- 🔴 **THE TOKEN-MIX ITEM — logged here for completeness, but it is a read-through AGAINST [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]] rather than a SPCX datapoint: DB estimates Cursor's internal Composer model costs just 10–20% of Claude/GPT, and with Cursor Router sending TWO-THIRDS of agent turns to Composer 2.5 the illustrative unit economics go −500% GM ➜ 25% ➜ 40%** (old uncapped Pro: user pays $20, Cursor pays Anthropic or OpenAI $120, gross profit −$100 → Team seat + meter: revenue $160, COGS $120, $40 gross profit → Router: revenue $80, COGS $48, $32 gross profit). DB names **Claude Code and OpenAI Codex** as Cursor's most direct competitors. See [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md), [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md), [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE CURSOR MERGER CLOSED. An 8-K dropped the same day, which is what unlocks management's ability to disclose Cursor-level progress and targets for the first time.** (MS Sales · Skyler Risom, "Friday Five," 2026-08-14, relaying Adam Jonas. ⚠️ **Sales commentary, explicitly not MS Research; the 8-K itself was not read for this entry — only the desk's characterisation of it.**)
- **As relayed:** *"**8-K just dropped: SPCX officially announced that Cursor merger CLOSED today.**"* And the consequence the desk draws: *"the 8-K today means that **SPCX management can now detail more progress and targets**."* ➜ **This is the disclosure gate, not the deal. The tradeable item is what management says NEXT about Cursor, and that is now legally possible in a way it was not on 08-13.**
- **Adam Jonas's Cursor numbers, unchanged from prior but now attached to a closed transaction: "Cursor can hit ~$8B ARR by year-end… and ~$33B by 2030."** ⚠️ **A 2030 ARR estimate for a just-acquired asset with no standalone reported P&L. MS is offering a bottom-up model ("quarterly thru 2027, down to subs and token/user") — treat the $8B as the near-dated, falsifiable half and the $33B as a scenario.** Adoption context from the same desk: *"Cursor still has just a **small share of total Enterprise spend but is growing m/m**… **only 55% of US businesses now pay for AI**."*
- ⚠️ **Structural detail that limits how fast this becomes float: "Cursor shares still classified as RESTRICTED SECURITIES under Rule 144, but no new specifics in S-1 or new 8-K."**
- **The valuation framing the desk uses, which is the argument rather than the fact:** *"SPCX current valuation indicates that the market is assigning just **~1.5x 2028 EV/Sales on the SpaceXAI**, **well below even Neoclouds with <7 GW of average compute in 2028**."* ⚠️ **A residual-SOTP argument: it is only as good as the value assigned to the launch business, which the desk does not show here. Logged as MS's framing, not adopted.** Read alongside this page's standing Susquehanna haircut (2027 compute ~6GW vs the stated 10GW) — **if the compute base is 6GW rather than 10GW, the "cheap vs neoclouds" comparison moves against SPCX.**
- **Model quality:** *"**Grok 4.6 is on the Pareto Frontier** per Artificial Analysis benchmark. **Performance inline with ChatGPT 5.6 Sol, with costs inline with Kimi K3** from Moonshot."* ➜ **Directly extends the 08-12 MS argument already on this page that SPCX ships frontier products rather than merely renting compute. "OpenAI performance at Chinese-model cost" is the specific claim; the benchmark is third-party (Artificial Analysis), which is the good part.**
- **Launch catalyst, dated:** **Starship Flight 14 projected "to set a new milestone in spaceflight in ~early September."** Core lockup overhang described as "in the rearview."

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE WIRELESS THREAT DEBATE IS NOW TWO-SIDED AND BOTH SIDES ARE SCHEDULED. MS's telecom analyst argues the market is OVERREACTING to SPCX; BofA has booked T-Mobile's CTO and CFO for 2026-08-25 to rebut it directly.**
- **The bear-on-the-threat case (MS · Sean Diffley, via Risom "Friday Five," 2026-08-14):** *"Investor feedback indicates that market **likely OVERREACTING to SPCX competitive threat in Wireless**, which has weighed on VZ TMUS T. Diffley thinks that **SPCX needs a) more Spectrum and b) a terrestrial network** (in addition to its existing satellites) to more meaningfully compete with the Big 3 for Mobile subscribers."*
- 🔴 **THE HARD CONSTRAINT, AND IT IS A NUMBER: "SPCX has just around 65 MHz vs the big players ~300-400."** Plus: *"**SPCX wasn't very aggressive in the most recent AWS-3 auction for Spectrum**."* ➜ **A ~5x spectrum deficit, and it did not press when spectrum was actually for sale. That is the most concrete disconfirming evidence on the mobile-substitution thesis this page carries.**
- **The three paths out, each with a named blocker (Diffley + an expert call with a former FCC Chairman + a spectrum expert lunch with J. Armand Musey):** **(1) MVNO with a Big 3 telco — "the Big 3 are vocally unwilling, even more underscored in the recent earnings prints." (2) Data-roaming rights — "lengthy and resource intensive legal and regulatory effort." (3) Build a terrestrial network — "would likely take years (and a low yield business to begin with!) and Upper C-band Spectrum might not be able to be deployed until ~2030."** Musey's middle ground: **SPCX offers RURAL coverage in collaboration with the major carriers**, and he reads the Big 3 as *"talking a Big Game on not working with SPCX."* Interim posture: **carrier-partnership D2D model (the TMUS relationship).**
- ⚠️ **THE COUNTER-EVENT IS ALREADY BOOKED AND IT IS THE SAME SIDE OF THE ARGUMENT, WHICH IS WORTH NOTING: BofA hosts "TMUS MANAGEMENT — REBUTTING THE SPCX/SATELLITE THREAT TO MOBILE OPERATORS" with T-Mobile CTO John Saw and CFO Peter Osvaldik on 2026-08-25, 12:00 ET, examining "the engineering, economics and spectrum constraints of a LEO-based wireless solution and its ability to compete with incumbent wireless service providers"** (BofA TMT Sales · Fenske, 2026-08-14). ➜ **Both scheduled inputs into late August come from parties with an interest in minimising the threat — MS's telecom franchise covers the incumbents, and TMUS is the incumbent. The bull side of the SPCX wireless case is, this week, unrepresented. Hold the asymmetry in mind rather than reading corroboration into it.**
- **The power leg, from the energy desk rather than the tech desk:** in a Jefferies BTM (behind-the-meter) debate, the bull side cites **"SPCX — 15-20 GW by YE27… who knows with Musk, but if need all of this power by end of 2027, BTM would be the solution"** (Jefferies Energy/Power Sales · James Snyder, 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **A power-desk restatement of the company's own compute ambition, sourced to no one, and it sits ABOVE the 10GW figure this page already carries — which Susquehanna has already haircut to ~6GW. Logged as a third, higher, less-supported mark; NOT adopted.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-12 — TWO ITEMS, AND THE SECOND ONE IS THE FIRST SELL-SIDE HAIRCUT TO THE 10GW CLAIM: (1) MS ARGUES GROK BOT PROVES SPCX CAN SHIP FRONTIER PRODUCTS, NOT JUST RENT COMPUTE; (2) SUSQUEHANNA — A HOUSE THAT IS POSITIVE ON [[NVDA]] AND THEREFORE HAS NO INCENTIVE TO SHADE IT DOWN — MARKS THE 2027 COMPUTE TARGET TO ~6GW VS THE STATED 10GW.**

- **🔴 (1) THE 10GW HAIRCUT, and it should be logged against every model on this page that uses the headline number:** *"SpaceX **exclusively committed to NVIDIA** for its AI infrastructure, including its upcoming Vera Rubin architecture. Interestingly, SpaceX discussed rapidly scaling compute capacity from **roughly 2 GW around the end of 2026 toward 10 GW by the end of 2027**, a positive for NVIDIA as the buildout should drive significant incremental demand (**though we believe this is closer to ~6GW vs. ~10GW**)"* (SIG / William Hearon, NVDA preview, **Positive on NVDA, PT $275**, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **A 40% cut to the headline GW figure, from a bull on the counterparty. Two things follow: (a) the exclusivity commitment to NVIDIA is confirmed by a third party, which strengthens the [[NVDA]] linkage on this page; (b) the 10GW-by-end-2027 number now has its first named external challenge and should never be quoted on this wiki without it. Cross-ref [[NVDA]], and note the wiki rule: never mix facility GW, IT-load GW and 800V-subset GW — SIG does not state which basis it is haircutting.**
- **🔴 (2) MS ON GROK BOT — the thesis is that the market is paying nothing for the AI stack (MS / Adam Jonas, William Tackett, Brian Nowak, Julian Herrera, "SpaceX: Grok Bot: Connecting the Dots", **Overweight, PT $300**, 2026-08-12):**
  - *"Grok Bot is **less a standalone product than early evidence of a broader AI platform** combining unique real-time data with vertically integrated compute, connectivity, and intelligence, **none of which the market is meaningfully valuing today**."* SpaceXAI launched a **beta of Grok Bot — an always-on assistant that orchestrates agents and tools inside a virtual computer**, available to **SuperGrok Heavy, Cursor Ultra and Cursor Teams Premium** subscribers on desktop and iOS.
  - **The two claimed moats: "(1) Large scale, highly unique, real-time data sets: X + Starlink + Cursor. (2) Physical-world moat enabled by deep vertical integration: Compute + Connectivity, accelerated by a multi-year advantage in space enabling unparalleled scalability."**
  - **🔴 THE VALUATION ARGUMENT, and it is the most usable part: "Most investors still value SpaceXAI as a modestly successful neocloud, with near zero credit for productizing its compute internally. Backing out Space + Connectivity ($127, or 52x 2028e EBITDA) leaves JUST $12 PER SHARE FOR CONSUMER + ENTERPRISE AI, roughly 1x 2028 EV/Sales and BELOW EVEN NEOCLOUD PEERS."** ⚠️ **That is a sum-of-the-parts claim resting entirely on the $127 Space+Connectivity mark at 52x 2028e EBITDA — a demanding multiple. The "$12/share for AI" conclusion is only as good as that anchor; state both halves whenever this is cited.**
  - **Cursor: MS published a primer 2026-08-11 estimating $8bn ARR by year-end and $33bn by 2030, calling it "an important 'wedge' into the enterprise (50k businesses, ~2/3 of the Fortune 500)."**
  - **Dated catalysts MS lays out: Cursor acquisition close "expected within weeks," Grok 4.6 this week, 4.7 later this month, Grok 5 before year end** — plus **a flagged lock-up expiry overhang.**
  - ⚠️ **MS's own hedge: "Critics may point to similarities with competing products from the large AI labs," and the network-effects argument is explicitly placed "at the platform layer rather than the individual product layer" — i.e. the bull case does NOT rest on Grok Bot beating any specific competitor product.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — THREE THIRD-PARTY VALIDATIONS LANDED THE SAME DAY, AND NONE OF THEM CAME FROM SPCX: Bernstein put Terafab inside its WFE model, JPM used SPCX's own compute-contract pricing as evidence of industry-wide scarcity, and a channel check says SpaceX is going direct to Taiwanese ODMs for AI servers.**
- **🔴 TERAFAB IS NOW IN A BROKER'S WFE MODEL.** Bernstein raised global WFE to **$148bn (2026) / $204bn (2027) / $259bn (2028)** — from $141bn/$175bn/$198bn — and in listing the drivers states **"SPCX Terafab also a key driver"** alongside DRAM (+53% '27), NAND (+58% '27) and logic/foundry (+20% '27, naming [[TSM]]/[[INTC]]/[[SAMSUNG]]) (Bernstein / Stacy Rasgon & David Dai, via David Patafio, 2026-08-11). ➜ **This is the first time Terafab appears as a *quantified demand input* in a third-party equipment forecast rather than as a company aspiration. It does not tell us the size of Terafab, but it tells us a semicap analyst is now underwriting it.** Cross-ref: [[AMAT]]'s page previously carried Terrafab only as *"tens of billions of WFE, not yet in numbers."*
- **🔴 SPCX's COMPUTE PRICING IS BEING USED AS THE INDUSTRY'S SCARCITY EVIDENCE.** JPM, initiating a Dec-27 target on [[CRWV]]: *"We are favorable on the demand outlook for CoreWeave, with **recent datapoints in relation to pricing for compute deals from SpaceX highlighting the supply-demand imbalance in the industry**, which we forecast to widen in the medium-term"* — specifically citing *"the favorable pricing for on-demand compute in the industry, for example with **SpaceX's contracts with [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[GOOG]]**"* (JPM / Samik Chatterjee, 2026-08-11). ➜ **The lease rates already logged on this page from primary documents ($7.80/GPU-hr to Anthropic) have now become a sell-side reference point for the whole neocloud complex. That is a credibility upgrade for the disclosure, and it also means the rates are being watched as a market indicator.**
- **⚠️ THE COST-SIDE ITEM: a channel check says SpaceX plans to buy AI servers DIRECTLY from Taiwanese ODMs rather than 100% from [[DELL]].** *"Our latest channel checks show that **SPCX and [[CRWV]] plan to buy AI servers directly from Taiwanese ODM's, instead of purchasing 100% from DELL** — and they are currently in discussions with **Hon Hai, Quanta, Wiwynn and Wistron**. We expect this take place in **2027E, most likely starting with VR200**"* (Fubon / Titan Cheng, via Jefferies / Conor O'Mara and Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11; **DELL -4%** on it). ➜ **For SPCX this is a BOM/margin tailwind on the 2027 buildout and further evidence of vertical integration. Unconfirmed by either company.** Context from the same note: **VR200 rack ASP up to US$8–9mn**.
