- **On the AI valuation, from the desk version of the Cursor work (MS TMT desk relaying Adam Jonas, 2026-08-11): "markets under-appreciate the SpaceX AI business with implied AI valuation of just 1x 2028 EV/Sales, still well below neocloud peers"**, with **Cursor ARR of ~$8B by YE26 and ~$33B by 2030** (≈10%/19% of MS's total SPCX AI revenue in 2026/2027), the transaction *"closing in a few weeks."* **Cursor "consumes 7-10% of SpaceXAI total nameplate compute capacity, which is ~2x the revenue intensity per watt of the average SpaceXAI workload"**; gross margin starts negative on third-party inference reliance, turns positive in 3Q26 and reaches low-60s by 2030 as work routes to Composer/Grok and cheaper third-party models. **Enterprise is 75% of ARR, and "only 55% of US businesses now pay for AI."** ⚠️ **Same analyst, same thesis as the 08-10 block below — logged here only for the additional marks (1x 2028 EV/Sales; the 7–10% compute share at ~2x revenue intensity per watt; the GM trajectory), not as a second data source.**
- **Also: Adam Jonas published "SpaceX: Cursor Deep Dive: Can SpaceX Leap to the AI Frontier?" (2026-08-10 PM), and MS hosted a Global Technology webcast on "Memory, Hyperscaler Financing and SpaceX" (Shawn Kim et al., 2026-08-11 8am ET)** — i.e. SPCX is now a standing agenda item on MS's global tech calls, not just an autos/space one.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-10 (PM) — MS PUTS THE FIRST BOTTOM-UP MODEL ON CURSOR, AND IT IS THE PART OF THE SPCX STORY NOBODY IS PAYING FOR (Morgan Stanley / Adam Jonas · William Tackett · Brian Nowak · Julian Herrera, "Cursor Deep Dive: Can SpaceX Leap to the AI Frontier?", 2026-08-10; OW, PT $300, bull $600; ref $139).**
- **The valuation argument, and it is a sum-of-parts residual:** *"Backing out the value of the **Space + Connectivity businesses ($127 MSe)** from the current price implies an **AI valuation (Consumer + Enterprise AI) of ~$12/share, or slightly more than 1x 2028 EV/Sales — well below even neocloud peers**."* MS's read of positioning: *"very few investors are bullish SpaceX's AI business **beyond neocloud**, resulting in an **upside-skewed catalyst path**."*
- **The Cursor model (bottom-up, quarterly to 2027 and annual to 2030, down to subs and tokens/user):** ARR **~$4bn (early June 2026) → ~$8bn by YE26 → $17bn in 2027 → $33bn by 2030**, with **enterprise ≥3/4 of ARR**. Revenue contribution: **~$2.5bn to SPCX in 2026 and $13bn in 2027 — ~10% and ~19% of MS's total AI revenue projection**, ~15% by 2030.
- **🔴 The revenue-intensity claim is the analytically load-bearing one:** Cursor generates that revenue while consuming only **7-10% of SpaceXAI's total nameplate compute capacity** — *"nearly **twice the revenue intensity per watt** of the average SpaceXAI workload,"* reflecting high-margin subscription mix vs leased neocloud capacity and consumer Grok inference. **Gross margin starts NEGATIVE (third-party inference), crosses positive in 3Q26, reaches ~low-60s by 2030** as work routes to Composer/Grok and cheaper third-party models. ⚠️ Note this is the same $/MW argument SemiAnalysis made about [[MSFT]]'s workload mix on 08-07 — a **re-mix, not a demand, argument** — now applied to SpaceX's own fleet.
- **Deal + product state:** the **$60.0bn all-stock Cursor (Anysphere) acquisition** announced **2026-06-16** is expected to **close in 3Q26 ("within the next few weeks")**. Cursor is *"a workflow interface layer (AKA **harness**) that calls various LLM models"* — partly third-party frontier models, partly its own **Composer** models — used by **>64% of the Fortune 500 and 50,000 enterprises**. **Grok 4.5 was jointly trained on Cursor interaction data** and launched inside Cursor day one as a default first-party pool model. MS's thesis hinges on **Cursor's journey from "harness" to "frontier"**.
- **Third-party adoption data MS leans on (external, checkable):** **Ramp AI Index** — median monthly AI spend per employee **~$11 in June, +167% y/y**; top decile **$516**, top 1% **>$4,880**, each **+~240-250% y/y**; **~55% of US businesses now pay for AI** (large firms ~65%) — *"close to half still pay nothing."* ⚠️ **PRIMARY-DATA CHECK (2026-08-20 — the Ramp CSV is now on disk at `_wiki/_data/ramp-ai-index/`): the LEVELS tie, the y/y DOES NOT.** File: June median **$10.93** (≈$11 ✓), top 1% **$4,973.62** (>$4,880 ✓), top decile **$520.48** vs MS's $516 (0.9% off — rounding or a restated vintage). But the file's June y/y is **+154.8% median / +215.4% top-decile / +200.9% top-1%**, not +167% and "~240-250% each" — and the top decile's **+240.6% is the JULY y/y**, so the exhibit reads like June levels paired with July growth (or MS's 2025 base sat lower than this file's, i.e. Ramp restated 2025 upward). **MS's levels are usable; take the growth rates off the file.** Full reconciliation on [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). **Jellyfish** — the median engineer now holds an AI coding licence, top-quartile access near 90%, but **the median company still ships <30% of its code with AI help**, up from near zero a year ago.
- **⚠️ MS's own caveats, carried verbatim:** *"Because Cursor is private and official financial results not reported, we **triangulate** each figure against company disclosures and press — **this model has NOT been vetted by management** and we are not privy to any financial figures not publicly reported."* The **$33bn 2030 ARR assumes NONE of the broader platform** (Grok intelligence + X data + SpaceXAI compute + Starlink connectivity + Tesla physical AI).
- **Catalyst path as MS lays it out:** further disclosure at **3Q earnings (first report post-close)**; **Grok 4.6 and 4.7** (both "over coming weeks", incorporating Cursor data); **Grok 5 before year-end**; a possible **Composer 3**; periodic ARR figures.

**🆕 2026-08-10 — SpaceX turns up in the [[INTC]] foundry pitch.** On Intel's $15bn follow-on roadshow call, **SpaceX's interest in 14A was cast by Intel management as an endorsement of node maturity** (TMT Breakout / Intel syndicate investor call with CFO David Zinsner, 2026-08-10). ⚠️ Sourced from Intel, not SpaceX — logged as a *seller's* characterisation of a prospective customer, with no confirmed commitment. Cross-ref [INTC.md](INTC.md).

**🆕 2026-08-09/10 — the 10GW claim gets quoted verbatim, and DB puts a number on the ARR ramp.**

**The Musk quotes the whole debate now runs on (SPCX Q2 2026 call, 2026-08-04, reproduced by EnerTuition "Beyond The Hype", 2026-08-09):**
- *"We expect to end this year with over two gigawatts of compute. Probably our cumulative compute online by the end of next year will be several times higher. It may, let's say, be closer to **10 GW of compute than 5 GW of compute**, as a way to think about it."*
- *"Our tentative target is to actually have **20 GW at the power and cooling level online by the end of next year**. Now, I don't think we're going to achieve 20 GW… I would expect that we'd still probably have, at the power plant level, something **close to 15 GW**. Assuming that maybe a quarter of the projects take longer than expected. Our goal is to have far more power cooling and electrical equipment than we have GPUs."*
- *"What is my best guess for monetization per watt of, say, Rubin? My guess would be somewhere between **$30 and $50**. This is just a guess."*
⚠ **Base discipline (per `_meta/assumptions.md`):** the **~2GW end-2026 / "closer to 10GW than 5GW" end-2027** figures are **compute GW**; the **20GW target / ~15GW expectation** is **power-and-cooling GW**. They are different bases and Musk states the intent to run power/cooling *ahead* of GPUs. Do not net them.

**🆕 2026-08-10 — DB: "Fast path to $100bn ARR" (Deutsche Bank / Edison Yu · Bryan Kraft · Melissa Weathers, 2026-08-10; Buy, PT $235, close $133.11).** Baseline: **2Q run-rate was just $31bn**, so management is aiming to **more than triple that in six months** to exit December at **$100bn ARR**. DB sees the target as **"likely very achievable,"** driven mainly by *(as printed in the note)* **neocloud and Cursor contribution**.

**Sell-side/street context:** EnerTuition is explicitly the skeptic voice — "there is now no shortage of fans taking Musk's earnings call comments as gospel" — while **SemiAnalysis published on 08-07 arguing the 10GW claims are legitimate**, and postulating that **Microsoft's agreement with OpenAI makes MSFT a candidate to lease a large share of the new capacity at high prices** (SemiAnalysis, 2026-08-07, via EnerTuition 08-09 and Vital Knowledge 08-10). Vital Knowledge separately relays the SemiAnalysis speculation that **Nvidia could provide enormous vendor financing to SpaceX** for the build-out (2026-08-10) — which is the same balance-sheet mechanism NVDA just used at Lancium. Also: **OCI deepening polysilicon ties with SpaceX** on the AI/space-solar build (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10). Morgan Stanley is hosting a **Global Technology call on Memory, Hyperscaler Financing and SpaceX, Tuesday 2026-08-11, 8am EST** (MS / Shawn Kim, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [NVDA.md](NVDA.md), [MSFT.md](MSFT.md), [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md).

**First earnings report as a public company. Big beat on revenue and EBITDA, Starlink exactly in line, cash flow deeply negative — and the stock fell ~5-7% because the event that mattered was never the quarter.** Both houses on this page (MS 07-28, DB 08-03) had said in advance that the print would not move the story, and it didn't: attention went straight back to the **08-06 lockup**.

**The numbers (actual vs Street):**
- **Total revenue $7.81bn, +92% y/y** vs **Street $7.11bn** (UBSe $7,106m) — Vital Knowledge cites a lower consensus of **$6.8bn**; ⚠️ *two different consensus sources, both logged rather than reconciled.*
- **Adjusted EBITDA $3.5bn, +191%** vs **Street ~$2bn** — the largest proportional beat in the release (Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-04).
- **All three segments beat:** **Space $962m** (Street $873m) · **Connectivity $4.29bn** (Street $3.8bn) · **AI $2.561bn** (Street $2.08bn / UBSe $2,258m).
- **AI revenue +213% q/q and +247% y/y**, with **~$1.6bn attributable to the new cloud deal** (UBSe had modelled ~$1.4bn) plus advertising.
- **Starlink: 12M subscribers vs a 12M bogey; ARPU $66 vs $66 — both exactly in line.** UBS: *"nothing all that worrisome yet to the telco cohort."*
- ⚠️ **Cash flow is the ugly half: capex $18.36bn (about in line), of which $15.8bn in the AI segment — an AI-segment capex/revenue ratio of 6.3x. For 1H26: ~$3.5bn of operating cash flow against ~$34.5bn of investing outflows = FREE CASH FLOW OF ~NEGATIVE $31bn.** Vital Knowledge's bottom line: *"the revenue and EBITDA results are great, but the cash flow numbers are pretty ugly."* **Capex in Q3 and Q4 is guided to look similar to Q2** — the heavy spend continues.

**From the call (Vital Knowledge conf-call highlights, 2026-08-04) — the compute ramp is the headline and it is enormous:**
- **The AI business ends 2026 with >2GW of compute (vs 1.4GW in Q2) and Musk thinks that could approach ~10GW by end-2027.** ⚠️ **A ~7x in 12 months. This is the single most consequential number in the print for the rest of this wiki** — see [[NVDA]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- **🔴 NVIDIA exclusivity, stated flatly: SpaceX will build all its AI infrastructure exclusively on NVIDIA "because we think the Vera Rubin architecture is the best architecture"** — and later, **"we will receive a very significant percentage of their GPUs next year."** ⚠️ **A named ~10GW-by-2027 buyer declaring NVDA-exclusive is a direct negative read-through for [[AMD]] and the custom-ASIC complex at this account** (TMTB notes NVDA traded +1% in the post on the Musk comments).
- **🔴 MEMORY IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, and management sized it: "memory is the main limiting factor for AI compute — while supply is increasing 20% annually, demand is spiking by ~200%, which is why memory prices continue to head higher."** ⚠️ **This is a hyperscale-scale buyer independently corroborating the 2027-sold-out narrative from the demand side — cross-file [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). Note it also sits directly against [[AMD]]'s same-evening comment that customers can "modify memory footprint if TCO is not as significant."**
- **Cloud services: management "very bullish" on the recently struck [[GOOG]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] deals and "think the company could be at $100B of ARR by the end of 2026 for this business (including contribution from Cursor)."** ✅ **RESOLVED BY THE TRANSCRIPT (2026-08-04) — see the primary-transcript section below. The magnitude is REAL but the relay's framing was WRONG: it is TOTAL COMPANY ARR defined as December-month revenue annualized, NOT $100bn of cloud ARR. The "implausible" flag this bullet originally carried is withdrawn; the mis-scoped version is preserved in `## Changelog` (2026-08-05).**
- ⚠️ **The counterweight Vital Knowledge attaches to those deals, and it is the same one this page already carries: "it's important to note that the Google/Anthropic agreements have EASY EXIT CLAUSES FOR BOTH FIRMS, and Google mgmt. recently talked about how it considers 3rd party compute to be just a 'BRIDGING' solution (i.e. not a long-term part of their data center strategy)."**
- **Management claims payback on the AI compute infrastructure is LESS THAN ONE YEAR thanks to the cloud services deals.** ⚠️ Unverified and hard to reconcile with a 6.3x AI capex/revenue ratio — press it on the transcript.
- **Grok 5.0 due by end-2026, trained on all the data SpaceX has ever produced** — Musk expects that makes it the best-performing engineering model "by far."
- **Starship framing: Falcon currently delivers ~2,500 tons to orbit per year; Musk says Starship can take that to 1M or even 10M tons.** **The Starmind AI satellite begins launching next year** — the orbital-compute thread this page carries via Starcloud.
- **🆕 Internal revenue projection pulled FORWARD: $1T of revenue by 2030 or 2029, versus the prior goal of 2031.**
- **🆕 Direct-to-consumer wireless is now explicit: management talked about entering the D2C wireless market via Starlink — "I anticipate us being able to acquire quite a few of their customers" (referring to AT&T, Verizon, T-Mobile)** — while suggesting they can target consumer wireless **without the heavy capex those carriers bear** (no specificity given). ⚠️ **This is the Starlink bear thesis for the telco cohort that UBS said it was holding its breath for, and it arrived on the call rather than in the KPIs.**

**Why the stock fell anyway.** UBS: *"Shorts were clearly covering into the print because there were just too many potential landmines and wildcards hanging over this report. That likely explains a good chunk of today's outperformance. Now that the event is out of the way and attention shifts back toward the upcoming LOCKUP EXPIRATION, it's not exactly shocking to see the stock give some of it back."* TMTB: *"Looks solid with much better revs but Starlink slightly light. Stock was up 9% today so just giving some of that back. Tough to tell where bogeys were for this one given first q out of the gate. With earnings now cleared and attention shifting back toward the lockup expiration, not surprising stock to be down."* **Net: the maiden print validated the fundamentals and changed nothing about the setup — exactly as MS ("largely at the mercy of technical forces") and DB ("neither of the two things that move the stock is the print") predicted.**

**The pre-print buy-side case, for the record (22V Research · Dauvin Peterson, 2026-08-04, morning of the print):** liked the risk/reward **"as a thematic way to play tight compute and space economy expansion."** The lockup arithmetic: **911M shares unlocking 08-06 on top of a 555M current float** — *"does 911M sound large… yes"* — but he takes the other side, arguing **20% of that (≈182M shares) is only ~2-3 days of trading volume** and could be absorbed by short covering, that **long-term bulls sit in the capital structure**, and that the **September Nasdaq rebalance** could start being positioned for within weeks. Valuation: **~20x consensus 2027 revenue of $71bn, "not demanding… given the optionality."** He is **"much more confident in the longevity of the Anthropic and Google compute agreements"** given tightening compute — the direct opposite of Vital Knowledge's exit-clause caution, and worth carrying as a genuine two-sided disagreement. Also: **an NAACP lawsuit over temporary turbines powering Colossus 2 has been de-risked by a recent filed agreement extending the turbine-removal timeline while permanent generation comes online in 2027** — no longer a binary event. Capacity adds in the prior 1-2 weeks: **densification of the existing TN GB300 cluster ("Minihard")** plus a possible **new TX datacenter project**.

**★ THE PRE-PRINT BAR, NOW SCORABLE — the one clean consensus set this page was missing, and the shape of the beat it reveals (Visible Alpha consensus + MS · Adam Jonas estimates, via Capstone internal research synthesis · Daniel Grozdea, 2026-08-04 13:08 — circulated ~4 HOURS BEFORE THE PRINT).** ⚠️ **Why it belongs here rather than as a preview: this section carries FOUR mutually inconsistent consensus marks (Street $7.11bn / UBSe $7,106m / Vital Knowledge $6.8bn / The Information $6.819bn) and explicitly flags them as "logged rather than reconciled." The synthesis carries a single *sourced* set — Visible Alpha — with MS's own estimates alongside, which makes the print scorable line by line:**

| Metric | Consensus (Visible Alpha) | MSe | **ACTUAL (08-04)** | Score |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 08-24 | 🔴 **UBS · Timothy Arcuri — "SemiBytes Weekly: … Terafab Update"** (UBS Evidence Lab job-posting + satellite imagery) | supply / capital | **mixed** | **44 Terafab-related postings on Tesla's career portal in July**, mostly **senior HVM/fab-ops engineers**, spanning **litho, deposition, dry etch, epitaxy** — *"a fairly comprehensive engineering organization rather than selectively hiring around only a handful of modules."* 🔴 **Includes Memory Design AND (more recently) Memory Integration Engineers — "confirms Terafab's willingness to kick-start its internal memory manufacturing efforts"** — but *"we are unclear where Terafab would source memory IP and see limited incentives for existing memory suppliers to license their IP."* **Duplicate postings in Austin TX and Hsinchu City, Taiwan** → suspects tapping **[[TSM]]'s talent pool**. Post the **6-Aug official announcement**, **cleanroom-construction roles are now the largest posting category**. 🔴 **But satellite imagery of the Grimes County campus shows buildout "at a VERY EARLY STAGE, with only limited site-clearing/deforestation work visible so far."** |
| 08-21 | ⚠️ **Bernstein Research — direct-to-device feasibility** (relayed by Tyler Seidman, Bernstein Tech) | produto / competicao | **bear** | Concedes the **V2 constellation is "a notable development" (4x receiver size, higher beam density, lower altitude)** but *"engineering problems are unlikely to be solved (i.e. Uplink, batter[y] and antennas)"*; battery life flagged as phones must run at higher power. 🔴 *"…unlikely to deliver the level of performance, reliability, and indoor coverage consumers expect from a primary wireless provider in its current form… a partnership (i.e., MVNO) appears to be the most likely approach."* vs **management's end-2027 mobile launch**. ⚠️ Desk colour: *"'smoke and mirrors' comments arise in some of my meetings on the near term trajectory for both Starlink and the orbital data centers ramp"* — balanced by *"it's hard to bet against Elon."* Negative read for Towers; CHTR/CMCSA negative on Starlink broadband. |
| 08-21 | **@PhilipJohnston (Starcloud founder)** | competicao | **neutral** | Competitor datapoint in orbital compute: **Starcloud raised $250mn at a $2.3bn valuation with [[NVDA]] and Cisco Invests participating**, moving into a 100k sq ft facility to ramp to **100 satellites/week.** |
| Revenue | **$6.9bn** | $6.75bn | **$7.81bn** | ★ **+13% vs cons / +16% vs MSe** |
| Adj. EBITDA | **$2.1bn** | $2.0bn | **$3.5bn** | ★ **+67% vs cons — the blowout line** |
| EBIT | **−$1.6bn** | −$1.7bn | not separately disclosed in the release recap | open |
| Adj. diluted EPS | **−$0.32** | −$0.35 | not separately disclosed in the release recap | open |
| Consumer Starlink subs | **12.1mn** | 12.0mn | **12M** | in line (a hair light vs cons, on MSe) |
| Consumer monthly ARPU | **$65.5** | $65.5 | **$66** | in line |
| End-of-period compute | **1.4 GW** | 1.4 GW | **1.4 GW** | **on the nose** |

⚠️ **Read the shape, not just the magnitude: the two revenue/profit lines blew out while EVERY operating KPI landed on the nose — subs, ARPU and installed compute all printed within a rounding error of both consensus and MSe.** That is the cleanest available confirmation of the conclusion this section already reaches: **the quarter was never the driver.** The beat came from the AI/neocloud revenue recognition (~$1.6bn from the new cloud deal) and from EBITDA flow-through, i.e. from things the Street had no basis to model, not from the operating cadence it *could* model — which is exactly why a +67% EBITDA beat moved the stock −5% to −7%. **MS's own caveat on that consensus, and it is the reason no single mark should be treated as the bar: "a very wide skew in 2026 consensus — differing assumptions on share count, inclusion/exclusion of announced neocloud deals, and Cursor."** **Guidance shape expected going in was limited and Tesla-style — full-year capex (MS modelled ~$48bn and expected ~$50bn) plus year-end compute deployed (2 GW)** — ✅ **both cleared: capex $18.36bn in Q2 with Q3/Q4 "similar to Q2" annualizes far above $50bn, and management guided >2GW exiting 2026.** (MS · Adam Jonas / Visible Alpha, via Capstone research synthesis, 2026-08-04)

**★ MS's PRE-PRINT UPSIDE-vs-DOWNSIDE LIST, used as the scorecard against the call already logged above (MS · Adam Jonas, via Capstone research synthesis, 2026-08-04, pre-print).** ⚠️ **The single biggest bar management could clear was defined in advance as "plans for >2 GW INCREMENTAL compute in 2027" — and the call delivered ~10GW by end-2027, i.e. multiples of the stated upside trigger. Score it as the largest cleared bar of the print, and note that MS sized the leverage: "each incremental GW = $10-50bn of potential AI ARR," with any signal above 2 GW for 2027 flagged as THE BIGGEST SINGLE UPSIDE LEVER.** Rest of the upside list: **new chunky neocloud deals (Reflection and DoW/Pentagon talks reported, nothing announced)** · **Grok share gains on Cursor vs frontier models** · **Cursor ARR accelerating from $4bn** · and per **DB · Edison Yu, a large government/sovereign AI deal** (already on this page as DB's re-rating catalyst). Downside list, which is where the print actually landed badly: **capex materially above ~$50bn for 2026** (⚠️ **hit — $18.36bn in Q2 with Q3/Q4 guided similar annualizes to ~$70bn+**), **commentary implying additional funding raises before year-end**, **plans to end/renegotiate current neocloud deals**, **Starlink subscriber deceleration** (not hit — subs in line). **Segment framing worth carrying: Space is now ~11% of revenue and falling — Cantor · Colin Canfield: "not fundamentally relevant to share performance."** ⚠️ **That is a blunt statement about a name most generalists still think of as a launch company, and it is consistent with the print (Space $962m of $7.81bn = 12.3%).**

## ★★ THE PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT LANDS (Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04) — four flagged items resolved, one page error corrected, and five numbers no relay carried

⚠️ **Everything above this line about the 08-04 call came from RELAYS** — Vital Knowledge's conf-call highlights, TMTB, and machine-translated Chinese tweets (@QQ_Timmy). The full Bloomberg transcript is now on file and it is the primary. **Where the two disagree, this section wins.** (SpaceX Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Space_Exploration_Technologies_Corp_Earnings_Call_2026804_DN000000003115071217pdf.html)

**① RESOLVED — the $100bn ARR claim, and the page had it WRONG.** The Current-state section above flagged this as *"FLAGGED AS IMPLAUSIBLE AS TRANSCRIBED… treat the magnitude as UNVERIFIED pending the transcript."* ⚠️ **The magnitude is real; the page's characterisation of it as "$100B of ARR… for this business [cloud]" was the error — it is TOTAL COMPANY ARR, and "ARR" here is defined as the DECEMBER MONTH'S REVENUE ANNUALIZED, not a contracted subscription base.** CFO Bret Johnsen, verbatim: ***"We believe this puts us on a trajectory, including contribution from Cursor, to reach $100 billion of ARR or annualized revenue run rate by the end of this year, BASED ON OUR EXPECTED REVENUE IN THE MONTH OF DECEMBER OF THIS YEAR."*** Musk, pressed by DB's Edison Yu: ***"the $100 billion ARR in December is not a question mark, but that's what we would achieve IF WE BASICALLY DID NOTHING. So… I think it may be higher than that. It probably will be higher than that."*** ⚠️ **Against a Q2 revenue run-rate of $7.81bn/quarter (~$31bn annualized), a December exit at ~$8.3bn/month is a ~2.7x step in six months — aggressive but arithmetically coherent, which "$100bn of cloud ARR" was not.** MS's day-after note read it correctly (">$100bn total company ARR"); this page's Current-state bullet did not. Old framing moved to `## Changelog`.

**② RESOLVED — the memory quote, now verbatim rather than machine-translated.** The page carried this via a Chinese-language tweet with the caveat *"treat the exact wording as approximate, the substance as clear."* **Substance confirmed exactly.** Musk, verbatim: ***"Then look at the rate at which logic and memory is being produced. And one was always considered a limiting factor here. LIMITING FACTOR CURRENTLY IS MEMORY. The memory output is increasing by around 20% per year. Now normally that would be a fantastically fast and amazing for any large mature industry. But ask yourself, is the demand increasing by 20% a year? No, THE DEMAND IS INCREASING BY 200% A YEAR, MAYBE HIGHER. So if you've got demand increasing much faster than supply, then Economics 101 would suggest that THE PRICE INCREASES. IT DOES NOT DECREASE."*** ⚠️ **Note the context the relay stripped: Musk said this while arguing that intelligence-per-watt is rising so fast that "even an H100 is able to deliver useful AI comparable to a human engineer" is "not an impossible outcome" — i.e. the memory-price call is embedded in a DEFLATIONARY compute-efficiency argument, not a naive demand-extrapolation.** Cross-filed [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]].

**③ RESOLVED — NVIDIA exclusivity, verbatim.** The relay had *"We have decided to build entirely on NVIDIA. We think Vera is the best architecture."* Actual: ***"And going forward, WE'VE DECIDED TO BUILD EXCLUSIVELY ON NVIDIA because we think the Vera Rubin architecture is the best architecture. We think it's the best AI computer, and we greatly value our close cooperation and partnership on many levels with NVIDIA. So, WE'RE EXCLUSIVE TO NVIDIA."*** And on allocation, to Adam Jonas: ***"our understanding with NVIDIA is that WE WILL RECEIVE A VERY SIGNIFICANT PERCENTAGE OF THEIR GPUs NEXT YEAR."*** ⚠️ Confirms the negative read-through for [[AMD]] and the custom-ASIC complex at this account — and note it sits directly against the [[MEDIATEK]]-second-ASIC-customer speculation this page carries from JPM and Fubon: **a company that just said "we're exclusive to NVIDIA" is a poor fit for a merchant-ASIC programme.** That thread should now be treated as materially less likely unless it refers to a non-compute ASIC.

**④ RESOLVED — the sub-1-year payback, and the CFO said it twice.** The page flagged it *"Unverified and hard to reconcile with a 6.3x AI capex/revenue ratio — press it on the transcript."* Bret Johnsen, prepared remarks: ***"The current economics have translated into a LESS THAN ONE-YEAR PAYBACK on our new capital deployments for Compute."*** And in Q&A, with the accounting analogy that is the actually-useful part: ***"we're able to deploy capital in such a way that we're getting less than a one year payback. IT'S ALMOST LIKE YOUR CAPITAL IS BECOMING ALMOST AN ITEM THAT YOU WOULD PUT OTHERWISE IN COGS because it's moving so quickly as far as monetization and moving into revenue."*** ⚠️ **It is a claim about NEW INCREMENTAL deployments at current contract pricing, not about the installed base — which is how it reconciles with a 6.3x AI capex/revenue ratio at the segment level. Still management's own unaudited framing; the check is whether AI-segment EBITDA keeps converting at Q2's rate.**

### 🆕 NET-NEW from the transcript — five things no relay carried

**1. 🔴 THE 20 GW POWER TARGET — and it is a DIFFERENT GW BASIS from the 10 GW everyone quoted.** Adam Jonas asked Musk to confirm the "closer to 10 than 5" figure and whether he had line of sight on permitting, chips and turbines. The answer went somewhere else entirely: ***"we're actually aiming to FAR EXCEED that gigawatt number in terms of power online, power cooling and electrical equipment. So OUR TENTATIVE TARGET IS TO ACTUALLY HAVE 20 GIGAWATTS AT THE POWER AND COOLING LEVEL ONLINE BY THE END OF NEXT YEAR. Now, I DON'T THINK WE'RE GOING TO ACHIEVE 20 GIGAWATTS, but we want to have a series of projects that cumulatively come to 20 gigawatts by the end of next year… I would expect that we still probably have AT THE POWER PLANT LEVEL, SOMETHING CLOSE TO 15 GIGAWATTS. So assuming that maybe a quarter of the projects take longer than expected."*** The rationale, which is the strategically important half: ***"our goal is to have FAR MORE POWER COOLING AND ELECTRICAL EQUIPMENT THAN WE HAVE GPUs. That's like logical thing to do given the RELATIVE EXPENSE OF GPUs VERSUS BALANCE OF SYSTEM."*** ⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT COLLAPSE THESE TWO NUMBERS. "~10 GW by end-2027" is NAMEPLATE COMPUTE (IT load). "15-20 GW by end-2027" is POWER AND COOLING / power-plant level — deliberately overbuilt ahead of the GPUs. This page, and [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), must carry them on separate bases (see `_meta/assumptions.md` on never mixing GW bases).** ⚠️ **The read-through is large and it is NOT to NVDA: a named buyer deliberately overbuilding electrical + cooling ahead of silicon, at a 1.5-2x ratio to IT load, is a demand statement for turbines, transformers, switchgear, gensets and cooling — the [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) complex — that is strictly larger than the compute number the Street is modelling off. It also directly answers the Citi-roundtable "TOWERS over DATA CENTERS 2-to-1" preference logged above with the opposite signal.**

**2. 🔴 MANAGEMENT'S OWN $/WATT: "somewhere between $30 and $50."** Asked by JPM's Anmuth whether premium compute pricing is sustainable, Musk: ***"if you say like what's my best guess for monetization per watt of say Rubin's — MY GUESS IS WOULD BE SOMEWHERE BETWEEN $30 AND $50. This is just a guess, I'm just, you know, take a guess as a guess."*** ⚠️ **$30-50/W = $30-50bn PER GIGAWATT — and this is the single most contested number on this page. It sits directly on top of: New Street's bear framing of "the spot-vs-planned compute arb: ~$50B/GW (the $15B / 0.3GW Anthropic deal) vs [[CRWV]]'s ~$12B/GW — and why that premium may not last"; Gavin Baker's "$50 BILLION A GIGAWATT"; and MS's base case of $16.5/Watt on avg 6 GW in FY28. Management is now on the record at 2-3x MS's modelled $/W and at the level New Street says is unsustainable. Explicitly hedged as "just a guess" — but it is the CEO's guess, and it brackets the bull case rather than the base case.** Note also this is monetization per watt of **Rubin** specifically, i.e. the newest and most expensive generation — not a fleet average.

**3. 🔴 ONLY ~10% OF COMPUTE GOES TO GROK TRAINING.** UBS's Hodulik asked directly how much of the 2027 compute ramp must be withheld for internal training versus leased out. Musk: ***"for Grok training, we do expect the percentage of compute that we use internally for Grok training to DECREASE OVER TIME, and to have far more of the compute used for inference or for renting training inference compute to others. So, I WOULD EXPECT MAYBE 10% OF OUR COMPUTE TO BE USED FOR GROK TRAINING, something like that."*** ⚠️ **This is the number the neocloud thesis needed and nobody had: ~90% of a ~10 GW 2027 fleet is available for external monetization. It is also the cleanest rebuttal on this page to the "SpaceX is really just an internal-compute story" objection — and it materially raises the ceiling on the AI segment versus any model assuming a large internal-training reserve.**

**4. 🔴 $6.7bn OF ADDITIONAL CLOUD REVENUE ALREADY CONTRACTED IN THE FIRST WEEKS OF Q3.** Bret Johnsen: ***"in the first few weeks of the third quarter, we've already contracted an ADDITIONAL $6.7 BILLION OF CLOUD SERVICES REVENUE OVER A SIX-MONTH PERIOD that begins ramping starting in OCTOBER of this year."*** ⚠️ **A hard, dated, incremental contract number disclosed 34 days into the quarter — on top of the [[GOOG]]/[[ANTHROPIC]] deals, which Johnsen separately confirmed "start to ramp EITHER LATER THIS QUARTER OR IN OCTOBER." $6.7bn over six months ≈ $13.4bn annualized, against Cantor's ~$26bn-annualized sizing of the Google+Anthropic pair and FundaAI's ~$28bn-at-full-rate. This is the mechanical bridge to the $100bn December ARR claim and it is checkable at the Q3 print.**

**5. 🔴 THE STARMIND NVL72 IS COMING TO THE GROUND, NOT JUST TO ORBIT.** The page carries Starmind as the orbital-compute thread. The transcript adds the terrestrial half: the Starmind satellite ***"will be essentially an OPTIMIZED VERA RUBIN NVL72 COMPUTER"***, launching next year — and then: ***"we think the design of the NVL72 VR computer is a much better design than is typical than say, having a STANDARD RACK-STYLE DESIGN, and so WE EXPECT TO ACTUALLY DEPLOY THIS ON THE GROUND AS WELL AS IN ORBIT, because we think it's going to be a RADICAL SIMPLIFICATION of the normal NVL72 rack, IT WILL COST LESS, it will be more effective. And so, if we're going to put it in space, why not put it on the ground?"*** ⚠️ **A self-designed, simplified, cheaper NVL72 deployed terrestrially by the buyer who just declared NVDA exclusivity is a two-sided read: neutral-to-positive for [[NVDA]] silicon, but a negative signal for the standard rack/ODM/integration layer at this account, and a datapoint for the "SpaceX builds datacenters at ~half industry cost per watt" claim MS underwrites (~$4-5 vs $9/W ex-chips).**

### The segment, balance-sheet and operating detail the release recap did not carry

**Segments (Bret Johnsen, prepared remarks):**
- **Space: revenue $962M, +55% q/q, +29% y/y** — driven by more large customer launches and favourable mix; segment costs +$389M y/y on Starship R&D. ⚠️ **Space adjusted EBITDA was a LOSS of $205M.** H1-26 operations: **78 launches, 1,041 tons to orbit.** **>$6bn of US government contracts won in Q2** (Space Force).
- **Connectivity: revenue $4.3bn, +32% q/q, +66% y/y.** Costs +$970M (+~58%) y/y. **Income from operations +79% y/y to $1.7bn** (~3pts of operating-margin expansion); **adjusted EBITDA +64% y/y to $2.6bn.** ⚠️ **Enterprise & government revenue +108% y/y** — and see the reframing below.
- **AI: revenue $2.6bn, +213% q/q, +247% y/y**, driven by the new cloud agreements plus Grok/X subscriptions; **advertising +7% q/q**. **The initial ramp of the Colossus / Colossus 2 cloud agreements contributed $1.6bn of incremental AI infrastructure revenue in the quarter.** ⚠️ **AI net operating loss narrowed to $1.3bn and ADJUSTED EBITDA TURNED POSITIVE AT $1.1bn** — *"The incremental revenue from new hosting deals generated HIGH INCREMENTAL EBITDA MARGINS as we monetized available compute capacity."* **This supersedes the bear-case line this page carries (AI-segment Adj. EBITDA $(1,237)M in 2025, $(609)M in Q1-26 "as it scales compute") — the segment inflected positive one quarter later; prior framing moved to `## Changelog`.**
- **Compute nameplate trail, now complete: 400 MW (Q2-25) → 1.0 GW (Q1-26) → 1.4 GW (Q2-26) → >2 GW (exit 2026) → ~10 GW (exit 2027).**
- **Capex $18.4bn, of which ~$15.8bn AI compute**; remainder Starship/launch infra, satellite production, ground stations. **Separately $856M paid under the spectrum credit agreement for the pending EchoStar transaction** (⚠️ not in the capex figure). Guide: ***"the next two quarters are very similar to the current quarter from a CapEx level perspective."***

**Balance sheet — the financing answer this page has been asking for (Johnsen):** **IPO net proceeds ~$85.7bn**, followed by a **$25bn inaugural investment-grade senior-notes offering across FIVE TRANCHES at a weighted-average interest rate of 5.855% and average maturity of 11.7 years**, partially used to repay a **$20bn bridge loan**. **Quarter-end: $100bn of cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities, and $47.5bn of backlog.** ⚠️ **Against ~$18.4bn/quarter of capex guided flat for two more quarters (~$37bn of 2H spend), a $100bn cash pile covers roughly 5-6 quarters at the current burn before external capital is needed again — which is the concrete answer to The Information's "it doesn't have the same deep pockets" framing and to MS's "~$84bn/yr external capital '27-34" requirement. It does not resolve the 2027+ funding question; it removes it for 2026.**

**Starlink operating detail (Shotwell), most of it net-new to this page:**
- **Q2 consumer net adds: +1.7M, "our best quarter of new customers to date," up from 1.4M in Q1** — with **ARPU held flat q/q at $66.** ⚠️ **The page recorded "12M subs vs a 12M bogey / ARPU $66 vs $66 — both exactly in line" from the relay. The net-add ACCELERATION (1.4M → 1.7M) is the part that was missing, and it is a better number than "in line" implies.**
- **Constellation as of Jun 30: ~10,200 operational broadband + mobile satellites, of which 9,600 broadband delivering ~800 Tbps of total downlink capacity.**
- **Service in 167 markets** (⚠️ Musk said "170 markets" in prepared remarks, Shotwell said 167 — a minor internal inconsistency in the same transcript; use 167, the operational figure).
- **Aviation: American Airlines signed in Q2; Southwest, Virgin Atlantic, Iberia and Aer Lingus activated. "Less than 10% penetrated" in aviation.** Colour worth keeping: *"we heard from one of our airline customers… customers are flying SHORTER HOP FLIGHTS INSTEAD OF DIRECT, so that they could ensure that they're on a Starlink activated flight. They'd never seen this in the business before."*
- **New international mobile partners: SoftBank, NTT Docomo, Spark New Zealand.**
- ⚠️ **THE REFRAME: "I would expect ENTERPRISE REVENUE TO SUBSTANTIALLY EXCEED CONSUMER REVENUE" (Musk), with Shotwell adding "WE HAVE NEVER LOST AN ENTERPRISE CUSTOMER" and that enterprise+government "have the potential to reach a scale at least comparable but likely EXCEED our consumer business."** **This cuts against the entire sell-side framing of Starlink as a consumer-subscriber story (JPM models subs 9M→95M+ by 2030; MS 35mn avg consumer subs @ $46/mo in FY28) and against the bear case built on consumer ARPU dilution from geographic mix. Enterprise/government at +108% y/y is already growing ~1.6x faster than the segment.**

**Starlink Mobile — the D2C build, with the capex question explicitly refused (Shotwell + Musk):** current system uses **~5 MHz via local telco providers**; the **65 MHz of EchoStar spectrum** plus **~10x the satellites** makes it *"100 times better, 10 times 10 better than what we've got right now."* **Mobile V2 satellites start flying next year; service starts end of next year.** On the US opportunity: ***"The big three in the United States — AT&T, Verizon and TMO, roughly between them $600 BILLION A YEAR, and I ANTICIPATE US TO BE ABLE TO ACQUIRE QUITE A FEW OF THEIR CUSTOMERS, because I think our service will be better."*** ⚠️ **Evercore's Kutgun Maral pressed the obvious objection — becoming a true fourth carrier "would imply well over $100 BILLION of mobile CapEx over time" — and Shotwell DECLINED to give a capex figure twice ("I'm not going to talk about the CapEx for that right now"), while pre-empting the premise: the EchoStar spectrum has terrestrial components, they do intend to build terrestrial, but via "a cellular base station on… the gear that holds a Starlink broadband dish — so you can have kind of these little FEMTOCELLS around the country, and you deploy that as you need it. You don't have to necessarily spend 10 or many billions of dollars in low-band spectrum all upfront."** Musk reinforced it: rooftop Starlink dishes doubling as mobile-band small cells with *"a clear view for providing connectivity directly to the cellphones on the ground… probably better and higher bandwidth than what is currently available from cellular providers."* ⚠️ **Log this as the mechanism behind the "D2C without carrier capex" claim the relay reported without specifics. It is unproven and unquantified — but it is a coherent architecture, not hand-waving, and it is the direct answer to the Citi-roundtable terminal-value panic in wireless logged above.**

**Starship — and it materially widens the gap to the New Street bear case (Musk):** Flight 13 *"went incredibly well. The heat shield appears to be very robust. The ship is still floating in the ocean."* **Ship-catch attempt on the next flight, "tentatively scheduled for the END OF THIS MONTH," regulatory approval permitting. Flight 14 is the first to fly V3 Starlink satellites to operational orbit.** ⚠️ **The cadence claim: *"We expect the cadence of flights to be increasing rapidly and probably A YEAR FROM NOW, WE WILL BE DOING AT LEAST ONE FLIGHT A DAY, possibly more."* Set that against New Street's bear case on this page, which needs ~1 Starship launch A WEEK and runs "~1 year behind Elon's timeline" — Musk is now guiding to ~7x New Street's required cadence within 12 months. Even applying New Street's own one-year haircut, the bear model's gating assumption is cleared. This is the single largest fundamental disagreement on the page and the transcript moves it.** On the heat shield, asked if it is the hardest remaining problem: ***"I would consider the HEAT SHIELD PROBLEM SOLVED at this point… we DO NOT SEE ANY TECHNICAL OBSTACLES at this point to achieving FULL AND RAPID REUSABILITY."*** ⚠️ Musk's own hedge included (*"I don't want to jinx it"*); the recovered ship is still to be inspected. **Starship economics restated: "quadruple payload capacity and reduce launch costs by 10 times compared to Falcon 9."** Tonnage framing: **~2,500 tons/yr to orbit today via Falcon (~80-90% of global mass to orbit; rest of world ~300 tons) → aspiration "well over 1 MILLION tons per year, and probably ultimately 10 MILLION tons per year."**

**Starlink V3 economics, in Musk's own arithmetic:** V3 is *"about an order of magnitude more capable"* than V2 and they expect to launch *"about an order of magnitude more"* of them — **"a roughly two order of magnitude increase in the delivered bandwidth. EVEN IF OUR MONETIZATION PER BIT DROPPED BY A FACTOR OF 10, THAT WOULD STILL MEAN 10 TIMES INCREASE IN THE REVENUE OF STARLINK."** **Critical mass for service improvement: ~1,000 V3 satellites, "approximately second quarter next year."** ⚠️ **This is the bull case for Connectivity stated as a bounded calculation rather than a vision, and it is the reason Musk pulled the $1T revenue date forward — he named the V3 step-change explicitly when asked what gave him the extra confidence.**

**Grok / AI model cadence (Musk):** **Grok 4.6 "probably next week"; Grok 4.7 "about three or four weeks from today"; Grok 5 before end of this year, incorporating "the ENTIRE CORPUS OF SPACEX DATA… all the data that SpaceX has ever produced… over the course of a quarter century"** — which Musk expects will make Grok *"by far the best engineer."* **Shotwell: enterprise feedback on Grok 4.5 "very positive," and TOKEN CONSUMPTION TRIPLED out of the gate after the July release.** Cross-filed [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).

**$1T revenue pulled forward, verbatim (Musk):** ***"our internal projections for reaching $1 TRILLION IN REVENUE, NOT ARR, BUT REVENUE, have moved up from 2031 to 2030. So prior to the IPO, the financial projections we had were reaching $1 trillion in revenue in 2031. We now expect that to be in 2030 and THERE'S A NON-ZERO CHANCE OF THAT BEING IN 2029."***

**Also on the call, logged for completeness:** **Cursor acquisition** — *"should close quite soon… we've withdrew almost all of the regulatory hurdles"*; product roadmap deliberately deferred to a separate announcement to avoid *"jumping the gun on regulators."* **Artemis / HLS (Shotwell): orbital propellant transfer is the critical milestone; ARTEMIS III NEXT YEAR (docking with Orion), then an uncrewed direct-to-lunar cargo mission, then "BOOTS ON THE MOON IN 2028."** Musk expects human-rated Starship safety *"probably by the end of next year."* **Competitive posture (Musk): "we actually ENCOURAGE OUR COMPETITORS TO COPY US. We do nothing to slow them down. We launch competing satellite constellations at… the same price we charge everyone… we don't use patents as a landmine exercise."** **On [[ANTHROPIC]]: Musk volunteered that Claude 4.5 "should be considered ONE OF THE MILESTONES, and the credit to Anthropic… it was a SHOCK TO THE SYSTEM, how good Claude 4.5 was"** — a competitor CEO's unprompted assessment, cross-filed to [[ANTHROPIC]].

## 🆕 The day-after sell-side verdict (2026-08-05) — four houses, three PT raises, one reiteration, and nobody downgraded

**The published research landed the morning after and it is remarkably consistent: every house raised numbers, none disputed the quarter, and the debate moved entirely to capex and the 08-06 unlock.**

| House / analyst | Rating | PT | The one-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MS · Adam Jonas** | Overweight | **$300 — REITERATED** | *"2Q featured a **16% revenue beat, 75% EBITDA beat**. Target for **>$100bn total company ARR (end Dec '26)** and **5 to 10GW of compute (YE27)** help 'feed the ducks' on the outlook. **Higher estimates offset by higher capex.**"* |
| **JPM · Anmuth / Seifman / Seb** | Overweight | **raised to $240** | *"Strong Debut w/ **AI & Compute Driving Accelerated Growth Trajectory**"* |
| **GS · Eric Sheridan** | Buy | **$205 → $220** | *"Inaugural Earnings Report Reinforces Multiple Large Addressable Markets; **Incremental Progress on AI Stands Out** & Drives Operating Estimate Revisions"* |
| **UBS · John Hodulik** | Buy | — | *"**Solid progress on all fronts**"* |

**★ MS's numbers, because they are the most explicit re-rate on the page (Adam Jonas, 2026-08-05):**
- **"$1.4bn EBIT beat driven by timing of AI neocloud revenue, Starlink Government & Enterprise and Space"**, with **stronger EBIT margins across EVERY segment**.
- ⚠️ **The capex problem, stated precisely: "$18bn of capex (including Echostar payments) was MODESTLY LOWER than MS estimate ($20.5bn)… HOWEVER the company is guiding to a SIMILAR LEVEL OF CAPEX PER QUARTER FOR THE REMAINDER OF THE YEAR, implying approximately incremental ~$18BN OF 2H CAPEX ON TOP OF OUR PRIOR FORECAST."** That is the mechanism behind "higher estimates offset by higher capex" and why a 75% EBITDA beat produced no PT change.
- **Forecast changes: FY26 revenue $45bn → $48bn; FY27 $91bn → $102bn.**
- **"Elon Musk pulled a few rabbits out of the guidance hat":** **≥$100bn TOTAL COMPANY ARR by end-December 2026** — against what MS's own prior 4Q forecast implied (**$19.1bn**); **"closer to 10GW than 5GW" of year-end-2027 nameplate compute** vs **MS at 4.15GW**; **V3 broadband sats deploying from the next Starship launch (flight 14), targeting 1,000 V3 sats by 2Q27** vs MS at 840; and the **heat-shield problem "largely solved"** on flight 13 evidence.
- **Consensus direction: "Modestly Positive for 2026/2027… We'd expect consensus forecasts to rise modestly for the next 12 to 18 months." ⚠️ But with a tail caveat: "we believe consensus forecasts for 2029-2031 are FULL/COULD FACE MODEST DOWNSIDE RISK, although 2Q is unlikely to 'trigger' a negative revision."**
- ⚠️ **On the unlock, MS frames it as the entry rather than the risk: "On Aug 6th, investors will see an unlock of around $100BN worth of incremental SPCX, which we see as AN OPPORTUNITY to gain exposure to a potential generational compounder."** ⚠️ **Note this is the mirror image of DB's read (below) that the lockup is a clearing event — the two houses agree on the mechanics and differ only on whether to buy before or after it clears.**

**GS's framing of the same print (Eric Sheridan, 2026-08-05):** three themes — **Space** (Starship flights 12 & 13 building momentum toward frequency/reusability), **Connectivity** (Starlink broadband subs **exceeded** GS forecasts with **minimal ARPU dilution**, against investor concern about where the incremental units were coming from geographically, plus optimism on the Enterprise/Government backlog), and **AI** (segment revenue and operating income **exceeded** forecasts, with management optimistic about **"building to more terrestrial compute and SIGNIFICANTLY ABOVE-AVERAGE MONETIZATION OF COMPUTE (given the current supply/demand imbalance in the industry) in the coming 12-18 months"**). ⚠️ **GS's caution is about price discovery, not fundamentals: "we expect that the shares will likely remain VOLATILE as elements of additional liquidity could result in MORE WIDELY DISTRIBUTED PRICE DISCOVERY in the coming days," and the long-term debates (Starship cadence, Starlink broadband/mobile scaling, orbital compute) are "unlikely to be resolved in the coming months/quarters."**

### ★ The two things from the call with read-through beyond this page

**1. MEMORY IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT — Musk said so, in economics-101 terms, on an earnings call.** Quoted from the call: **"The current limiting factor is memory. Memory output increases roughly 20% per year. For any large, mature industry that is normally an extremely fast, extremely impressive rate. But think about it — is demand increasing 20% per year? No, demand is increasing 200% per year, maybe more. So if demand is growing far faster than supply, per Economics 101, prices should go UP, not down."** (@QQ_Timmy quoting the SPCX Q2 2026 earnings call, 2026-08-05 — translated from Chinese.) ⚠️ **This is a hyperscale-scale BUYER of memory publicly arguing memory prices go up — the demand-side counterpart to everything [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]] and [[KIOXIA]] have been saying from the supply side, and it landed the same week [[SNDK]] guided "modest" ASP growth and its stock fell on peak-margin fears. Logged as an attributed quote from a machine translation of a call this page does not hold a transcript for — treat the exact wording as approximate, the substance as clear.** Cross-filed to [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

**2. THE COMPUTE BUILD IS ALL-[[NVDA]], AND IT IS EXCLUSIVE.** Musk on the call: **"We have decided to build entirely on NVIDIA. We think Vera is the best architecture and the best AI computer."** Musk expects **compute to exceed 2 GW by end of this year, and close to 10 GW (not 5 GW) by end-2027** (@QQ_Timmy, 2026-08-05). The market read it immediately: **"NVDA leads Mag 7 stocks higher, poised to extend gains for a fifth consecutive session, after SPCX announced an EXCLUSIVE PARTNERSHIP to build its future AI infrastructure ENTIRELY ON NVIDIA'S PLATFORMS"** (@zerohedge, 2026-08-05). ⚠️ **The sizing floating on the buy-side, logged as third-party arithmetic and NOT adopted: Melius Research reckons 6 GW of GPU compute bought by SpaceX in 2027 would add ">$200 BILLION" to [[NVDA]]'s 2027 revenue, and "a more realistic 2-3 GW would add $100 billion" (@firstadopter, 2026-08-05). Those figures imply ~$33-35bn of NVDA revenue per GW, which is 2-3x the $10-20bn/GW range this wiki carries elsewhere for total system revenue — do not use it without reconciling the GW basis.**

## 🆕 The 10GW question gets its first bottom-up defence — and a named largest offtaker (2026-08-07)

**🔴 SemiAnalysis (Ontiveros / Knuhtsen / Nanos / Kan / Patel / Zuhair), "SpaceX 10GW in 2027 – Why It's Real, Will Drive $500B ARR for SpaceX, and Why Microsoft Will Be the Largest Offtaker" (2026-08-07; via the daniel.grozdea research forward).** The first published work that tries to *verify* the number rather than react to it.
- **The claim being tested, restated: Musk "conservatively" aims to build and deliver an INCREMENTAL 6–8GW IN 2027 ALONE, "with potential for that number to be well above +10GW. AT 50B PER GW, THAT'S $300–500B IN CAPEX IN 2027, ON PAR WITH WHAT WE EXPECT FROM [[AMZN]] AND [[GOOG]] — an unbelievable number for a company SIGNIFICANTLY LESS PROFITABLE THAN RIVAL HYPERSCALERS."**
- **The verdict: "we believe THE NUMBER IS REAL. We see SpaceX ON TRACK TO BUILD ABOUT 10GW BY YEAR-END 2027."** ⚠️ **The support is model-based and paywalled — "we've evaluated all sites suitable for SpaceX and provided the list to our Datacenter Model subscribers," plus a quarter-by-quarter gas-generation equipment list across 30+ turbine/engine/fuel-cell suppliers. The site list and equipment schedule are NOT in the captured text; the conclusion is, and is logged as an attributed claim, not as verified capacity.**
- 🔴 **THE ECONOMIC ENGINE — and it is the number that makes the capex arithmetic survivable: "our Tokenomics Model and Inference Simulator demonstrate that at realistic performance levels, BOTH [[OPENAI]] AND [[ANTHROPIC]] CAN GENERATE OVER $100B/GW/YEAR OF REVENUE when selling API inference on a GB300 cluster," against "AROUND $12B/GW/YEAR OF COST… using a conservative rental pricing rate of $3/GPU-hr."** ⚠️ **That is an ~8x revenue-to-rental-cost ratio and it is the single most aggressive economic assumption on this page. It is a MODELLED figure from a proprietary simulator, blending input / cache-read / cache-write / output tokens at the authors' own agentic-coding workload ratios (their "AgentX" benchmark). Not adopted. But note the direction of travel: this wiki already carries SemiAnalysis's earlier margin work (inference GM >60% from January, Opus 4.8 at 85%+ from June) and the leaked DeepSeek investor call citing a 10-MONTH GPU PAYBACK as an independent sanity check.**
- **SPCX's own pricing, per the same note: "SpaceX will continue to sell large-scale compute with 3–5 MONTHS LEAD TIME, an unbeatable offering, and price it accordingly at 30–50M/MW/YEAR. THAT PAYS BACK THE CAPEX IN LESS THAN A YEAR."** ✅ **Independently consistent with two figures already on this page from the call itself — management's "payback periods UNDER ONE YEAR" and the "$30 TO $50 PER WATT for Rubin systems" monetization estimate (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07). Same numbers, different route. The $30–50/MW/year and $30–50/W framings are ARITHMETICALLY THE SAME THING ($30–50M per MW = $30–50 per W); do not double-count them as two datapoints.**
- **THE FINANCING ANSWER, which is the open question this page has carried since the print: two legs. "(1) SUPPORT FROM [[NVDA]], IN THE FORM OF VENDOR FINANCING to lower the upfront cash cost. THIS IS LIKELY WHY ELON DECLARED TO BE NVIDIA EXCLUSIVE ON THE EARNINGS CALL — xAI/SpaceX have actively evaluated alternatives like TPU and AMD, so THE FINANCIAL ARGUMENT LIKELY MADE THEM ABANDON THESE. (2) INDUSTRY-HIGH PRICING, ENABLED BY FASTEST TIMELINES."** ⚠️ **This is a materially different explanation of the NVIDIA exclusivity than the one on this page, which carries Musk's technical framing ("we think Vera is the best architecture and the best AI computer"). Both retained; the vendor-financing reading is a SemiAnalysis inference, not a disclosure.**
- **The ARR conclusion: "a path to $300B OF ARR BY THE END OF 2027 for SpaceX. THIS ASSUMES ONLY 50% OF THEIR 2027 INCREMENTAL COMPUTE IS MONETIZED, the remainder being for the Grok & Cursor teams for training (NO INFERENCE REVENUE MODELLED)."** ⚠️ **Note the headline says $500B ARR and the body says $300B on a 50%-monetization assumption — the two are not reconciled in the captured text. Carry the $300B, flag the $500B.**
- 🔴 **AND THE READ-THROUGH THAT MAKES THIS A [[MSFT]] NOTE AS MUCH AS A SPCX NOTE: "there is actually a THIRD COMPANY in the world capable of printing such numbers: MICROSOFT. Having full access to OpenAI models, they can generate the exact same revenue and margin per MW WHILE PAYING NONE OF THE TRAINING COSTS. Satya nailed the negotiations with OpenAI: THE DEAL REWORKED IN APRIL 2026 DROPPED THE OLD 20% REVENUE SHARE from the equation."** **"Microsoft has contracted OVER 10GW across [leasing, neocloud contracting, self-build starts and binding PPAs/ESAs] — the equivalent of ~$300B IN NEW BINDING COMMITMENTS" year-to-date, reversing "last year's pause." The impact claimed: "MICROSOFT AZURE ACCELERATING REVENUE GROWTH FROM ~42% TO OVER 100% BY NEXT YEAR."** ⚠️ **Extraordinary claim, single source, modelled. Logged in full on the [[MSFT]] page as a flagged claim, not adopted.** Mechanism given: MSFT's October-2025 $250B OpenAI agreement (~7GW on SemiAnalysis's estimate) "has left Microsoft HIGHLY COMPUTE-CONSTRAINED on their other use-cases… unable to leverage their access to OpenAI models for their API business Foundry, or for their applications like Copilot" — the highest-margin uses. Current OpenAI capacity is served at **~$14M/MW/year**, versus the ~$100M/MW/year opportunity.

### 🆕 THE COMPANION PODCAST TO THE SAME NOTE — the supply-side workings that were paywalled, one flagged conflict effectively resolved, and a demand risk the note did not carry (~2026-08-07/11)

**SemiAnalysis "Something Else Weekly" Ep. 024, "SpaceX's 10GW Plan Drives $300B ARR by 2027" — hosts Jordan (Nanos) + Jeremy + "Rake"/Rick; Zuhair named in-episode.** ⚠️ **THIS IS THE SAME SOURCE AS THE 08-07 NOTE ABOVE, NOT A SECOND ONE — where a number appears in both, cite the note and do not double-count.** ⚠️ **Publisher and publish date are UNCONFIRMED and flagged for Felipe: the pasted title carries no publisher, and the date is bounded only by internal evidence to ~08-07→08-11 (recorded after the article; "the site scan was two days ago"). Attribution rests on in-episode corroboration — the hosts call it "the article we put out," two speaker names match the note's byline already on this page, and the proprietary assets named (inference simulator, InferenceMAX-style benchmark, Tokenomics model, Datacenter Model subscribers, Taiwan supply-chain team) match.** [Full digest + raw transcript](../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-07_SemiAnalysis_SpaceX-10GW-podcast.md)

- ✅ **THE 90-DAY CANCELLATION CLAUSE GETS A SECOND INDEPENDENT SOURCE — this is the most useful thing in the episode, because it moves a conflict this page has carried since 07-28.** *"An amazing term in the contract… is the **90-day cancellation policy**, because for a Google, for a Microsoft, for an Anthropic they have **zero risk**"* — applied to **[[ANTHROPIC]], [[GOOG]] and Reflection, "same terms,"** with a monthly payment annualising to *"about 50 million bucks [per MW] a year."* ➜ **The page's open item (a) under the FUNDA 07-28 block — FUNDA's "90-day termination + on-demand pricing" vs Bernstein's "multi-decade clauses (Anthropic and WULF cited)" (07-11) — should now be scored in FUNDA's favour. Bernstein's line is NOT withdrawn, but it is now the minority read.** 🔴 **THERE ARE ACTUALLY THREE SOURCES, AND THE EARLIEST PREDATES BOTH — surfaced from this wiki's own corpus on 2026-08-12: the 2026-05-29 company-specific briefing already carried "@wallstengine (15K views): Anthropic-SpaceX compute deal is a 180-DAY BASE TERM + 90-DAY CANCELLATION, NOT the $45B 36-month figure widely circulated. Closer to a ~$7.5B bridge capacity" — also logged on the [[ANTHROPIC]] briefing page as "compute access is a bridge, not a long-term anchor."** ⚠️ **NOTE THE EXTRA TERM ONLY THE MAY SOURCE CARRIES: a 180-DAY BASE before the 90-day cancellation right attaches. The podcast describes the clause as if cancellation were available from day one, on Anthropic, Google and Reflection alike. The two are not the same contract shape — do not merge them.** ⚠️ **The whole thing cuts against the DURATION of the neocloud revenue, not its level — the same $50M/MW/year, terminable.**
- 🔴 **NET-NEW MARK — REVENUE PER MEGAWATT BY CHIP GENERATION: GB200 $73.4M/MW/year → GB300 $99.7M/MW/year (+36%)**, off what they call a *minor* Nvidia architectural change (memory capacity, memory bandwidth, FP4 flops). ⚠️ **The forward leg of that series rests on an ASR-garbled architecture placeholder ("Fable 5" — the speaker's own words are "our fake … assumptions for the architecture"). Cite the GB200/GB300 pair; do NOT cite the forward number.**
- ✅ **THE 85% LAB GROSS MARGIN IS INTERNALLY CONSISTENT, which is worth recording because the ~$100B/GW/year assumption above is the page's most aggressive:** *"the leaked financials we're seeing from the labs right now is showing 85% gross margins… which would imply around 100 billion of selling [revenue] at a cost of around $15 billion per gigawatt."* **(100 − 15)/100 = 85% ✓.** And the 85% is *"a blend which includes older or less performing GPUs, not just the GB300 — so you could argue 100 is actually conservative for GB300."* **Method disclosed for the first time (inputs still proprietary): a LOWER bound from real workloads on GB200/GB300 running open models as frontier proxies (Kimi K3 ~3T params, DeepSeek sparse attention, ~1M context), and an UPPER bound from an op-by-op simulator (GEMMs + collectives measured on real hardware) traced through an assumed frontier-model shape.**
- ⚠️ **NUMBER CONFLICT — LOGGED, NOT RESOLVED: the Google lease is quoted here at "the equivalent of $14 an hour" per GPU (vs a ~$3/hr average GB300 on-demand rate). This page carries $11.50/GPU-hour for the Google agreement from PRIMARY DOCUMENTS (S-1 + the Google agreement, via Exponential View/13D, 2026-08-06) — see the section directly below. $14 vs $11.50 is ~22% apart and neither source states its basis (whole-rack vs per-GPU, contract vintage). Both stand, separately attributed.** Reverse-engineered the same way, the deal is described as Google buying at *"roughly $48 billion per gigawatt"* needing *"a 50% gross margin to get to 100 billion"* — internally consistent with the $30–50M/MW/year band already on the page.
- 🔴 **THE SUPPLY-SIDE WORKINGS THE NOTE PAYWALLED — this is the part that is genuinely additive to the 10GW debate, and it speaks directly to the two gates FUNDA said decide it (power generation + permitting):**
  - **Sites:** ~**1M US permit sites scanned in ~2 days**, yielding **~5 strong candidates** including **~1M sq ft and ~800k sq ft warehouses** — *"over a gigawatt per million square feet, potentially two."* Some surfaced through **liens against MZX / Elon entities** (**MZX Tech** is already on this page as the Southaven subsidiary — an independent corroboration, not a garble).
  - **Why warehouses, and it is a permitting argument:** a warehouse is *"already permitted and zoned… All I need is an air permit"* — deliberately avoiding the construction-permit pole on powered land.
  - **Turbines:** **~7 GW of unidentified/undisclosed turbines** located, **excluding** secondary-market units (**Fermi** named as one such source — the same company 22V floated as an acquisition/offtake fit on 08-07). Separately: **Elon "already has something like 9 to 10 GW of turbines on order or in operations."** Other availability named: **[[ORCL]] in New Mexico**, **[[NBIS]] New Jersey** engines, delayed fuel cells.
  - 🔴 **LABOUR — called "the single bottleneck," and the sharpest new datapoint: Colossus 2 ran ~3,000 workers/day at peak, which per GW is "about 3x lower than what you see even from the very best data center developers" — Crusoe named as the benchmark being beaten.** Mechanism: extensive **prefabrication out of China**.
  - **Electrical/mechanical equipment: expects pre-assembled modules from China at scale** — ✅ **corroborates FUNDA's 07-28 finding of Chinese transformer vendors (Sanbian, Jinpan, Sieyuan, TBEA, China XD) backstopping the buildout.** The concession is explicit: you would not accept this on a 20-year high-SLA offtake, *"if it's an on-demand cluster, I think you kind of don't care."*
  - **Permitting, described without softening:** Mississippi built **over the state line to escape Tennessee permitting**, permitted ~**1.2 GW**, then **mobile turbines rolled in past the allowance to 69 turbines**, with the **DOJ intervening to say it was okay** — *"an unprecedented precedent."* ⚠️ **Read against the 22V constraint on this page ("the permitting path is getting harder, not easier, as we head into the midterms") and FUNDA's "every turbine at Colossus 1 operates unpermitted" — this source treats the regulatory seam as a solved problem BY PRECEDENT rather than by compliance. That is a bet, not a finding.**
  - **Siting mechanism, net-new:** at Colossus 2 the **power plant sits ~2 miles from the warehouse**, joined by a **private transmission line at medium voltage — "highly inefficient"** but fast; expect the pattern repeated (find the shell, then scout any nearby parcel permittable for air emissions).
  - **Chips:** their **Taiwan supply-chain team tracked orders for 5–10 GW for next year**, placed over the prior few months. ✅ Direction consistent with FUNDA's 15k Rubin racks (~4GW+) for 2027.
  - **SLA regime shift, with an external anchor: an [[ANTHROPIC]] self-build at 99.7% uptime — "no multiple nines, no tiers from the Uptime Institute"** — redundancy stripped to compress lead times and capex. **Tesla batteries** cited as SPCX's partial redundancy answer.
- **FINANCING — the arithmetic behind "pay for it with operating cash flow":** contracts signed on *"one to one and a half"* GW already produce **~$50bn annualised ≈ $4bn/month** at *"over 90% margins"* [⚠️ ASR garbles the margin tier between gross/EBITDA/EBIT — treat the tier as uncertain]; extrapolated to **10 GW built / 5 GW monetised at $50M/MW/year ≈ $250bn** of OCF. ⚠️ **The ~$50bn on ~1–1.5 GW implies ~$33–50M/MW/year — arithmetically INSIDE the $30–50M/MW/year band already on this page. Same number, not a second datapoint.** On [[NVDA]]: vendor financing *"likely to step in"* but *"I don't think anyone at all knows exactly what it's going to look like"* — supported by NVDA's overseas backstops and **direct datacenter leases (Hut 8 named)**.
- 🔴 **THE SEQUENCING READ ON MUSK'S OWN WORDS, and it is a good catch: Musk said gigawatts "of power and cooling"** — from which they infer **shells and power first, GPUs second**, because *"when you have the data center built and it's extremely close to delivery… you can bully your customer"* and financing options multiply. **Musk is quoted as having conceded the chips could fall short.**
- ⚠️ **AMBIGUOUS ANTECEDENT — DO NOT ATTACH TO SPCX's BALANCE SHEET WITHOUT CONFIRMATION.** The cancellable rentals are contrasted against *"the compute agreements or data center agreements they've signed to date… **those 10 gigawatts** equates to well over **$300 billion in total contractual value, binding contracts.** So that's going to be spent no matter what."* **"They" is never named. The 10GW / >$300bn pair matches the note's MICROSOFT year-to-date commitments exactly, so it most likely refers to [[MSFT]].**
- 🔴 **THE RISK THEY THEMSELVES NAME IS DEMAND-POLITICAL, NOT EXECUTION — and it is net-new vs the note.** Asked for the steelman against their own call: *"the biggest concern that I have is **not actually the execution**… It's that **demand has a problem in the future**. And not because I think the models aren't going to be good enough. I actually think **the models are going to be too good and then people will be so scared they're going to shut down access**… If there's any case where this doesn't work, it's **more political than technical or operations related**."* Trigger cited: an **[[OPENAI]] disclosure of a Hugging Face security incident** presented at **Black Hat**, tied to their **"Astra"** model in testing (compared to **Mythos** / **"Project Glasswing"** — cf. `_equity_calls/Overall/2026-06-18_BTG_Mythos-expert.md`), in which **autonomous agents coordinated a swarm attack via FILE NAMES on a remote JFrog Artifactory instance** and found a **zero-day in the HDF5 format**. A **forthcoming SemiAnalysis piece on neocloud security** is pre-announced: lower-tier providers running **three-year-old published CVEs**, with working PoC exploits built by a model *"in afternoons, not weeks."* ⚠️ **Note this cuts BOTH ways for SPCX: it is a sector-wide demand risk, but the same passage explicitly exempts SpaceX from the sloppy-security cohort ("providers out there that are not SpaceX, but are on the lower tier").**
- ✅ **PARTIAL RESOLUTION OF A FLAGGED DISCREPANCY ON THIS PAGE: the episode title says "$300B ARR by 2027" while the note's title says "$500B ARR."** This page already flags that the note's headline said $500B and its body said $300B on a 50%-monetisation assumption. **The authors' own podcast headline uses $300B — evidence the $300B is the operative figure. NOT a full reconciliation: neither the podcast nor the note explains the $500B.**
- ⚠️ **THE LOAD-BEARING DEMAND CLAIM, UNSOURCED AND NOT ADOPTED: "[[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]] combined are adding over $20 billion of ARR PER MONTH, actually close to 30 now… that drives AI revenue adding close to $400 billion per year."** **That is ARR ADDED PER MONTH, not ARR.** The stated mechanism is margin, not volume — *"gross margins for these labs just kept going up… that has been the key driver of their ARR acceleration"* — i.e. revenue per watt rising on a fixed compute base. **Single source, unaudited, no exhibit shown. Cross-check candidate: the ~$40B+ ARR on the [[ANTHROPIC]] page.**
- **Cross-filed to [[MSFT]] (the offtaker mechanism, with dated supporting deals — 7GW OpenAI offtake served at ~$12M/MW/year vs the ~$100M/MW/year opportunity; ~7GW of pre-leasing YTD; Fairwater Wisconsin; Nscale; the 2.7GW Chevron behind-the-meter deal in Pecos County TX modelled for 2028; and the late-26/1H-27 gap that is the actual trade) and [[NVDA]] (exclusivity-as-financing, Hut 8, the TPU-defence motive). Also touches [[GOOG]] — "we think Google is not going to be able to compete at the frontier," evidenced by the Jeff Dean / David Silver / John Jumper / Noam Shazeer departures raising $1–2bn seed rounds against ~$300bn of capex; logged as commentary, not a datapoint.**

## 🆕 THE LEASE RATES SHOW UP IN A THIRD-PARTY CHART — sourced to the S-1 and the Google agreement — and they are 2-8x below what the same GPU earns serving an open model (2026-08-06)

**🔴 13D Research & Strategy, "What I Learned This Week" (excerpt 2026-08-06), reproducing an Exponential View analysis titled "Running Kimi K3 pays 2–8x more than leasing the GPUs" — "Earnings per GPU-hour, USD, same hardware put to different uses," source line: "SpaceX S-1 filing & Google agreement; Kimi K3 API pricing; EV analysis."** ⚠️ **PROVENANCE FIRST: 13D is a thematic strategy publication, not a broker; the dropped PDF is watermarked as a StoneX-licensed copy and has no text layer (read by rendering its pages). The CHART is Exponential View's, not 13D's. But the two lease numbers are attributed to PRIMARY DOCUMENTS — this company's own S-1 and the Google agreement — which makes them the most checkable compute-pricing marks this page holds.**

| Same hardware, different use | Earnings per GPU-hour |
|---|---|
| **SPCX lease to [[ANTHROPIC]]** | **$7.80** |
| **SPCX lease to [[GOOG]]** | **$11.50** |
| Serving Kimi K3 inference — agentic pricing | **$23** |
| Serving Kimi K3 inference — chatbot pricing | **$60** |

- 🔴 **WHY THIS MATTERS TO THE SPCX THESIS RATHER THAN JUST TO THE AI DEBATE: this page's entire neocloud bull case rests on SpaceX capturing a PREMIUM for large-scale, near-term compute — "value-based pricing," "$30–50M/MW/year," "industry-high pricing enabled by fastest timelines," "payback in less than a year." This chart says the landlord leg of that business, as contracted with its two largest disclosed AI counterparties, earns $7.80–$11.50 per GPU-hour, while the MERCHANT leg — serving tokens off the same silicon — is modelled at 2–8x that.** ⚠️ **If both are right, the highest-value use of SpaceX's fleet is not renting it to Anthropic and Google. That is either (a) a large unexploited margin pool that no sell-side model on this page includes, or (b) evidence that the modelled merchant economics are unrealistic at scale. Do not resolve it here; it is now the sharpest open question on the compute segment and it is testable against the next disclosure of compute-segment revenue per GW.**
- ⚠️ **BASES — READ BEFORE USING, AND DO NOT NET AGAINST THE $30–50M/MW/YEAR MARK ABOVE.** The SemiAnalysis figure (2026-08-07) is **$ per MW per year**; this is **$ per GPU-hour**. Converting between them requires a GPU-per-MW assumption this wiki does not hold canonically, and the `_meta/assumptions.md` rule against mixing per-chip and per-watt bases applies directly. **Both marks stand, separately attributed, unconverted.** Further: the $7.80/$11.50 are **CONTRACTED** revenue with utilisation risk on the counterparty; the $23/$60 are **MODELLED gross revenue** at published Kimi K3 API pricing at unstated utilisation, carrying all the serving cost, latency-SLO and demand risk the lease does not. **It is a landlord-vs-merchant comparison, not a like-for-like.**
- ✅ **WHAT IT CORROBORATES: the direction is consistent with the "spot-vs-planned compute arb" already on this page from New Street (Ferragu, 2026-07-30) — "~$50B/GW (the $15B / 0.3GW Anthropic deal) vs [[CRWV]]'s ~$12B/GW — and why that premium may not last." A ~$7.80/GPU-hour Anthropic lease rate is the same story told per-chip: the Anthropic relationship is the LOW end of SpaceX's realised pricing, not the high end.** ⚠️ **Note the asymmetry the chart makes visible for the first time: the GOOGLE agreement prices ~47% ABOVE the Anthropic lease. This page carries the Google 9/30 delivery deadline as the nearest hard test on the compute contracts (FundaAI, 2026-07-29) — the same contract is now also the better-priced one.**
- **Cross-filed to [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md) (where the elasticity and value-capture framing sit), [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[GOOG]].** (13D Research & Strategy, "What I Learned This Week", excerpt 2026-08-06, reproducing Exponential View; underlying sources cited as the SpaceX S-1 filing and the Google agreement)

## 🆕 THE MODEL FRONTIER RE-ENTRY, AND A NEW COMPUTE ARCHITECTURE ARGUMENT — MS's "1TW" note (2026-08-13)

**Morgan Stanley · Adam Jonas / William Tackett, "SpaceX: The Fastest Way to 1TW of Compute (That Nobody's Talking About)?" — OVERWEIGHT, PT $300 UNCHANGED (2026-08-13).** Written *"after a nearly >20% run over the past 5 days"* — so read it as a post-rally justification, not a call initiation.

- **🔴 GROK IS BACK AT THE FRONTIER, AND THE MODEL CADENCE IS THE MOST DATABLE PART OF THE NOTE.** **Grok 4.6** — built on the **same 1.5tn-parameter foundation model as Grok 4.5** (mid-July) — scores **61 on Artificial Analysis's Intelligence Index**, against **63 for the highest-scoring model, 56 for Grok 4.5 and 38 for Grok 4.3**, at **the same cost/task as Kimi K3**, i.e. *"well on the Pareto Frontier."* Forward cadence, from Musk's own X posts and MS's reporting: **Grok 4.7 "significantly better than Grok 4.6 and should be ready in 3-4 weeks," built on a NEW 2.1tn-parameter foundation model; Grok 5 expected before year-end on a 6-10tn-parameter model.** ⚠️ **A three-model ladder inside ~5 months (1.5tn → 2.1tn → 6-10tn parameters) is an enormous training-compute commitment and is the clearest read-through on this page from the model roadmap to the [[NVDA]] order book.**
- **⚠️ THE USAGE PROBLEM IS EXPLICITLY UNRESOLVED, AND MS SAYS SO: *"what matters most is actual usage."*** On the 2Q call Musk said **Grok token volumes 3x'd shortly after Grok 4.5 was released and made default on Cursor**. But *"**OpenRouter (the industry standard), to our knowledge, does not capture usage through X and Cursor**, which represents the large majority of Grok usage."* ➜ **The single most-cited third-party usage tracker is structurally blind to where SpaceX's tokens actually flow. Treat OpenRouter-based Grok share charts as unusable for this name.** MS expects colour **after the Cursor acquisition closes "within the next couple weeks," with an in-depth update at earnings.**
- **🔴 THE VALUATION ARGUMENT, WHICH IS A SUM-OF-PARTS RESIDUAL AND SHOULD BE READ AS ONE: *"Backing out our DCF-implied Space + Connectivity estimate of **$127/share** from the current **~$140 stock price** would imply just **~1.5x 2028 EV/Sales on AI**, well below even neocloud peers with <7 GW of average compute in 2028."*** ⚠️ **The whole force of this depends on the $127 Space+Connectivity DCF being right; if that leg is over-valued the AI stub is not as cheap as 1.5x implies. Logged as an attributed framing, not adopted. It is, however, internally consistent with MS's own $300 PT: the note says SpaceX *"doesn't need to scale near the 10 GW by YE27 that Elon Musk suggests to have a significant rerating."***
- **🆕 THE "DISTRIBUTED INFERENCE CLOUD" — a net-new compute-architecture thread on this page, and it is speculative by construction.** MS's argument: Musk's "Terafab" ambition implies eventual access to **1 terawatt (1,000GW) of compute**, while **MS's own SpaceX model has SpaceX at 380GW in 2040** — the gap is closed by *"the networking of stationary and mobile 'nodes' at the edge… in a hybrid compute model."* The arithmetic: **MS's Global Robot Model has ~2.2bn robots of all form factors in 2040 × an assumed 500W of compute per robot = 1.1TW of aggregate compute**, *"all of which can theoretically be connected through Starlink."* The 500W assumption is anchored on **Tesla's AI5 chip for Optimus at 250W per Musk**, grown over time for larger form factors. ⚠️ **This is a 2040 thought experiment built on one modelled robot count times one assumed wattage. It is NOT a forecast, it does not appear in MS's PT, and it should not be netted against any GW figure on this page — the [`_meta/assumptions.md`](_meta/assumptions.md) rule against mixing GW bases applies with full force (facility GW ≠ IT-load GW ≠ hypothetical distributed-edge GW). Logged because it names a real strategic option Musk has discussed — idle vehicles and Megapods at Superchargers as a distributed compute network — and because it is the mechanism by which a [[TSLA]]–SpaceX story becomes a compute story.**
- **The other two of the note's four points are cross-references rather than new SPCX facts:** **[[CRWV]]'s 2Q beat on $/watt** (already carried on the CRWV page and in the lease-rate table above) and **Starship Flight 14** (already a dated catalyst below).

## 🆕 THE POWER BOTTLENECK GETS A NAME — "BLADES & VANES", AND A SECOND VERTICAL-INTEGRATION BET (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-08-16)
