**Morgan Stanley · Adam Jonas / William Tackett / Kristine Liwag / David Arcaro / Justin Lang / Jeremy Herring — "SpaceX: Blades & Vanes: SpaceX Tackles Time to Power" — OVERWEIGHT, PT $300 UNCHANGED, bull case $600 (2026-08-16, 15pp).** ⚠️ **The entire thesis is reverse-engineered from FOUR SPACEX JOB POSTINGS (Automation, Sr. Automation, Sr. Operations, Sr. Materials Engineer — "Blades and Vanes Foundry", Bastrop TX, posted back in April) plus an FTC early-termination notice. SpaceX has announced nothing and "has not commented directly on any of the aforementioned reports." Treat every GW number below as MS's reconstruction, not disclosure.**

- 🔴 **THE GAP THIS NOTE IS ACTUALLY ABOUT, and it is the page's standing 10GW question restated as a POWER question rather than a CHIP question: "After the exclusivity agreement with NVIDIA, we would think CHIP SUPPLY for SpaceX is MOSTLY SECURED, at least up to the 10 GW given by Elon Musk on the 2Q call. However, both LACK OF CLARITY IN ACCESS TO OFF-GRID POWER and aggressive time-to-deploy is the reason WE MODEL JUST 5 GW BY YE27 vs. the 10 GW suggested by Musk."** (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-08-16). **MS's estimates beyond 2027 are explicitly conditional on this bottleneck — "our SpaceXAI estimates beyond 2027 will depend on ability to tackle this critical bottleneck."**
- **THE TURBINE COUNT, dated and sourced: "we can identify 3-4 GW of LIKELY ACCESSIBLE gas turbine power BY YE27 through a mix of purchase agreements and Musk's personal acquisition of mobile-power provider APR Energy, with TOTAL REPORTED COMMITMENTS CLOSER TO 8 GW under any timeline, and likely more that has not been publicly disclosed."** Terrestrial compute modelled to peak at **~15 GW in 2031** (with upside if orbital is delayed). **Per the S-1: ~$2.8bn committed to AI-infrastructure turbines = an $805mn purchase agreement through 2029 (March 2026) + a ~$2.0bn mobile gas turbine "Turbine Acquisition"** — the latter "potentially corresponds to" Musk's personal purchase of **APR Energy** (FTC early-termination notice dated 2026-05-14 names him personally as buyer; APR fleet >1.1 GW mobile). Reported-but-unconfirmed suppliers: **Doosan Enerbility, Caterpillar Solar Turbines via Solaris (SEI), FTAI, APR Energy**.
- **THE REVENUE ANCHOR PER GW, and note the unit: "Each GW of SpaceXAI compute represents an opportunity of UPWARDS OF $50BN IN ANNUAL REVENUE"** (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-08-16). ⚠️ **This is REVENUE per GW, not capex per GW — do not net it against build-cost anchors elsewhere on this page or in [[themes/ai-datacenter-power]].**
- **WHY A FOUNDRY AT ALL — Musk's own words, from the February Dwarkesh Patel / John Collison "Cheeky Pint" interview, quoted in the note: "The turbines are sold out through 2030." … "In order to bring enough power online, I think SpaceX and Tesla will probably have to make the turbine blades, the vanes and blades, internally." … "The limiting factor is the vanes and blades. THERE ARE ONLY THREE CASTING COMPANIES IN THE WORLD that make these, and they're MASSIVELY BACKLOGGED."** MS's own framing is that this is **not principally about whether megawatts exist** — "beyond 2030, there is very possibly adequate turbine capacity" — but about **optionality over schedule, co-design of the airfoil with the machine, and capturing economics in a chain where "pricing has TRIPLED and OEMs are now recognizing MID-30% EBITDA MARGINS on new orders."**
- ⚠️ **THE TIME-TO-PRODUCTION CAVEAT IS THE BEAR SIDE OF THE SAME NOTE, and MS states it plainly: "IT MAY BE 2-3+ YEARS BEFORE THE FIRST VOLUME PRODUCTION FOR SPACEX."** Single-crystal casting is "widely described as the most technologically demanding casting process in the world" — a 40-50 step sequence, alloys surviving >3,000°F at 12,000-15,000 RPM, **capital equipment lead times of up to three years**, NADCAP/OEM part-number qualification measured in years. **Minimum greenfield-to-first-qualified-production is two years; full run-rate three or more.** Incumbents: **Precision Castparts (private), Consolidated Precision Products (private), Howmet (HWM), Doncasters (DPC)** — blades-and-vanes EBITDA margins ~15-20%+ (per DPC, the cleanest public pure-play).
- **Dual-use is the stated economic argument — "one foundry, two customers": the same nickel-superalloy single-crystal castings feed BOTH the datacenter gas turbines AND the Raptor turbopumps** (Raptor turbomachinery already cast from an Inconel-like custom "SX500" superalloy; **each V3 Starship needs 39 Raptor engines** — 33 booster + 6 ship — with SpaceX "likely to eventually require hundreds or even thousands a year"). MS expects **>50% of launches from 2029 to be orbital-compute related**.
- **🔴 THE SOTP RESIDUAL MOVED IN 3 DAYS AND THE PAGE SHOULD CARRY THE NEW MARK: "Backing out the value of Space + Connectivity ($127/share) from the current share price ($140) implies just $13 PER SHARE for Consumer + Enterprise AI, roughly 1x 2028 EV/SALES and BELOW EVEN NEOCLOUD PEERS"** (MS, 2026-08-16) — vs **~1.5x 2028 EV/Sales** on the same $127 space value at the same ~$140 price three days earlier (MS, 2026-08-13). Same house, same residual method; the multiple compressed. **Prior value moved to `## Changelog`.**
- **The trade framing is unchanged and explicitly lockup-anchored: "the LOCK-UP EXPIRY OVERHANG offers an attractive entry-point for a generational compounder converting energy into intelligence at scale."**
- **Would SpaceX sell blades to others? MS says effectively no: "we would NOT expect SpaceX to be a material supplier of blades & vanes for supply OUTSIDE OF SPACEX USE"** — i.e. this is a cost/schedule play, not a new revenue segment. Read-through to [[GEV]] / [[ETN]] / power-chain names is on the DEMAND side (SpaceX keeps buying turbines through the ramp), not on merchant-casting supply.

Wells.

Exact reason unclear, but likely REGULATORY in our view."** ➜ **Against management's own 2Q26 call guidance — *"assuming we receive regulatory approval to do so, we'll attempt to catch the ship with the tower on the next flight"* — this is the second-stage tower catch moving out by "a few months." A dated, checkable slip on the reusability milestone.**

Two independent Chinese recoveries in six weeks is a moat-erosion datapoint, even at a large capability gap.**

NOT ADOPTED pending a primary source — note also that a separate, unrelated "Cognition" is the venture firm [[CRWD]]'s departing CTO is launching, so the name collision is a trap.**
- **Also on the tape:** *"Fireworks and Baseten are the two largest inference clouds… Also first time seeing SpaceXAI on the list of 'fastest growing' IIRC"* (@GavinSBaker, 2026-08-19) ➜ **a third-party sighting of SpaceX's inference business in an independent growth ranking — relevant to the "Distributed Inference Cloud" topic above and to the 10GW-by-2027 claim this page tracks.** And, from the [[NVDA]] side: **"[[SPCX]] has committed to 'all-NVDA' architecture"** and is named as a Vera CPU customer (Jefferies · Curtis, 2026-08-20) ➜ **corroborates the all-NVDA deployment commitment from the supplier's analyst, independently of SpaceX's own disclosure.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 THE COMPUTE LEASES GET AN INDEPENDENT PER-MW BUILD — AND A NET-NEW HOUSE ARRIVES WITH THE HIGHEST BASE-CASE PT ON THIS PAGE (Arete Research, 2026-08-21)

**Arete Research, "NEO Clouds: Greater Pricing Power" (Andrew Beale / Alex Pound / Pierre-Marie d'Ornano / Frederik Strauss, 2026-08-21) — BUY, PT $450 (Dec '27E), UNCHANGED in this note** (the raises in it were on [[CRWV]] to $317 and [[NBIS]] to $415; SPCX and `IREN` were left alone, SPCX's model having been updated in Arete's earlier "SpaceX: Grabbing Terrestrial Compute", Aug '26). Ref price **$134 as of 19-Aug-26, mkt cap $1.8trn, implied upside +240%**. Methodology: **DCF at 13% WACC with a 20x FCF terminal multiple, plus net cash and digital assets**. Multiples on Arete's own numbers: **EV/Rev '28E 7.3x · EV/EBIT '28E 17.7x · EV/Rev '30E 3.2x · EV/EBIT '30E 7.5x.** ⚠️ **Where $450 sits on this page: it is the HIGHEST BASE-CASE PT the page carries — above MS's $300 (bull $600), DB $235, JPM $240, Bernstein $239, BofA $235, UBS $210, Citi $200 — and below only MS's bull scenario and the still-unconfirmed FinTwit-sourced RJ $800. It is a net-new house, so nothing on the page is superseded.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE DATAPOINT, AND IT IS THE FIRST BOTTOM-UP RECONSTRUCTION OF BOTH LEASES THIS PAGE HAS HELD — GPU COUNTS, MW, MONTHLY DOLLARS AND THE IMPLIED PRICE, FROM ONE SOURCE.** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT, STATED BEFORE THE NUMBERS AND BINDING: every `$m/MW` below is REVENUE per MW per annum — the compute SELLING PRICE. It is NOT capex, NOT build cost and NOT a $/GW anchor. Do not net it against any capex or build-cost figure on this page, and do not chain it into a capex series.**
  - **[[ANTHROPIC]] lease — 325,000 GPUs / 476 MW** (C1 H100 170,000 / 221MW · C1 H200 50,000 / 55MW · C1 GB200 30,000 / 57MW · C2 GB200 75,000 / 143MW), **monthly price $1.25bn ⇒ annual $15.0bn ⇒ $31m/MW p.a.** Arete's characterisation: **largely LAST-GENERATION, Hopper-based INFERENCE capacity.**
  - **[[GOOG]] lease — C2 Southaven GB300 110,000 GPUs / 200 MW**, **monthly price $0.92bn ⇒ annual $11.0bn ⇒ $55m/MW p.a.**
  - **Fleet map behind those leases: COLOSSUS 250,000 GPUs / 333MW · COLOSSUS II 330,000 / 640MW · COLOSSUS II Southaven 670,000 / 1,218MW ⇒ 1,250,000 GPUs / 2,191 MW across all COLOSSUS. Residual (unleased) capacity 497 MW today, rising to 1,515 MW once Southaven is complete.**
  - ✅ **INDEPENDENT SECOND HOUSE ON THE SAME TWO PRICES, AND THEY AGREE CLOSELY: JPM's initiation (2026-07-07) had Anthropic at a blended ~$29/W and Google at ~$50/W for GB300; Arete has $31m/MW and $55m/MW. Two houses, six weeks apart, within ~7-10% on both legs — this is now the best-corroborated pricing pair on the page.**
  - ⚠️ **DO NOT CONVERT AGAINST THE $7.80 / $11.50 PER-GPU-HOUR CHART ABOVE — and note what the comparison does and does not say.** Both sources agree on DIRECTION (Google is the better-priced contract, Anthropic the low end of realised pricing). They disagree on MAGNITUDE: Arete's spread is **$55m vs $31m = +77%**, the 13D/Exponential View chart's is **$11.50 vs $7.80 = +47%**. **That gap is exactly what the different bases predict — GB300 packs far fewer GPUs per MW than Hopper, so a per-GPU-hour spread and a per-MW spread cannot be the same number.** ➜ **Both marks stand, separately attributed, UNCONVERTED, per the `_meta/assumptions.md` rule against mixing per-chip and per-watt bases. The unreconciled +47% vs +77% is logged as an open item, not resolved here.**
  - ⚠️🔴 **TWO BASIS COLLISIONS THIS SOURCE CREATES, BOTH FLAGGED RATHER THAN RESOLVED.** (1) **The page's `## AI compute` section carries "~325,000 NVIDIA GPUs across COLOSSUS / COLOSSUS II" from the 424B4 — and Arete's 325,000 figure is the ANTHROPIC LEASE ALONE, inside a total fleet it maps at 1,250,000 GPUs / 2,191MW.** The numbers are identical and the bases are not; the prospectus figure now looks stale rather than wrong (it pre-dates the Colossus II expansion), but do not treat the two 325,000s as the same quantity. (2) **JPM (Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-06-30) reported the SAME $1.25bn/month Anthropic deal as covering "220,000+ NVIDIA GPUs (H100, H200, GB200)"; Arete builds it at 325,000 GPUs. Same price, same chip families, ~48% more silicon.** ➜ **One of the two GPU counts is wrong, or they are dated differently (a C2 GB200 tranche of 75,000 would roughly bridge the gap). The PRICE is agreed; the DENOMINATOR is not — which matters, because any per-GPU-hour figure derived from this contract inherits the disagreement.**
- 🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL PRICING ARGUMENT, AND IT IS THE CLEANEST STATEMENT OF SPCX'S EDGE OVER THE LISTED NEOs ANYONE HAS PUT ON THIS PAGE: "Because SpaceX is new to the compute leasing game, ALL compute that it can lease is at higher prices, and the majority of that is so far at short-term (up to $55m/MW) rates rather than the 'new normal' of $20–25m/MW for longer-duration deals."** SpaceX is pricing short-term compute at **2–4x the historic 5-year NEO take-or-pay rate of $10–15m/MW**, while [[CRWV]] and [[NBIS]] are achieving **$20–25m/MW** on 2–3-year enterprise/AI-native business ⚠️ (**revenue per MW p.a. throughout**). ➜ **The mechanism is a mix advantage, not a quality advantage: SpaceX has no legacy book of cheap five-year hyperscaler contracts to average down its realised price. That is a genuine, durable-for-now bull point — and its exact flip side is the duration risk below.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE 90-DAY CANCELLATION TERM IS NOW CORROBORATED BY A RESEARCH HOUSE, WHICH UPGRADES IT OUT OF PODCAST-HEARSAY: "These deals are not five-year take-or-pay contracts but shorter-term deals with 90-day EACH WAY cancellation rights."** Arete carries it as a formal investment risk: *"[[ANTHROPIC]] or [[GOOG]] GPU contracts get cancelled under 90-day clause."* ➜ **This page has held that term since 08-18 on David Sacks's All-In relay ("spot minus 90 days") with an explicit caveat that it was not a filing or company disclosure. It is still not a company disclosure — but two independent sources now state it, and the second is a research house that has built a model around it. The caveat softens; it does not disappear.** ⚠️ **Direct consequence, and it is the qualification on everything above: the $31m/$55m per-MW prices are ROLLING, not contracted for five years. Premium pricing and 90-day duration are the same fact viewed twice.**
- **Payback, and the unit trap inside it, spelled out because it is exactly the confusion this page has been careful about:** Arete says **SpaceX states a ONE-YEAR PAYBACK on AI capex at its premium pricing**, and finds that "realistic to us **given that their revenue yield/MW on short-term compute is approximately the same as their capex/MW**." ⚠️ **Read that sentence precisely: Arete is asserting an APPROXIMATE EQUALITY between two separately-measured quantities as the REASON payback is ~1 year. It does NOT make $31m/MW or $55m/MW capex figures, and it must not be used to back out a capex/MW number for this page.** For calibration, the peers Arete gives: **[[NBIS]] at 1 year 10 months on its latest 2Q deals (from 2–3 years previously), and neither NBIS nor [[CRWV]] "yet comes close to SpaceX's claim of one-year payback."**
- ✅ **CORROBORATION OF THE TURBINE / SITE THREAD, FROM THE COMPANION NOTE: in "SpaceX: Grabbing Terrestrial Compute" (Aug. '26) Arete "materially increased" its SpaceX AI-infrastructure estimates on the assumption that SpaceX uses GAS TURBINES (fixed AND mobile fleets) to power a series of new sites or expansions — "notably near MEMPHIS, BASTROP and CONROE, but potentially acquiring additional sites in Texas given the Batch 0 pause."** Arete's view: **"We see SpaceX moving faster than others to light gas-turbine-powered sites and to try to capture as much of the premium-priced, short-term contract compute opportunity it can."** ➜ **Independent support for the "Blades & Vanes" power-bottleneck framing (MS, 08-16) and for the ERCOT Batch 0 pause as a live constraint, from a house that treats turbine speed as the competitive weapon rather than the risk.** ⚠️ **Bastrop and Conroe are net-new site names on this page; they are ARETE'S ASSUMPTION for its model, not a SpaceX disclosure.**
- **Supply, demand and affordability, as the frame under the $450:** compute supply is unlikely to catch up soon — gated by **wafer and power constraints**, with **US states (Pennsylvania, Texas and New York named) slowing data-center permitting** — so **"any AI cloud player not completely sold out can sell compute at very attractive prices"**, and Arete expects tightness **at least through '28E**. Demand marker: **Node.js SDK downloads up 9.2x y/y in July** (Arete Software-team GenAI tracker), with only **17% open-source take-up in its CIO surveys**. Affordability: **frontier-lab revenue yield ~$50m per inference MW** ⚠️ (**also a revenue/MW figure but on a DIFFERENT denominator — the LAB's revenue over the LAB's inference MW, not the price SpaceX charges. It is a coverage ratio, never a comparable to the $31m/$55m lease prices**), and Arete concedes the SpaceX levels are "perhaps slightly harder to justify against current token economics… simply a reflection of the shortage of compute."
- **M&A tail worth logging: Arete floats SpaceX as one of the plausible all-stock acquirers of `IREN`'s ~6GW gross power portfolio (US, Australia, Spain), alongside [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]], a hyperscaler or a frontier lab** — "given their relatively pedestrian execution on capacity delivery." (`IREN` has no wiki page — plain text by convention.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260821_Arete_Research_NEO_Clouds-_Greater_Pricing_Power.html)


**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — UBS IS NOW TRACKING TERAFAB WITH EVIDENCE-LAB JOB-POSTING AND SATELLITE DATA, AND THE TWO SIGNALS DISAGREE IN A USEFUL WAY: THE HIRING SAYS "COMPREHENSIVE FAB, INCLUDING MEMORY", THE SATELLITE IMAGERY SAYS "BARELY STARTED".** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, "SemiBytes Weekly: … Terafab Update", 2026-08-24)
- **Hiring, measured:** **Terafab-related job postings on Tesla's career portal reached 44 in July**, *"the vast majority focused on hiring senior engineers with high-volume manufacturing/fab operations competence"*, and — importantly — the postings **span essentially all major semiconductor process steps: litho, deposition, dry etch, epitaxy, etc.** UBS's inference: *"SpaceX may be building out a fairly comprehensive engineering organization rather than selectively hiring around only a handful of modules."*
- 🔴 **The net-new strategic fact: the hiring includes Memory Design Engineers and, more recently, Memory Integration Engineers.** UBS: *"While this confirms Terafab's willingness to kick-start its internal memory manufacturing efforts… a lot of questions remain unanswered here — most notably, we are unclear where Terafab would source memory IP and see limited incentives for existing memory suppliers to license their IP."* **That IP-sourcing question is the cleanest bear point on Terafab-memory anyone has published.** Filed also to themes/hbm-memory.
- **A tell on where the talent is expected to come from:** UBS found **duplicate versions of several job postings in both Austin, TX and Hsinchu City, Taiwan** — *"we suspect SpaceX would want to tap into [[TSM]]'s talent pool, though it is less clear to us how Taiwan-based hires would directly support the physical fab ramp in TX in the next few years."*
- **Construction, and this is the sober half:** since the **official 6-August announcement**, *"a significant uptick in the number of Terafab job posting related to cleanroom construction — which now represents the largest category of Terafab-related open positions."* But: 🔴 **"our initial satellite imagery of the rather large Grimes County campus expected to host Terafab suggests the infrastructure buildout remains at a VERY EARLY STAGE, with only limited site-clearing/deforestation work visible so far."**
- **Why this matters beyond SPCX:** GS's WFE raise the day before explicitly names **"Logic/Other (medium term, including Terafab)"** as a driver of its **$281bn 2028 WFE** number (GS · Schneider, 2026-08-23). **Terafab is now inside a bulge-bracket WFE forecast while its site is still being cleared** — that timing mismatch is the risk to the 2028 leg of the semicap call. Filed to themes/semicap-wfe.

**🆕⚠️ 2026-08-21 — BERNSTEIN'S DIRECT-TO-DEVICE VERDICT IS THE FIRST PROPERLY ENGINEERED PUSHBACK ON THE MOBILE STORY, AND IT LANDS ON "MVNO, NOT CARRIER".** (Bernstein Research, relayed in Bernstein Tech by Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-21)
- **What Bernstein concedes:** the **V2 constellation is "a notable development" — 4x the receiver size, higher beam density, lower altitude.**
- **What it does not:** *"engineering problems are unlikely to be solved (i.e. Uplink, batter[y] and antennas) given the primary challenge related to the distance between phone and the satellite."* Battery life is flagged specifically, *"with satellite usually requiring the phone model to operate at a higher power level, which could hinder the user experience."*
- 🔴 **The conclusion, quoted: "While D2D can provide valuable supplemental coverage in remote areas, we believe it is unlikely to deliver the level of performance, reliability, and indoor coverage consumers expect from a primary wireless provider in its current form… a partnership (i.e., MVNO) appears to be the most likely approach."** Against this: **management expects the mobile service to launch by end-2027.**
- ⚠️ **The buy-side colour attached is unusually blunt for a relayed daily: *"'smoke and mirrors' comments arise in some of my meetings on the near term trajectory for both Starlink AND the orbital data centers ramp… most I speak with have a tough time articulating a path for direct to cell feasibility by the end of next year."* Balanced by: *"it's hard to bet against Elon right now given his track record and the overhang will unlikely change in 2H or 2027."* Read-through: **negative for Towers broadly; feedback on CHTR/CMCSA "remains negative given broadband adoption from Starlink."**
- **Cross-reference on the compute side, from a sales note the same week:** Elon's own gigawatt framing on the 07-Aug call — *"lets say closer to 10 gigawatts of compute than 5"* in 2027 — is *"still being treated as a 'show me' part of the story"*, and **SPCX now appears inside sell-side cloud-capex aggregates alongside AMZN/GOOGL/META/MSFT/ORCL, with SPCX among the biggest upward revisions of the past month** (BofA sales · Fenske/Franco, 2026-08-24). Filed to themes/hyperscaler-capex.

## Debate / thesis
**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 — SPACEX IS THE NAMED SOURCE OF THE $26.5 TRILLION AI TAM THAT ANCHORS JEFFERIES' ENTIRE AI DECK, AND ITS CFO SUPPLIES THE STRONGEST PAYBACK NUMBER IN THE COMPLEX.** *(Jefferies · **Brent Thill** (w/ John Byun, Mohit Gogia, Bo Yin, Maximilian Joseph, Ria Naidu, ShengQi Lin, Matt Molta) — "AI Deep Dive: Addressing Key Debates Across Hyperscalers and Software", 58pp chart deck, priced intraday 9-2-26. ⚠️ **NO SPCX RATING, PT OR ESTIMATE IN THIS DECK — SPCX is Adam Jonas's coverage at Morgan Stanley; nothing on this page is superseded.** ⚠️ **PAGE-STRUCTURE NOTE, RECORDED AGAIN RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: this page still has NO `## Intra-quarter — calls, commentary & reports` section, so the mandatory full-log ROW and the `Signal vs management` re-scoring could not be performed for this source either. Nothing was hand-scaffolded. Filed here as a dated block per this page's actual convention — the fourth consecutive run to hit this.)*
- 🔴🔴 **THE ITEM THAT IS ACTUALLY NOTEWORTHY IS THE ATTRIBUTION, NOT THE NUMBER: the deck's headline TAM slide (p3) is titled, in Jefferies' own words, *"**SpaceX Estimated Global AI TAM By Segment**"* — i.e. the $26.5 trillion figure on which a 58-page sell-side deck is built is carried as a **SPACEX-SOURCED ESTIMATE**, not a Jefferies one.** ➤ **A private-company-turned-recent-IPO supplying the market-sizing anchor for a major bank's AI thematic is a positioning datapoint about this name in its own right: SpaceX is no longer only being valued inside the AI complex (as MS Global Credit did on 08-20 when it folded SPCX into its ~$1.4trn hyperscaler capex aggregate) — it is now supplying the complex's framing numbers. That is agenda-setting influence, and it is the second independent instance in two weeks of an outside desk treating SpaceX as a primary AI-infrastructure reference rather than a space/telecom name with an AI segment.**
- **THE DECOMPOSITION, ALL FIVE FIGURES VERIFIED OFF THE RENDERED SLIDE (p3): **Total AI Addressable Market $26.5T**, of which **Enterprise Applications $22.7T**, **AI Infrastructure $2.4T**, **Consumer Subscriptions $760bn** and **Digital Advertising $600bn** — footing to ~$26.5T — and flagged as **"~21% of Current Global GDP"**.** ⚠️ **Read the shape before the size: **86% of the TAM sits in Enterprise Applications**, and AI Infrastructure — the segment SpaceX actually competes in — is **$2.4T, roughly 9%**. So the number that gets quoted ($26.5T) is overwhelmingly a bet on application-layer value creation, not on the compute build. Anyone using this slide to size the neocloud opportunity on this page must use the $2.4T infrastructure leg, not the headline.** ⚠️ **This is an unmodelled, undated, methodology-free TAM estimate reproduced from a source with an obvious interest in its size. It is logged as a POSITIONING and ATTRIBUTION datapoint, not as an addressable-market input, and it is NOT to be chained with the GW, $/W or lease-rate anchors already on this page — different bases entirely.** ⚠️ **Extraction note: pdfplumber pairs these bar values with the WRONG labels on p3/p4; every figure above was read from the rendered page image instead.**
- 🔴 **AND THE HARD NUMBER — A MANAGEMENT PAYBACK MARK, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST IN THE ENTIRE DECK: *"Specifically **on the AI compute side**, we’re able to deploy capital in such a way that **we’re getting less than a one-year payback**"* — **Bret W. Johnsen, SpaceX CFO, 2Q26 Earnings Call** (p40, *"…And Improving Cash Payback Periods"*).** ➤ **Jefferies uses it as the STRONG end of a two-point AI-payback spectrum whose weak end is [[AMZN]]'s 2Q26 disclosure that *"for servers and networking equipment, on average it takes a little less than 3 years to breakeven"* on capacity *"contracted for at least 5-year terms."* ➤ **A sub-one-year payback against Amazon's just-under-three is the single most favourable unit-economics comparison this page carries, and it comes from management on the record rather than from a broker model.** ⚠️⚠️ **BUT THE TWO ARE NOT LIKE-FOR-LIKE AND MUST NOT BE PRESENTED AS A RANKING: Johnsen scopes his to "the AI compute side" and gives no asset base, no contract tenor and no definition of payback; Amazon's is explicitly servers-and-networking-equipment against 5-year contracted terms. Different perimeters, different denominators, and the shorter payback may partly reflect a different (and smaller) capital base rather than better economics. Carried as management's own claim, unverified, and NOT reconciled against the Arete per-MW build or the S-1/Google lease rates already on this page — which are revenue and cost measures, not payback measures.** ⚠️ **No corroboration for this figure exists elsewhere on the page; SPCX has one print of history.** _(Jefferies "AI Deep Dive: Addressing Key Debates Across Hyperscalers and Software" · Brent Thill, 2026-09-02)_ [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AI_Deep_Dive_Jeffries.html)
**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-24/25 — MORGAN STANLEY'S OWN ASIA DESK PUTS THE 10GW AMBITION AGAINST TSMC'S PHYSICAL OUTPUT, AND MS'S OWN SPCX ANALYST UNDERWRITES ONLY HALF OF IT.** (Morgan Stanley Asia-Pacific Technology · Charlie Chan / Daniel Yen / Daisy Dai / Tiffany Yeh, "AI Supply Chain: Google ASIC, who gets what for design services?", **2026-08-24**.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GLOBAL_20260824_1435.html)
- 🔴 **THE ARITHMETIC, QUOTED IN FULL BECAUSE IT IS THE FIRST TIME ANYONE HAS SIZED THIS PAGE'S HEADLINE NUMBER AGAINST A FOUNDRY CONSTRAINT: *"when SpaceX (covered by Adam Jonas) indicated plans to expand its computing capacity from 2GW by the end of this year to nearly 10GW under an ambitious scenario … investors also asked what this would imply for TSMC's CoWoS capacity consumption. Notably, Adam models only 3GW of incremental computing demand, reaching 5GW by end-2027. As a rough illustration, the additional 8GW implied by SpaceX's target would equate to approximately 4mn Rubin GPUs, or roughly 20% of the AI accelerators that TSMC could produce in 2027."*** ➜ **Two things follow, and both belong in the bear column of this page. ① MS's OWN SPCX analyst models 5GW by end-2027 — HALF the ambitious scenario management has put in the market. That is a house disagreeing with its own covered company's stretch case, in print, and it is the cleanest external mark on the 10GW claim this page has. ② Taken at face value the incremental 8GW would absorb roughly a fifth of TSMC's entire 2027 AI-accelerator output. The gate is silicon and packaging allocation, not ambition — which is consistent with the "15-20GW of power/cooling deliberately overbuilt ahead of the silicon" framing already on this page from the 08-04 primary transcript.**
- **The industry context MS Asia builds it on, useful as an independent denominator: its 2027 CoWoS-implied chip volume is ~19mn units, and *"assuming an average TDP of 2kW per GPU/ASIC, the installed base would require roughly 38GW of power capacity"* globally. Against that, a 10GW single-buyer target is ~26% of the world's 2027 accelerator power.** ⚠️ **These are MS Asia estimates from Taiwan supply-chain checks, not SpaceX disclosure, and the ~4mn-Rubin conversion is explicitly labelled *"a rough illustration."***
- ⚠️ **DO NOT CHAIN THESE FIGURES INTO A CAPEX OR BUILD-COST SERIES. This is a units-and-power calculation (GPUs × TDP ⇒ GW). It carries no $/W and must not be combined with the $/GW or $M/MW marks elsewhere on this page.**
**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — SPACEX NOW APPEARS AS A NAMED LEADING-EDGE LOGIC FAB IN MORGAN STANLEY'S WFE MODEL — AT A DELIBERATELY IMMATERIAL NUMBER.** (Morgan Stanley Semiconductor Capital Equipment · Shane Brett / Lee Simpson / Nigel van Putten / Charlie Chan / Suzune Tamura, "3Q'26 WFE update: 2026/27 Bull Case is the Base Case", **2026-08-25**; industry view In-Line.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SEMICONDUCTOR_20260825_0401.html)
- 🔴 **SpaceX is one of only FIVE names in MS's leading-edge logic wafer model — *"TSMC, Samsung, Intel, Rapidus, SpaceX"* — appearing in the 3nm, 2nm and 1.4nm capacity-add exhibits alongside the incumbent foundries. That is a structural promotion: the semicap desk now models SpaceX as a fab operator, not only as a compute buyer.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **BUT THE NUMBER IS THE OPPOSITE OF AN ENDORSEMENT, AND IT MUST TRAVEL WITH THE HEADLINE: on 2028 leading-logic WFE of $90bn (+17% y/y), MS writes *"We don't reflect a meaningful contribution from SpaceX ($2bn)."* Two billion dollars against a $90bn leading-logic market — SpaceX is IN the model and explicitly discounted WITHIN it.** ➜ **The honest read is that MS's tool analysts treat Terafab as real enough to name and small enough to ignore through 2028. That is a useful calibration for this page against the $55bn Grimes County filing and the Terafab headlines: presence in the model is not the same as volume in the model, and 2028 is the first year MS gives it any number at all.**
- **For scale on the same page: MS raises 2026/27/28 WFE to $163bn (+39%) / $223bn (+37%) / $254bn (+14%), with leading-logic at $51bn / $76bn / $90bn, TSMC at $43.3bn and [[INTC]] at $20.5bn of 2027 leading-logic spend.**
**🗄️ ARCHIVE BACKFILL — DAMNANG (Substack), 2026-05-16 and 2026-05-22. ⚠️ THE INGEST ROUTER MISSED THIS PAGE ENTIRELY ON THE 05-16 PIECE, WHICH IS A SPACEX-PRIMARY ARTICLE.** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT KOREAN SEMIS SUBSTACK (damnang2.substack.com; also publishes as "The Optical Edge"), NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no formal estimate.** Launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, paid; the author discloses he may hold positions. **Three months old, written PRE-IPO and PRE-S-1; filed as historical context ONLY. Every figure below is superseded by the 424B4, the maiden print and the post-print research already on this page — it is retained to date the pre-IPO expectation set, not as current data.**
- **🗄️ THE PRE-IPO SUPPLY-CHAIN MAP, AND ITS VALUE IS THE DISCIPLINE RATHER THAN THE NAMES** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "The Real SpaceX IPO Beneficiaries Are in Semiconductors: A Supply Chain Anatomy - 8 Layers, 4 Baskets, 1 Map", **2026-05-16**; ⚠️ independent author, NOT a broker). **Pre-IPO expectation set as printed, all now superseded by the 424B4: *"Target valuation: $1.75T. Capital raise target: $75B"*, with Reuters-sourced timing of *"roadshow … June 4, pricing for June 11, and the first day of Nasdaq trading for June 12"* under ticker SPCX.** 🔴 **THE OPERATING DATAPOINT WORTH KEEPING, because it is a production rate rather than a valuation: *"SpaceX is producing over 20,000 Starlink terminals per day"* (per the STMicroelectronics/SpaceX partnership announcement), with V3 satellites at *"roughly 1 Tbps of capacity"* each and Starlink Mobile announced at MWC in March 2026.** **His terminal BOM reverse-engineering, explicitly an estimate: total semiconductor content of *"$130-165"* per terminal against a ~$299 retail price — *"roughly 40-55% of the retail price"* — implying annual semiconductor spend of *"$1.3-1.8B"* including satellite and ground chips.** **He grades the eight supply layers by evidence, and the grading is the useful part: only STMicroelectronics (RF front-end; *"more than 5 billion RF antenna chips"* shipped, LEO revenue ~$600mn in 2025 rising toward ~$1bn in 2026 against a *"cumulative target of over $3B for 2026 through 2028"*) and Filtronic (E-band ground-station SSPAs; a £47.3M/$62.5M contract in August 2025, with SpaceX holding warrants for up to 10% of its equity) are **confirmed and materially levered**. [[AMD]] is **confirmed but immaterial** — Lisa Su named SpaceX on the Q3 2024 call, supplying Versal AI Core and rad-hard Kintex parts, but *"the space segment is a tiny fraction of AMD's total revenue. Buying AMD on the basis of SpaceX upside is a weak thesis."* [[QCOM]] is **pure optionality** — *"No current revenue linkage. Ramp begins 2027-2028"* on the X105 NR-NTN modem, with MediaTek *"not entirely ruled out."* [[INTC]] is a **vertical-integration partner, not a component supplier** — contributing *"14A process node, EMIB, and Foveros 3D packaging"* to Terafab.**
- **🗄️ THE VERTICAL-INTEGRATION TIMELINE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT HAS AGED BEST AND CUTS AGAINST THE MS WFE NUMBER ABOVE** (same piece). **Bastrop: a *"$280M expansion announced in 2025"* for FOPLP chip packaging, *"target production start is the end of 2026."* Terafab: announced March 2026 as a Tesla/SpaceX/xAI joint fab at *"$20-25B"*, with Reuters reporting in May 2026 that *"SpaceX filed for an initial $55B semiconductor fab investment in Grimes County, Texas"*, targeting AI5 (Tesla inference) and D3 (*"SpaceX radiation-hardened AI processor for satellites"*).** 🔴 **His timing estimate is the item to hold: *"pilot production at 2028-2029 at the earliest, with full-scale production in the 2031-2032 timeframe"*, and he sizes SpaceX's full insourcing gradient (PCB → packaging → chip design → fab) as *"a 10-15 year process."*** ➜ **That independently corroborates Morgan Stanley's semicap desk giving SpaceX only $2bn of leading-logic WFE in 2028 and nothing meaningful before it. Two sources, three months apart, using different methods, both say Terafab is a next-decade capacity event — which is a useful counterweight to the headline capex numbers.**
- **🗄️ One market-structure note, filed because it is a testable prediction about this page's own listing** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "A Mid-Point Review of the Stocks I've Covered", **2026-05-22**): *"The one thing to watch is the large IPOs still coming this year. SpaceX, OpenAI, and Anthropic hitting the public market are the biggest variables. If companies adding up to more than a trillion dollars in value hit the market at once, capital could rotate toward them, and existing semiconductor names could see temporary outflows."* ⚠️ **An independent author's flow hypothesis from May, not a datapoint. Recorded because the rotation question it raises is live again around this page's unlock calendar.**
- **🆕🔴 2026-08-18 — THE CURSOR ACQUISITION CLOSES THE LOOP FROM COMPUTE → MODEL → APPLICATION, and it explains the Grok capability jump (DIGITIMES headline + All-In Ep. #285).**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — THE ORBITAL DATA CENTER STOPS BEING A CONCEPT AND GETS HARDWARE, A PARTNER AND TWO DATES — FROM MUSK DIRECTLY.**
- 🔴 **@elonmusk (2026-08-24, via @dnystedt): _"**SpaceX, in partnership with Nvidia, has designed a SPACE-OPTIMIZED VERA RUBIN NVL72 system for launch to orbit in Q4 next year**"_** — i.e. **Q4 2027**.
- 🔴 **Corroborated with a second date by Bloomberg the same day: _"Musk said the first AI data center for space will launch in Q4:27 and hit 'SIGNIFICANT SCALE' in 2028"_** (via Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli, "Market in a Minute", 2026-08-24). ➜ **Two independent statements of the same Q4:27 launch date, one of them naming the exact system (a space-optimised NVL72) and the partner. This page previously carried the orbital ambition without hardware or a date; it now has both.**
- 🔴 **AND THE TERRESTRIAL SIDE IS ALREADY A NAMED, SHIPPING NVDA CUSTOMER — FOR TWO PRODUCT LINES, INCLUDING ONE THAT IS NOT A GPU.** *"**@SpaceXAI is deploying NVIDIA Vera to power agentic AI — faster agents, GPUs fully utilized, and a single architecture FROM EARTH TO ORBIT**"* (@nvidianewsroom, 2026-08-24), and **SpaceX and [[NBIS]] are named as early customers of BOTH the Vera CPU and the Groq LPX racks** (The Information, 2026-08-24, citing NVDA's Monday announcement). **NVDA's own release: *"SpaceXAI Adopts NVIDIA Vera CPU to Accelerate Agentic AI at Massive Scale."*** ➜ **Consistent with, and an extension of, the 08-04 primary-transcript statement already on this page (*"we're EXCLUSIVE TO NVIDIA"*, *"we will receive a VERY SIGNIFICANT PERCENTAGE OF THEIR GPUs NEXT YEAR"*) — the exclusivity now demonstrably spans CPU and inference-rack SKUs, not only GPUs.**
- 🔴 **THE STAKE IS MARKED, AND IT IS LARGE: NVDA's public-equity portfolio rose from $18.4bn (2026-03-31) to $63.0bn (2026-06-30), and The Information attributes part of the step-up to "the FIRST-TIME INCLUSION of Nvidia's $21 BILLION STAKE IN SPACEX"** (Martin Peers, "The Briefing", 2026-08-24, citing NVDA securities filings). ➜ **A $21bn cross-holding in the supplier's own filings is a harder disclosure than any relayed deal term on this page.**
- **Two softer items from the same day, logged with their evidence class attached.** *"Musk during a Cursor employee meeting vowed to **stockpile more compute capacity than any company in the AI race**"* (The Information, via Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-24) — ⚠️ *reported remarks at an internal meeting; rhetoric, not a commitment.* And **MS's [[GOOG]] note names "the SPCX compute deal" as an explicit MARGIN HEADWIND to Google Cloud** alongside TPU mix (Brian Nowak, 2026-08-24) — 🔴 **which is the first time a third party has priced the SPCX–Google arrangement as dilutive to the counterparty. That is corroborating evidence for the low lease-rate reading already on this page (the 08-06 chart showing rates 2-8x below what the same GPU earns serving an open model).**
- ⚠️ **Competitive marker, not a threat assessment: The Information's TITV interviewed the CEO of STARCLOUD "about the company's progress on its orbital data center ambitions" the same day (2026-08-24). At least one other funded entrant is public about the same market.**
  - **DIGITIMES (2026-08-17): "SpaceX-Cursor acquisition closes the loop between AI compute, models and applications."** ⚠️ **Headline only — the body was NOT read this run, and no consideration, structure or close date was captured.**
  - 🔴 **Independent corroboration of the *effect* from the All-In discussion (2026-08-15): Gavin Baker attributes xAI's catch-up to "the Cursor team plus a management reset that brought SpaceX operators into xAI", and says Grok 4.6 "screens at or near the frontier at lower cost in Cursor and Databricks/Mercor evaluations", with a larger ~1.5T-parameter Grok 4.7 expected within weeks.** ⚠️ **Baker himself flags the conflict: "grading your own homework" risk in Cursor-linked benchmarks, since the acquirer now owns the benchmark surface. Forward product claims, not disclosures.**
  - **Why it matters for this page's valuation debate, in Baker's framing: investors debate Starlink, terrestrial compute and orbital compute but "often assign little explicit value to Grok, despite [[ANTHROPIC]] demonstrating how quickly a frontier lab can scale."** ⚠️ **All-In participants disclose they are early SpaceX investors and are talking their book.**
- **🆕🔴 2026-08-18 — THE 10GW 2027 TARGET GETS A SITE-LEVEL ENDORSEMENT FROM SEMIANALYSIS.** *"There are a lot of haters of @elonmusk, but we believe that, **due to Elon's first-principles engineering, SpaceX has likely found a handful of sites to deploy the remaining GW, and the 10GW 2027 target is within reach**."* (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-08-18, 2,204 likes / 239k views.) 🔴 **This is the first third-party research house to publicly back the 10GW figure on a SITE-availability basis rather than a demand basis** — the standing objection on this page has been power and land, not orders. ⚠️ **A tweet, not a report; no sites named, no MW per site, no interconnect dates. It is an assertion of belief, and the page's 10GW row should carry it as corroboration of direction only.**
- **🆕 2026-08-18 — THE COMPUTE ARRANGEMENT'S CANCELLATION TERMS, and the "spot minus 90 days" framing (All-In Ep. #285, David Sacks).** Sacks frames SpaceX/xAI compute as a two-sided option: *"if Grok wins, route capacity internally; if it trails, rent compute to frontier labs."* 🔴 **He says BOTH SIDES of the [[ANTHROPIC]] arrangement have a 90-DAY CANCELLATION RIGHT, leaving SpaceX close to "spot minus 90 days."** ⚠️ **A reported deal term relayed by a podcast participant who is an early SpaceX investor — NOT a filing or company disclosure.** **Why it matters: it converts the largest named offtake on this page from contracted revenue into rolling spot exposure, which is the single most important qualification to the offtaker-quality argument the 08-07 bottom-up defence rests on.**
- **🆕⚠️ 2026-08-18 — AMD STILL HOLDS US$565M OF SPACEX SHARES EVEN THOUGH SPACEX WENT NVIDIA-EXCLUSIVE (DIGITIMES headline).** *"Elon Musk has made SpaceX's AI hardware strategy unusually clear: future infrastructure will rely **exclusively on [[NVDA]] chips and technology**. Yet [[AMD]], Nvidia's largest rival in AI accelerators, still…"* ⚠️ **Headline + lede only, body NOT read.** **Two things to carry: (1) an explicit, on-the-record NVDA exclusivity for future SpaceX AI infrastructure — relevant to the accelerator-choice risk this page carries; (2) a $565m related-party equity position at a direct competitor of the sole supplier, which is a governance/disclosure item to look for in the next filing.** **Adjacent, same day: "SpaceX, Nvidia push space AI from lunar exploration toward orbital data centers" and "SpaceX sets Falcon 9 turnaround record with two launches 38 minutes apart" (DIGITIMES headlines, 2026-08-18).**

**🆕 2026-08-17 (INGEST) — A SECOND HOUSE PUTS A NUMBER ON THE MOBILE LEG, AND THE NEAR-TERM SETUP HAS A DATE ON IT (LOCKUP 08-20).**

**(1) UBS · John Hodulik — Buy, PT $210 (SOTP, ~30x total-company EBITDA on 2028E) — "Thoughts on Starlink Mobile's coverage plan" (2026-08-17).** UBS frames the equity as trading "on demand for tokens given the leverage in the model as a major cloud provider and frontier model," but the note itself is about the part of the story this page has least evidence on: **how Starlink Mobile actually covers dense urban areas.** SpaceX said on the 2Q call it would use **femtocells built into its next-generation Starlink terminals**. UBS's two gating variables: **(a) low-band spectrum** — scarce, with the plausible sources being T-Mobile's 800 MHz sold to Grain (13-14 MHz), EchoStar's 700 MHz E Block, NextNav (~15 MHz petitioned) and Anterix (~6-10 MHz), plus UHF broadcast (470-608 MHz) as a multi-year long shot; and **(b) density** — a femtocell covers only a **10-50 metre radius**, and UBS models **3mn US Starlink subs today → 6mn by YE27 → 20mn by YE31**. 🔴 **The bear line UBS will not walk away from: "using this strategy to replicate coverage of the 3 MNOs in urban centers is going to be difficult given the large number of multi-dwelling units — roughly 30% of Americans live in MDUs, making this portion of the market difficult to reach,"** and prior non-traditional wireless attempts (Sprint's Magic Box, Altice strand-mounted small cells) mostly failed. UBS still believes **an MVNO with an incumbent carrier is SpaceX's preferred US path**, with a hybrid satellite + ground build (including towers) as the fallback. ⚠️ **Note the PT spread this opens on the page: UBS $210 vs MS $300 (bull $600) — the two houses are ~43% apart and are underwriting different segments (UBS connectivity-led SOTP, MS compute-led).**

**(2) The 3-4 week tactical setup, with levels (TMT Breakout, 2026-08-16/17).** SPCX **+4.5% on 08-17**; the desk flags catalysts as **[[NVDA]] earnings (08-26), the Nasdaq rebalance, a Grok 4.7 release, and Flight 14**, and puts hard levels on it: **"a break above $150 ahead of the AUGUST 20 LOCKUP EXPIRY would confirm the stock likely wants higher near term. $135, the IPO price, is the level we're watching on the downside; a break below would suggest we're wrong on the near-term demand/supply dynamics."** ⚠️ Desk positioning commentary, not research — carried for the **08-20 lockup date**, which is the only non-discretionary item in it.

**(3) Ownership datapoint.** Gavin Baker's Atreides disclosed **SPCX as a new position at $4.67bn as of 30 June — roughly 42% of its disclosed equity portfolio and its largest holding** (@wallstengine, 2026-08-16). Also relevant to the [[ANTHROPIC]] page: **Reuters reports SPCX is one of three public comps (with [[NET]] and [[PLTR]]) bankers are using to frame the Anthropic IPO** (2026-08-16).
- **🆕 THE BUY-SIDE BULL RESTATEMENT INTO THE PRINT — Invest Like the Best podcast · Gavin Baker (Atreides), ~2026-08-04.** ⚠️ *Informal podcast commentary, not verified financials.* **"The most important new company to be public," and "the fundamentals have gotten better since it IPO'd: Grok 4.5, the Cursor acquisition — Cursor has clearly accelerated meaningfully — and they've shown over the last three years they can bring on more compute FASTER THAN ANYONE AT LOWER PRICES."** The monetization math he runs: **SpaceX is "monetizing at something like $50 BILLION A GIGAWATT, and consensus estimates for next year are $73 BILLION"** — against the FundaAI channel-check report of **8 GW in 18 months** (the 07-28 FUNDA deep-dive above), which he quotes *because* it is public and then discounts: **"it seems very implausible to me — I almost don't believe the fund report… I don't think it's anywhere near eight. But 8 gigs at $50 billion a gig [≈$400bn] — very little is built into that stock, from my perspective, for the amount of compute that they might be able to bring on."** Supporting scarcity marker: **"to this day the only companies that have brought on more than 500 MW of power in a year are the hyperscalers, CoreWeave, Crusoe and SpaceX — and SpaceX has brought it on both the fastest and at the lowest cost… people actually really like their clusters."** Demand tell from the IPO-era capacity dump: **"they put a vast amount of compute into the market overnight and it wasn't even really a blip — the market just utterly absorbed it, the freight train didn't slow down at all."** Near-term P&L path: **Grok 4.5 + Cursor "probably hits a $10 BILLION ARR pretty quickly — forget Starlink V3, forget direct-to-cell."** He names the other side explicitly: **"there's a big New York hedge fund short case on it — the spot price for compute is going to go down 90%, and all this compute won't generate nearly as much revenue as you think. Maybe."** — but answers with track record ("we specialize in making the impossible late") and the SpaceX-cluster-premium fact already on this page (Meta's rent-out was modeled on SpaceX selling training-optimized clusters at a big premium to contracted rates, see [[META]]). **Orbital compute: "feels more real every day" post-Starship landing — the sanity check being that BENCHMARK (non-Elon ecosystem) funded Starcloud, the orbital-compute company SpaceX is partnering with via Starlink laser links: "maybe I'm crazy and maybe Elon's crazy and maybe Benchmark is also crazy — that just doesn't seem that probable."** [Recorded at Benchmark's office — disclosed in-episode; weight the Starcloud validation accordingly.] (Invest Like the Best · Gavin Baker, Atreides, ~2026-08-04) [Source](../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-04_GavinBaker_ILTB-AI-repricing.md)
- **🆕 A SECOND HOUSE ON THE PAGE, WITH A LOWER PT AND THE SAME DIAGNOSIS — Deutsche Bank · Edison Yu, "Bumpy ride into maiden earnings report" (2026-08-03). BUY, PT $255.00, spot $108.37.** ⚠️ **The PT is materially below MS's $300 while the thesis is nearly identical, so the disagreement is on magnitude not direction — and both houses attribute the drawdown to mechanics rather than fundamentals.** Verbatim: **"While we remain STEADFAST IN OUR LONG-TERM BULL THESIS for SpaceX, the stock has clearly been under significant downward pressure. Simplistically, we think the main issue has been a TACTICAL FEAR OF THE UPCOMING LOCK-UPS, further exacerbated by (i) UNCERTAINTY AROUND THE AI BUSINESS, (ii) COMPLEXITY OF A POTENTIAL TESLA MERGER, and (iii) LOWER THAN EXPECTED PASSIVE INDEX BUYING."** ⚠️ **Item (iii) is net-new to this page and is the most checkable of the three: a passive-demand shortfall is a measurable flow fact, not a sentiment claim, and it compounds the dated 08-06 lockup schedule MS laid out (>930mn shares, ~$100bn, the first of 8 tranches releasing ~4bn shares through January 2027). Items (i) and (ii) are the ones this page has been carrying as open threads.** **The path out, per DB: "we are OPTIMISTIC that FOLLOWING THE LOCK-UPS COMING OFF, THE STOCK CAN STABILIZE at some level. Moreover, ANNOUNCING A LARGE GOVERNMENT/SOVEREIGN AI DEAL WOULD BE VIEWED POSITIVELY."** ⚠️ **Note what that implies for sequencing: DB is saying the lockup is a clearing event rather than a persistent overhang, and that the re-rating catalyst is a sovereign AI contract — i.e. neither of the two things that move the stock is the 08-04 print. Same conclusion MS reached from the opposite starting valuation.** (Deutsche Bank · Edison Yu / Bryan Kraft / Roshan Ranjit / Melissa Weathers / Laura Li, 2026-08-03)
- **🆕 MS re-published its initiation as a video the day before the print — "SpaceX: AI's Final Frontier | Initiate OW, PT $300" (Adam Jonas et al, 2026-08-03).** ⚠️ **Logged for completeness with the date discipline stated: this is a VIDEO RESTATEMENT of the 7 Jul 2026 initiation, NOT a new initiation or a PT action — the underlying note is dated 07-07 and the PT is unchanged at $300. The framing: "With an 'X OF 1' POSITION IN SPACE INFRASTRUCTURE, we believe SpaceX can CONVERT ENERGY INTO INTELLIGENCE AT SCALE with optionality to monetize through a range of consumer and enterprise solutions for the next era of AI."** Re-upping the bull case on the eve of a first print that MS itself expects to be a non-event is a positioning signal about the desk's conviction, not new information.
- **🆕 THE 08-04 FIRST-EVER PRINT AND THE 08-06 LOCKUP EXPIRY LAND 48 HOURS APART — the setup, the Street numbers and the two-sided flow argument (The Information · Martin Peers, "The Briefing: SpaceX's First Earnings Day", 2026-08-02).**
  - **The consensus marks, which this page has not carried: "Wall Street analysts are projecting REVENUE FOR THE JUNE QUARTER OF $6.819 BILLION" against 2025 full-year revenue of $18.6bn and 1Q26 of $4.69bn (+15% y/y).** ⚠️ **Note the implied sequential: $4.69bn → $6.82bn is +45% q/q, which would be a very large step for a launch-and-connectivity business and implies either the compute/neocloud line or Starshield is inflecting hard. The absence of 2025 quarterly comparatives in the IPO filing means nobody can compute a clean y/y — "we don't know what growth that represents." Treat the $6.819bn as a low-conviction consensus.**
  - ⚠️ **THE CASH NUMBER IS THE STORY, AND IT IS LARGE: "Analysts are estimating the company will report a CASH BURN OF $10.9 BILLION, thanks to $14 BILLION IN CAPITAL EXPENDITURES, according to S&P Global Market Intelligence. (In the first quarter, SpaceX reported CAPEX OF $10 BILLION.)" And the forward path: "Analysts project SpaceX's capex will DOUBLE NEXT YEAR, TO $25 BILLION IN THE JUNE QUARTER OF 2027."** **Peers' framing of the asymmetry versus the hyperscalers is the right one: "while SpaceX is spending a FRACTION of the $40 billion to $50 billion each of the big cloud firms is currently laying out on capex every quarter, ITS REVENUE IS A FRACTION OF WHAT THE CLOUD GIANTS BRING IN, which means IT DOESN'T HAVE THE SAME DEEP POCKETS." A ~$14bn quarterly capex against ~$6.8bn of quarterly revenue is a fundamentally different funding profile from [[AMZN]] or [[MSFT]], and it is the reason the neocloud story on this page is a financing question as much as a demand question.**
  - **THE LOCKUP, dated: "Two days after SpaceX's earnings report comes out, ON THURSDAY [08-06], SPACEX'S FIRST LOCKUP RESTRICTIONS ON EARLY SHAREHOLDER SELLING EXPIRE. That will bring HUNDREDS OF MILLIONS OF EXTRA SHARES ONTO THE MARKET, DRAMATICALLY EXPANDING THE FLOAT."** **Where the stock sits going in: "SpaceX closed Friday at $108.37, 49% LOWER THAN ITS POST-IPO PEAK and 20% BELOW ITS IPO PRICE" of $135, market cap ~$1.4tn.** ⚠️ **Peers' own view is explicitly bearish on valuation — "SpaceX was RIDICULOUSLY OVERVALUED at the IPO price of $135, and with a market capitalization now of $1.4 trillion, IT'S STILL VASTLY OVERVALUED" — while conceding the near-term tape could bounce "if only because of what's likely to be an UPBEAT FINANCIAL REPORT."**
  - ⚠️ **THE CONTRARIAN FLOW CALL, and it is worth logging because it is the mirror image of the standard lockup trade: "Controversial take: @SpaceX stock will RISE NOT FALL on the first unlock day next week. A TON OF FUNDS ARE ALREADY SHORT IN ANTICIPATION and they'll wanna cover the short ASAP"** (@PhilipJohnston, 2026-08-01, 840 likes). **This is testable on 08-06 and the two views are cleanly opposed: Peers says lockup expiries "typically put downward pressure on a stock IN THE WEEKS BEFORE they occur, and SpaceX has been no different" — i.e. the −49% already IS the lockup trade — which is logically consistent with the short-covering call rather than against it. If both are right, the pre-positioning is done and the expiry is a covering event.**
  - **Scale marker for the float discussion: "SpaceX's reported $86B IPO represented OVER HALF OF FIRST-HALF U.S. IPO ISSUANCE — $146 billion of common stock was offered in U.S. IPOs in the first 6 months of this year, dwarfing the $26.46 billion in 1H 2025 and the $42.09 billion offered ALL OF LAST YEAR"** (S&P Global Market Intelligence research analyst, via @TheTranscript_, 2026-08-01).
  - ⚠️ **THE MERGER THREAD GETS ITS FINANCIAL LOGIC: "there's a VERY GOOD CHANCE SPACEX AND TESLA WILL MERGE. And [[TSLA]], as it revealed when it reported earnings, IS NOW BURNING CASH ITSELF, thanks to capex increases. What a combination that would be." Two cash-consuming capex programmes under one roof — SpaceX at ~$14bn/quarter rising toward $25bn, Tesla newly FCF-negative — is a materially worse balance sheet than either standalone, which cuts against the usual "Musk conglomerate synergy" framing. Speculative; nothing announced.**
  - **Separate but relevant to the shareholder base: "Investors who BLACKLIST SPACEX OUT OF GOVERNANCE QUALMS are too hung up on box-checking and risk depriving themselves of long-term financial gains, according to Aniket Shah of Jefferies"** (Bloomberg Opinion relay, 2026-08-02) — i.e. the governance discount is now being argued against publicly by a sell-side head of sustainability strategy, which is at least a marker of where the marginal institutional buyer's objection sits. **And the demand-side item that belongs with the neocloud story: [[MEDIATEK]]'s second ASIC customer is believed by JPM to be SPACEX, with confirmation expected before end-2026 and revenue "late 2028 or beyond" (JPM · Hariharan, 2026-08-02) — see the AI-compute section above.**
- **🆕 The Situational Awareness post-mortem, and why it matters for a low-float newly-public name (Doug Garber / Pitch The PM, "Situational Awareness. Risk Management and Experience Absent.", 2026-07-31).** The mechanics of the Citadel purchase, which are directly relevant to how SPCX's own float behaves under stress: **"Situational Awareness had an awesome start to 2026, up over 400%, was running 4X LEVERED, and was down 67% IN JULY before the prime brokers called in Citadel to bail them out."** On why Citadel bought: **"Every 1% discount to the market price is $160MM in PnL/residual/alpha. At 3% it's $480MM, at 5% it's $800MM depending on the hedging and unwind execution… Think of it like an overnight equity offering from an issuer. Only this was a fund of stocks that hit a MARGIN CALL from the prime brokers."** MLP and Jane Street reportedly also bid. ⚠️ **The read-across to SPCX is the float point this page has already flagged: the same episode showed how violently a crowded, levered AI book can move a thin float in both directions — and SPCX was described pre-print as a name where "the catalyst of the V3/starship launch has NOT STOPPED THE SHORTS FROM PILING INTO A LOW FLOAT STOCK" (Garber, 2026-07-30). Aschenbrenner also retains a ~$5bn Anthropic stake and, per one read, "can probably raise $2B-$3B more" (@zephyr_z9, 2026-07-30) — so this is not necessarily a permanently retired participant.**
- **🆕 Corroboration that the New Street bear case is the framing the buy side is actually debating (Bernstein AM note, 2026-07-31).** Bernstein's morning note lists **"SPCX (feedback)"** among its items alongside AMZN, DDOG, PWR, AAPL and LHX — i.e. SpaceX is now in the standard daily rotation of names generating client feedback, one day after the New Street piece. Read with the existing contradiction on this page: **FundaAI models FY27 revenue ~$83B while New Street says its own numbers are "not even half of consensus" — that gap is the single largest unresolved disagreement carried on this page, and it is now being actively litigated rather than ignored.**
- **🆕 Two demand/structure items from reporting, both logged as claims rather than facts (2026-07-31).** ⚠️ **① "SpaceXAI is exploring a MAJOR DATA CENTER EXPANSION IN TEXAS" — a scoop that "Reuters and Data Center Dynamics followed" and that "prompted a research note from Wedbush"** (The Information · Grace Kay, via the 07-31 recap). Relevant to the xAI leg of the sum-of-the-parts and to the [[MEDIATEK]] second-ASIC-customer question, where both JPM and Fubon independently guess SpaceX/xAI. **② "Elon Musk designed Tesla's China business to be easily separated from its U.S. business because of geopolitical tensions. It might also come in handy IF HE PROCEEDS WITH A SPACEX MERGER"** (WSJ, 2026-07-31) — the first public link between [[TSLA]]'s China-separability structure and SpaceX merger mechanics. Neither is confirmed; both are watch items.

- **New Street Research (Pierre Ferragu, via Pitch The PM, 2026-07-30) — the sharpest published bear case on the syndicate numbers: "Our numbers are not even half of consensus."** "Irrational Exuberance–Space Winner or Street Estimates 50% Too High." The direct quotes: **"Treat the syndicate's number as an extremely optimistic bull case"** and **"We think we're already very optimistic—but consensus is even more optimistic."** Key structural points: (1) **"Launch is what makes 100% of the value of SpaceX"** — the model needs **~1 Starship launch a week**, and New Street runs **~1 year behind Elon's timeline** (with the caveat that Musk's targets have a "world-class track record of being late but delivered"); (2) **Starlink has a structural cost advantage** and is already sold out in places, raising prices in capacity-limited markets while adding **0.5-1M subs a month** — which is why the V3/Starship ramp is the gating factor; (3) **the xAI framework: ~$575B base value from a $750B-$1T 2030 market at ~15% share** (on Anthropic/OpenAI's ~4.6x 2030 revenue), "and why it's a balanced oligopoly, not winner-take-all"; (4) **the spot-vs-planned compute arb: ~$50B/GW (the $15B / 0.3GW Anthropic deal) vs [[CRWV]]'s ~$12B/GW — "and why that premium may not last"**; (5) the honest setup: "**Consensus was built on Elon-optimism to sell the deal**; the stock will trade quarter-to-quarter on 'where there's light' (Starlink subs, Starship launches)." Host's framing: "The catalyst of the V3/starship launch has not stopped the shorts from piling into a low float stock … 'Irrational Exuberance' and the bond market might be signaling the same thing." _(Episode originally recorded as a 2026-06-22 webinar; published 2026-07-30.)_
- **FundaAI Institutional preview (2026-07-29) — the bull-side counterweight, and it is specific on compute.** "Q2 print as an unlock trigger: the quarter itself has limited surprise risk, with **Q2 revenue modeled at ~$6.89B versus consensus ~$6.99B**. The larger event comes around **August 6, when up to 911.5mm shares, or roughly $105B at $115, may release; the Q3 print later unlocks another ~1.28B shares.**" On compute: "**Three signed leases with Anthropic, Google and Reflection annualize to ~$28B at full rate, with total contract value above $75B. External compute revenue is expected to step from ~$1.4B in Q2 to ~$4.6B in Q3 and ~$7.0B in Q4**, but exit clauses and the **Google 9/30 delivery deadline** remain key risk dates." On capacity: "**xAI serving ~1.5 GW today, exiting 2026 near ~2.0 GW and potentially reaching ~5.0 GW by YE27. Our FY27 revenue view is ~$83B** versus a signed-contracts-only [base]." Separately, @jukan05 (2026-07-30, 461 likes): "I don't understand why this FundaAI report hasn't received more attention. **SpaceX plans to build 4 GW of compute capacity using Rubin racks next year.**"
- **Note the direct contradiction between the two above.** FundaAI models **FY27 revenue ~$83B** while New Street says its own numbers are **"not even half of consensus."** These are not reconcilable; both are on this page deliberately. The discriminator is Starship cadence (New Street: ~1/week needed, running a year behind Elon) and whether the xAI compute leases survive their exit clauses — the **Google 9/30 delivery deadline** is the nearest hard test.
- **Also flagged:** Fubon's Sherman Shang believes a **second major [[MEDIATEK]] ASIC project may emerge later, potentially tied to xAI/SpaceX** (via JEF, 2026-07-30) — a second-order read on SpaceX's compute ambitions showing up in the Taiwan supply chain.

- **🆕 ⚠️ Terafab — [[KLAC]] was asked on the record whether it is engaged, and declined to confirm (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 earnings call, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-07-28).** Citi's Atif Malik put it directly: *"one of your customers, SpaceX, publicly Terafab, has talked about improving fab manufacturing efficiencies, cutting down steps, and the cycle time and all that. And I was curious if you guys are engaged on that project."* CEO Rick Wallace: ***"we don't talk about any specific customer engagements. But you can imagine that if anybody wants to have any kind of innovation or driving new capabilities in the semiconductor industry, we're going to be along the list of the stops that they make. But we don't talk about specific engagements."*** ⚠️ **Read it as neither confirmation nor denial — it is KLA's standard customer-confidentiality posture, and the question itself frames SpaceX as "one of your customers."** But the *absence* of confirmation is itself worth logging on this page, for two reasons. **(1) It is a live input to a bull leg other people are underwriting elsewhere in the wiki.** UBS's Arcuri has the [[KLAC]] bull case resting partly on *"Terafab ramping from zero"* (2026-07-25), and UBS has separately sized Terafab as a potential **>$50B/yr WFE spender** (~$135B over five years; pilot ~$5B C2027 → ~$10B C2028, 2026-07-07). Those are supplier-side estimates built on Terafab's public statements, **not on disclosed orders** — and the leading process-control vendor would not confirm an engagement when asked. **(2) It sharpens the disclosure test.** Elsewhere on this page KLA's peers appear in the Terafab narrative only through sell-side sizing; the first *company-confirmed* semicap engagement — from anyone — is the datapoint that would convert Terafab from a stated ambition into a funded capex programme. None exists yet. ⚠️ Note also that in the same call KLA said it has **not modelled any Terafab-type new entrant into its own CY27 plan**, describing potential contribution from *"some of our legacy customers"* and non-plan sources as *"potentially… an upside factor, although not a big one for us"* — i.e. the WFE incumbent is not yet spending against it. (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 earnings call, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260728_KLA_Corp-_Earnings_Call_2026-7-28_RT000000003114277744pdf.html)
- **🆕🔴 SPACEX HAS BEEN PROMOTED INTO THE AI-CAPEX COMPLEX BY MORGAN STANLEY'S CREDIT DESK — and the RECLASSIFICATION is the datapoint, not the issuance (MS Global Credit Strategy · Fernanda Lima / Carolyn Campbell / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Vishwas Patkar / James Egan, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20).** **MS states that its hyperscaler capex aggregate "now includes SPCX"** — i.e. SpaceX is now tracked alongside **AMZN, GOOGL, META, MSFT and ORCL** inside a **~$1.4 TRILLION 2027 capex** aggregate whose combined 2027 estimates have **nearly doubled versus November 2025 estimates**. ➤ **Why this matters beyond taxonomy: it moves SPCX out of "space/telecom name with an AI segment" and into the cohort whose credit spreads, issuance calendar and capex revisions are followed as a single trade. That is close to the re-rating MS's equity side has been arguing for since the 07-07 initiation — arriving independently, from a desk with no equity view to defend.** **On the issuance itself the tracker CORROBORATES rather than adds: SpaceX $25bn YTD-2026, from a single June deal** — the same **$25bn inaugural five-tranche investment-grade senior-notes offering** already on this page from the Q2 primary transcript, so treat it as a second source on a known number, not a new one. **Sector context: HQ hyperscaler spreads ~30bp WIDER YTD and supply has not slowed regardless — $54bn done in July, $41bn in August MTD, ~$445bn of issuance across credit channels YTD vs $217bn for FULL-YEAR 2025, FY26E $500-550bn**, with MS expecting a busy fall and continued widening and noting HQ issuers *"will continue to flex their ratings muscles to provide credit support to others."* Within YTD-2026 IG issuance ($271bn total): **HQ Hyperscalers $132bn** (monthly Jan→Aug 0, 20, 37, 25, 0, 0, 25, 25), **SpaceX $25bn**, Oracle $25bn, Semiconductors $42bn, Data Center Construction $43bn; **only Alphabet and Amazon have used non-dollar markets this year.** ⚠️ **NO per-name capex split was taken and none should be inferred — that exhibit is column-interleaved and cannot be read reliably from the text layer. Aggregate and constituent list only.** ⚠️ **PAGE-STRUCTURE NOTE: this page has no `## Intra-quarter — calls, commentary & reports` section, so the mandatory full-log row could not be added — see `## Changelog`.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USCREDIT_20260820_2103.html)
- **Bull:** Unique vertical integration — the only entity pairing frontier AI (Grok/xAI), gigawatt-scale terrestrial compute (COLOSSUS), a dominant satellite-connectivity franchise (Starlink ~10.3M subs), reusable launch (Falcon/Starship) and a chip ambition (Terafab). Fast, low-cost datacenter construction (91–122 days/cluster, below-benchmark per-MW cost). Starlink is the cash-generative anchor; the orbital-compute angle is asymmetric optionality. New incremental AI-compute demand node for the supply chain (Jeff Pu CRDO Buy, 2026-06-29). **NEW:** external compute monetization is now real and large — xAI's Colossus 1 sold to Anthropic for **$1.25bn/month** covering 220k+ GPUs (JPM, 2026-06-30) — a concrete external-revenue proof point that de-risks the "deeply loss-making AI segment" bear case, if the deal is representative of a broader neocloud pivot (Vital Knowledge parallel to Meta's "Meta Compute," 2026-07-01). A Tesla-SpaceX merger, if it happens, would also unlock Tesla's Terafab/AI-chip roadmap and balance sheet alongside SpaceX's (The Information, 2026-07-01). **Color (FinTwit):** with SpaceX finally going public, investors expecting Space/Mars/rockets "may instead see a galaxy of server racks" — the datacenter/compute angle increasingly framed as the real core of the SPCX story (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-07-09).
- **Bear:** **Deep losses** — pro-forma net loss ~$(4.9)bn FY2025, AI-segment Adj. EBITDA **$(1,237)M in 2025** (from +$347M in 2024) and **$(609)M in Q1-26** as it scales compute. ⚠️ **THIS LEG IS NOW LARGELY SPENT: AI-segment adjusted EBITDA turned POSITIVE at +$1.1bn in Q2-26 (net operating loss narrowed to $1.3bn) on high incremental hosting margins — a swing of ~$1.7bn in one quarter (SpaceX Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04). The durable version of the bear case is now CASH, not segment profitability: ~$18.4bn/quarter of capex guided flat for two more quarters against ~$3.5bn of 1H operating cash flow. Prior framing retained above for the trail; see `## Changelog` (2026-08-05).** **Extreme key-man / governance risk** — Musk ~82.4% voting, controlled-company exemptions, Class B vesting tied to a literal Mars colony. **GPU supply on purchase-orders only** (no long-term contracts). The 100 GW-to-orbit goal is explicitly unproven. Valuation (~$1.7tn+ implied) prices in enormous execution. **NEW:** xAI reportedly **dialing back internal AI-tool ambitions** (Grok, Cursor) just as it pivots to selling compute externally (Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-01) — could read as a tacit admission that in-house frontier-model economics aren't working as well as the neocloud/reseller model, undercutting the "unique vertical integration" bull framing. The **AI-handset prototype** (WSJ via Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-01) and **TMUS-merger rumor** (Jefferies/Handelsblatt, 2026-07-01) both read as scope-creep speculation with no confirmed strategic rationale; Crisafulli explicitly skeptical SpaceX can scale a consumer device platform, and reiterates Starlink stays complementary to AT&T/Verizon/T-Mobile rather than displacing them. **Valuation skepticism (FinTwit):** "65 bid for SpaceX $SPCX. Not 165. 65." — a pointed quip that the name is worth a fraction of where it trades (@mark_dow, 2026-07-08).
- **Where the sell-side stands — the initiation wave landed 2026-07-07, uniformly Buy/OW (stock ref ~$160):**
  - **Morgan Stanley (Adam Jonas et al) — OW, PT $300** (bull $600 / bear $75; >85% upside). Thesis: integrated 'Space + Connectivity + AI Infrastructure' platform. **Revenue $45B (2026) → $319B (2030) → $3.3T (2040)**, Enterprise AI + Connectivity nearly all the value. Terrestrial AI capex **~half industry per watt (~$4-5 vs $9, ex-chips)**; compute adds 1.2GW/2.2GW/3.8GW ('26/'27/'28) → ~13GW terrestrial by 2030; orbital 160MW ('28) → 2.7GW ('30) → 364GW ('40). Starlink subs 9.3M→116M ('25→'30). **Not FCF+ before 2035**, needs ~$84B/yr external capital '27-34. Cursor ~$4B ARR "underappreciated." (Morgan Stanley · Jonas/Nowak/Liwag/S.Kim, 2026-07-07)

It is a clean integrity check on both the screen and the page.** 🔴 **Two facts this page did not carry: SPCX is the LARGEST MARKET CAP in MS's entire 50-name screen, and it carries the WIDEST BULL CASE in it. "Core to Thesis" is MS's top materiality bucket — the only other name patched on this wiki from the same exhibit, [[ETN]], sits one tier down at "Significant" with a +9% base case.** ⚠️ **Scope note recorded once, because both figures come from the same house within five weeks: the note's AI-capex frame is "$860bn in the US this year alone" (US-scoped), while the $877bn in MS's 2026-07-14 "Power Struggle" note is the GLOBAL 2026 estimate. Different measures, not a revision.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html)
