### JPM initiation deep dive (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07)
_The single most detailed sell-side model on the name; stock ref $160.42 (06-Jul-26), 52-wk range $147.11-225.64, BBG ANR 9 Buy / 4 Hold / 1 Sell._
- **Valuation / PT.** OW, **Dec-27 PT $225**, based on **~41x 2028E GAAP EPS of $5.50**, with EV/GAAP-EBIT (~44x 2028E) + SOTP + DCF as secondary support. Premium justified vs mega-cap peers (Mag7 + PLTR ~38x avg / ~20x median) by the integrated Launch + Connectivity + AI platform and the financial ramp. JPM's own estimates: **revenue $18.7B (FY25A) → $41.3B (FY26E) → $74.8B (FY27E) → $160.4B (FY28E)**; Adj. EBITDA $6.6B → $19.8B → $44.4B → $110.9B (margin 35%→69%); Adj. EPS $(0.84) → $0.43 → $1.19 → $5.77; **FCFF deeply negative throughout** ($(11.9)B FY25 → $(78.8)B FY28) — a self-funding gap that requires heavy external capital. JPM treats the DCF as directional only (cash flows don't inflect positive until 2031E, so ~all DCF value sits in the terminal year); the SOTP is a cross-check complicated by the fact the three segments are intentionally NOT independent (Launch subsidizes Connectivity subsidizes orbital compute). BBG consensus is far below JPM (BBG EPS $(0.99)/$(0.04)/$0.50 for FY26/27/28).
- **Starlink scale (as of 31-Mar-26).** World's largest LEO constellation — **9,600+ broadband & mobile satellites (~75% of all active maneuverable satellites in orbit)**, serving **12M+ active customers across 164 countries**. JPM models Starlink subs **9M (2025) → 95M+ (2030)**, driving US broadband share **3.0% (today) → 8.3% (2030)** — a trajectory driven entirely by Starlink. Launch backbone: ~670 orbital launches at 99%+ mission success, 80%+ of all mass to orbit since 2023, but only ~25% of launches / <15% of mass last year were for external customers (the rest is internal Starlink deployment).
- **D2D / Starlink Mobile — cautious on US wireless.** JPM does **NOT** expect Starlink Mobile to take meaningful US wireless share over the next several years: the mid-May **direct-to-device JV** (Starlink/T-Mobile) and all three US carriers' stated lack of MVNO interest cement D2D as a supplement, not a replacement — Starlink stays a rural/supplemental niche (models only "a couple million" subs with minimal impact on incumbents' profit pool). JPM sees a US MVNO as ~50/50 long-term and a standalone terrestrial build as less advantageous. Notably, JPM models **~56% of Connectivity capex (~$111B through 2031) devoted to mobile** — so the mid-2027 Upper C-band spectrum auction is the key swing on whether the "disruption overhang" on the telecom group resolves. The competitive D2D field JPM frames: **Starlink/T-Mobile vs. AST SpaceMobile+AT&T/Verizon vs. Apple/Globalstar vs. Amazon Kuiper**. Longer-term, JPM assumes Starlink reaches **~14% penetration of a $740B mobile TAM**.
- **Starship & launch cadence.** Starship (full/rapid reuse) ramps from a handful of launches in 2026 to **~5,000 in 2031** (~7,500 by end of the orbital-compute build; ~8k cumulative Starlink+compute launches through 2031). Orbital compute needs ~75 GW by early 2030s → ~7,500 launches weighted to the back end (10+ refueling launches per deep-space Starship). Payload rising toward a Starship V4 at up to 200 MT (not required to hit the early-2030s orbital-compute target). **COLOSSUS + COLOSSUS II already provide ~1 GW of terrestrial compute**; JPM models terrestrial ~8x to **~8 GW by end-2028**, then orbital compute scaling in earnest from 2029, ramping to **75 GW in 2031** at capex/orbital-GW <half of terrestrial buildout cost.
- **Competitor note — Amazon Kuiper.** JPM flags Amazon LEO (Kuiper) as a D2D/broadband competitor with FCC authorization for **~7,725 satellites but <400 launched to date**, gated by slow non-SpaceX launch cadence (ULA Vulcan, Blue Origin New Glenn) — underscoring SpaceX's launch moat. (See [AMZN.md](AMZN.md) for the Kuiper read-through.)

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴🔴 2026-11-12 — [[OPENAI]] CUTS CURSOR'S DIRECT ACCESS TO ITS MODELS. A hard date on a newly acquired asset.** OpenAI's own post: *"**We're ending our partnership with Cursor following its acquisition by SpaceX.** Under our proposal, **Cursor's direct access to our models would end on November 12**"* (@OpenAI, 2026-08-29; confirmed by CNBC and Bloomberg; carried by UBS and JPM's Tech Sketch). ➜ **THE NUMBER TO FIND BEFORE THE DATE: what share of Cursor's usage and revenue runs on OpenAI models today. Without it the impact is unquantifiable.** ⚠️ **Two aggravating facts: (a) Musk's own first address to Cursor reportedly conceded *"Grok Is Falling Behind"* (The Information, 08-31), so the in-house substitute is not ready on the acquirer's own account; (b) the practice is not unique — *"[[ANTHROPIC]] did the same thing with Windsurf too. Frontier labs are insanely protective of their IP over distillation risks, while expecting enterprise customers to trust them with their IP"* (@Hesamation, 2026-08-29). **Owning an application layer that depends on a rival lab's models is now a recognised single point of failure.**
- **🆕⚠️⚠️ CURSOR'S AGENT WAS USED IN A LIVE INTRUSION CAMPAIGN — a product-liability and enterprise-trust exposure on the same asset, independent of the OpenAI cutoff.** *"**Russian-speaking hackers used SpaceX-owned Cursor's AI agent to help breach at least 7 companies.** The group reportedly **tricked the [[ANTHROPIC]]-powered agent into treating the attacks as simulations**, helping **speed up break-ins by as much as 50%**"* (Semafor via @wallstengine, corpus, 2026-08-27). **Watch for: named victims, any regulatory or contractual fallout, and whether enterprise Cursor deployments are paused. Cross-filed [[ANTHROPIC]].**
- **🆕 THE BASTROP FOUNDRY BUILD CLOCK — the falsifiable milestones are construction permits, furnace capital-equipment orders (lead times of up to three years per MS) and NADCAP/OEM part-number qualification.** ➜ **Falsifier for the Musk 18-month claim: no qualified production part out of Bastrop by ~2030 would put the whole time-to-power argument behind the 10GW ambition it exists to serve.** (The Information 08-29 · Musk 08-30 · MS desk 08-31)
- **🆕 THE AI-CAPEX-COHORT READ-ACROSS IS NOW A LIVE CHANNEL (MS Global Credit Strategy, 2026-08-20).** With MS's hyperscaler capex aggregate **"now includ[ing] SPCX"** alongside AMZN/GOOGL/META/MSFT/ORCL (**~$1.4trn 2027 aggregate, nearly double the Nov-2025 estimate**), SPCX should be expected to trade on hyperscaler capex revisions and **HQ hyperscaler credit spreads (~30bp wider YTD)** rather than only on launch/Starlink/Cursor news. Watch: whether the next SpaceX IG deal prices with the cohort, and whether MS's equity and credit desks converge on one capex path. ⚠️ **No per-name capex figures are readable from the tracker — do not build a SPCX capex line off it.**

- **🆕🔴 Through end-2026 — THE UNLOCK SELL-THROUGH RATE IS NOW A SCOREABLE NUMBER, NOT A NARRATIVE.** MS (Jonas, 2026-08-19): **~3.3bn shares unlock between now and year-end**, and the bear premise "supply is undefeated" depends on two unobserved variables Jonas challenges directly — **what fraction is actually sold (he floats 30%)** and **how much of that is absorbed by PASSIVE INDEX FUNDS**. **Watch actual sell-through against the 3.3bn and passive/ETF flow data; this is the cleanest falsifiable test on the page.**
- **🆕🔴 Chinese reusable launch — 2 orbital-class booster recoveries in 6 WEEKS (LandSpace Zhuque-3, Gansu, 2026-08-19; Reuters + MS · Jonas).** LandSpace joins SpaceX and Blue Origin as the only private companies to recover an orbital-class booster. **This is the structural moat item: watch cadence and payload class, not the individual landing.**
- **🆕 2026-08-20 10:00am ET — MS investor webcast, "SpaceX: Key Catalysts and Risks on the Path to $300"** (Jonas/Tackett).
- **🆕 THE BASTROP "BLADES AND VANES" FOUNDRY — a multi-year watch item with no announcement to anchor it (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-08-16).** Watch for: (i) any SpaceX confirmation of the foundry (today it exists only as job postings), (ii) groundbreaking/equipment orders — **vacuum-furnace lead times run up to 3 years**, so the first equipment order dates the ramp better than any press release, (iii) **first qualified production, which MS puts at 2-3+ years out at the earliest**, and (iv) whether SpaceX's disclosed turbine commitments keep climbing past the ~$2.8bn / ~8 GW currently piece-able together. **The falsifiable test on the near-term number: does SpaceX get to MS's 5 GW by YE27, or Musk's 10 GW?**
- **🆕 THE CURSOR CATALYST PATH (Morgan Stanley / Adam Jonas, 2026-08-10).** In order: **close of the $60.0bn all-stock Cursor/Anysphere acquisition (3Q26, "within the next few weeks")** → **3Q earnings, the first report post-close, for further disclosure** → **Grok 4.6 and 4.7 "over coming weeks"** (both incorporating Cursor interaction data) → **possible Composer 3** → **Grok 5 before year-end** → periodic ARR figures. ⚠️ MS's model is unvetted by management; the ARR marks are the checkable output.
- **🆕 [[INTC]] 14A — SpaceX interest cited by Intel management as an endorsement of node maturity (Intel $15bn follow-on roadshow, 2026-08-10).** ⚠️ Sourced from the seller, not from SpaceX; no commitment confirmed. Watch whether SpaceX itself ever names a second foundry.

**🆕 DATED FROM MANAGEMENT ON THE 08-04 CALL (SpaceX Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04) — the forward calendar in the company's own words:**
- 🔴 **END OF AUGUST 2026 — STARSHIP FLIGHT 14, "tentatively scheduled for the end of this month," regulatory approval permitting.** ⚠️ **This is the highest-information near-term event on the page after the lockup, and it carries THREE firsts at once: (i) the first attempt to CATCH THE SHIP with the tower, (ii) the first flight of V3 Starlink satellites TO OPERATIONAL ORBIT, and (iii) the first flight after Musk declared the heat-shield problem "solved." It is also the catalyst the October leg of SIG's call calendar was constructed to own.**
- **~1 WEEK (mid-Aug 2026) — Grok 4.6. ~3-4 WEEKS (early Sep 2026) — Grok 4.7. BEFORE END-2026 — Grok 5**, trained on the entire 25-year SpaceX data corpus.
- **Q3-26 / OCTOBER 2026 — the [[GOOG]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] cloud agreements "start to ramp either later this quarter or in October," plus the additional $6.7bn of cloud services revenue contracted in early Q3 "begins ramping starting in October."** ⚠️ **The Q3 print is therefore the first clean read on whether the $100bn December ARR bridge is real — and the Google 9/30 delivery deadline already on this page sits inside that window.**
- **DECEMBER 2026 — the $100bn ARR test itself**, since management defined it as December-month revenue annualized. **Also: >2 GW of nameplate compute exiting 2026.**
- **2027 — Mobile V2 satellites start flying "next year," with Starlink Mobile SERVICE STARTING "END OF NEXT YEAR"; EchoStar's 65 MHz integrated "later next year." ~1,000 V3 broadband satellites (the critical mass for a service step-change) by ~Q2-27. Starmind AI satellites begin launching. Exit-2027 targets: ~10 GW nameplate compute AND 15-20 GW of power/cooling** (⚠️ different bases — see the transcript section above).
- **~AUG 2027 — "probably a year from now, we will be doing at least ONE FLIGHT A DAY, possibly more"** (Musk, on Starship cadence). ⚠️ **The single most falsifiable claim on this page, and the one that decides the New Street bear case.**
- **2027 — ARTEMIS III (docking with Orion); then an uncrewed direct-to-lunar cargo mission; 2028 — "BOOTS ON THE MOON."** Human-rated Starship safety expected *"probably by the end of next year."* Orbital propellant transfer is the gating milestone.
- **NEAR-TERM, UNDATED — the Cursor acquisition close ("should close quite soon… we've withdrew almost all of the regulatory hurdles"), with a combined product roadmap announcement deliberately held for a separate venue.**

- 🔴 **2026-08-06 — THE LOCKUP. The only thing that matters near-term, and both houses say so.** **>930mn shares (~$100bn) release on Aug 6 — the first of 8 tranches unlocking ~4bn shares through January 2027** (MS · Adam Jonas, 2026-07-28). 22V's counter-arithmetic: **911mn unlocking shares against a 555mn current float, but 20% of that (~182mn) is only ~2-3 days of volume** and could be absorbed by short covering plus **September Nasdaq-rebalance positioning starting within weeks** (22V · Dauvin Peterson, 2026-08-04). DB's view is that it is a **clearing event, not a persistent overhang** ("we are optimistic that following the lock-ups coming off, the stock can stabilize at some level"). **With the print now behind, this is the page's single live catalyst.**
- 🔴 **★ 🆕 THE LOCKUP IS A NINE-WEEK DRIP, NOT ONE EVENT — the granular tranche calendar, which changes the shape of the overhang (via Capstone internal research synthesis · Daniel Grozdea, 2026-08-04, pre-print; the synthesis is the only source on this page for the Aug-21/Sep-10/Sep-25 marks).** ⚠️ **The page carries the 08-06 tranche and 22V's Dec-9 mark, and describes the scheme as "8 tranches." The synthesis describes an unusually complex NINE-TRANCHE scheme deliberately built to stagger insider selling across the first six months rather than a single 180-day expiry — and the intermediate tranches are the net-new part:**
  - **Aug 4 (earnings) — ~10% of shares unlock.**
  - **Aug 6 — >930mn shares (~$100bn), the first major tranche, against a current float of ~555mn** (already on the page, MS 07-28 / 22V 08-04).
  - **🆕 Aug 21 — another ~7%.** ⚠️ **Note this is also the expiry of the short leg in SIG's suggested call calendar (see Debate) — the August strike is being sold into a supply date.**
  - **🆕 Sep 10 and Sep 25 — further tranches, taking ~30% OF SHARES TRADABLE BY SEP 25.**
  - **Through Jan 2027 — ~4bn shares total across 8-9 tranches** (consistent with MS 07-28). **Mid-2027 — Musk personally plus some institutions.**

It argues that DB's "clearing event" framing (08-03) resolves in late September rather than on Aug 6, and it is the mechanical reason a durable re-rate more plausibly waits for Flight 14 and the late-September Nasdaq rebalance than for the 08-06 print-plus-two-days.** ⚠️ **Also note the ~30%-by-Sep-25 figure is the same threshold 22V flagged (07-08) as the trigger for SPCX moving from float-adjusted to FULL market-cap weight in the Nasdaq-100 — i.e. the peak-supply date and the peak-passive-demand date are the same date.**

- **Starship Flight 13 (Fri 2026-07-24) — RESOLVED, operationally successful; Flight 14 is now the key catalyst (MS · Adam Jonas; Bernstein · Tyler Seidman, both 2026-07-27).** **Jonas grades the mission 'A-' overall and concludes it MATERIALLY IMPROVED CONFIDENCE IN STARSHIP'S PATH TOWARD OPERATIONAL SERVICE, addressing near-term concerns after the JULY 17 LAUNCH ABORT.** Milestones achieved: **clean ascent with all 33 Super Heavy engines operating; successful hot-stage separation; FIRST DEPLOYMENT OF 20 PRODUCTION STARLINK V3 SATELLITES; successful in-space Raptor relight; and the BEST STARSHIP REENTRY AND SPLASHDOWN TO DATE, with the ship remaining intact and transmitting telemetry.** (Bernstein notes the splashdown was hard.) **The new upside catalyst: Elon Musk indicated FLIGHT 14 MAY ATTEMPT THE FIRST-EVER CATCH OF A STARSHIP UPPER STAGE USING THE MECHAZILLA TOWER ARMS — expected LATE AUGUST OR EARLY SEPTEMBER 2026. A ship catch would likely require Starship's first ORBITAL trajectory, potentially enabling the first deployment of OPERATIONAL Starlink V3 satellites.'** Bernstein frames the sequence as **putting FULL REUSE IN SIGHT AT FLIGHT 15**: 'full reuse is key for SpaceX to set launch rates above any competitor, though THE MARKET HAS YET TO UNDERWRITE THESE LAUNCHES, which could become positive catalysts given the recent drawdown post-IPO and as the LOCKUP OVERHANG BEGINS TO DISSIPATE FOLLOWING 2Q/3Q EPS (8/4).' Jonas separately argues the pullback created an attractive entry because fundamentals are largely unchanged while sentiment has turned increasingly bearish.

## Risks
- **Profitability / cash burn** — multi-billion net losses; AI segment deeply EBITDA-negative while scaling.
- **Key-man & governance** — Musk control (~82.4% vote), controlled-company exemptions, Mars/Tbps milestone vesting; concentrated decision-making.
- **GPU / supply concentration** — purchase-order-only GPU procurement; dependence on a concentrated set of advanced fabs and on power availability.
- **Execution / unproven moonshots** — 100 GW orbital compute, Terafab (1 TW/yr), Starship — all explicitly uncertain in the prospectus.
- **Valuation** — ~$1.7tn+ implied equity value on a still loss-making, capex-heavy combination.
- **Regulatory** — spectrum, launch licensing, AI/chatbot safety (Grok), cross-border satellite operations.

Could Starlink technically do this? Sure. Would also blame POSITIONING for the magnitude of this move: telco investors have been navigating the 'wireless is over' narrative for months so most were largely de-risked, whereas TOWERS were viewed as a THEORETICAL BENEFICIARY from SPCX so a lot were LONG INTO LAST NIGHT" (UBS TMI · Christina Dwyer, 08-05). That is why a 75% EBITDA beat produced a reiterated PT rather than a raised one.** **The guidance items MS calls "rabbits out of the hat," with its own prior marks for scale: ≥$100bn TOTAL COMPANY ARR by end-Dec 2026 (MS's prior 4Q forecast implied $19.1bn); "closer to 10GW than 5GW" of YE27 nameplate compute (MS was at 4.15GW); 1,000 V3 sats by 2Q27 (MS 840) with V3 deploying from Starship flight 14; and the heat-shield problem "largely solved" on flight-13 evidence.** ⚠️ **MS's honest tail caveat, worth carrying against the enthusiasm: consensus should rise "for the next 12 to 18 months," BUT "we believe consensus forecasts for 2029-2031 are FULL/COULD FACE MODEST DOWNSIDE RISK."** ⚠️ **On the 08-06 unlock the two published houses agree on the mechanics and split on the timing: MS calls the ~$100bn unlock "AN OPPORTUNITY to gain exposure"; DB (08-03) argued the stock "can stabilize" only AFTER the lock-ups come off. Buy before or after — that is the whole disagreement.** **GS adds the segment read: Starlink broadband subs EXCEEDED its forecasts with minimal ARPU dilution (against investor concern over the geographic source of incremental units), AI segment revenue and operating income both exceeded, and management is optimistic about "building to more terrestrial compute and SIGNIFICANTLY ABOVE-AVERAGE MONETIZATION OF COMPUTE (given the current supply/demand imbalance) in the coming 12-18 months." Its caution is about price discovery, not fundamentals.** **★★ TWO CROSS-COMPANY READ-THROUGHS, both from the call and both material beyond this page: (1) ⚠️ MEMORY — Musk: "The current limiting factor is MEMORY. Memory output increases roughly 20% per year… but demand is increasing 200% per year, maybe more. So if demand is growing far faster than supply, per Economics 101, PRICES SHOULD GO UP, NOT DOWN." A hyperscale-scale BUYER publicly arguing memory prices rise is the demand-side counterpart to the supply-side case at [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]] and [[KIOXIA]] — and it landed the SAME WEEK SanDisk guided "modest" ASP growth and fell on peak-margin fears. Cross-filed to [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). (2) ★ COMPUTE IS ALL-[[NVDA]] AND EXCLUSIVE — "We have decided to build entirely on NVIDIA. We think Vera is the best architecture and the best AI computer," with compute >2 GW by end-2026 and close to 10 GW by end-2027. The tape moved on it: NVDA led the Mag 7 higher for a fifth consecutive session on the exclusivity headline.** ⚠️ **Third-party sizing logged but NOT adopted: Melius reckons 6 GW of SpaceX GPU purchases in 2027 would add ">$200bn" to NVDA's 2027 revenue and "a more realistic 2-3 GW would add $100bn" — that implies ~$33-35bn per GW, 2-3x the $10-20bn/GW this wiki carries elsewhere. Flagged as a GW-basis mismatch to reconcile before use.** ⚠️ **Both call quotes come via @QQ_Timmy's Chinese-language transcription of the call; this page holds no SPCX transcript, so the exact wording is approximate and is presented as such. The SPCX Q2 earnings-call PDF is sitting in `_inbox` awaiting the 23h /run-inbox pass — that will be the primary source and should be reconciled against this entry.** Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [[KIOXIA]], [[TSLA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

- **2026-07-27** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook). 1 Catalysts entry — a catalyst RESOLVED plus its successor dated. Nothing superseded.** **Starship Flight 13 (Friday 2026-07-24) was operationally successful. MS (Adam Jonas) grades it 'A-' and says it MATERIALLY IMPROVED CONFIDENCE IN STARSHIP'S PATH TOWARD OPERATIONAL SERVICE, addressing near-term concerns after the JULY 17 LAUNCH ABORT — which is the specific overhang this flight needed to clear.** Milestones: **all 33 Super Heavy engines clean on ascent; successful hot-stage separation; FIRST DEPLOYMENT OF 20 PRODUCTION STARLINK V3 SATELLITES; successful in-space Raptor relight; and the BEST REENTRY AND SPLASHDOWN TO DATE with the ship intact and transmitting telemetry** (Bernstein notes the splashdown itself was hard — both characterisations kept). **The successor catalyst is now dated and is materially bigger than Flight 13 was: Musk indicated FLIGHT 14 MAY ATTEMPT THE FIRST-EVER CATCH OF A STARSHIP UPPER STAGE ON THE MECHAZILLA TOWER ARMS, expected LATE AUGUST OR EARLY SEPTEMBER 2026 — and a ship catch would likely require Starship's first ORBITAL trajectory, potentially enabling the first deployment of OPERATIONAL Starlink V3 satellites. Bernstein puts FULL REUSE IN SIGHT AT FLIGHT 15.** **The investment argument both houses make is the same and it is about what is not yet in the price: 'full reuse is key for SpaceX to set launch rates above any competitor, though THE MARKET HAS YET TO UNDERWRITE THESE LAUNCHES' — with the LOCKUP OVERHANG BEGINNING TO DISSIPATE FOLLOWING 2Q/3Q EPS ON 8/4.** Jonas adds that the pullback created an attractive entry since fundamentals are largely unchanged while sentiment turned increasingly bearish. **Two competitive items logged alongside: [[AMZN]] applied to the FCC for 5,105 SATELLITES for a direct-to-device service (post-Globalstar, alongside its Leo broadband build) — The Information's caution being that 'it won't be as profitable a business for either SpaceX or Amazon once they're both in it'; and Bernstein's US telco work concluding that while Starlink has substantial broadband runway, MOBILE DISRUPTION MAY NOT BE AS IMMINENT AS THE MARKET PRESUMED — an MVNO looks unlikely near-term and incumbents largely view satellite as a replacement to terrestrial, so the risk 'will linger into 2027 and 2028' rather than landing in 2H26.** Also: **Exploration Company GmbH, a SPCX rival, is in talks to raise $300M at a $2B valuation**; and SPCX joined the NVDA/[[MSFT]] AI-safety initiative. OW / PT $300 both unchanged — additive, no figure superseded.** The note reverse-engineers the price many investors expect on the first lockup-expiry waves. Against MS base-case **FY28 $157bn revenue** (incl. **$16.5/Watt on avg 6 GW** and **35mn avg consumer subs @ $46/mo ARPU**) **and $69bn EBIT**: **$100 = 18x EV/EBIT FY28** (≈ comp median but on **3x the revenue CAGR, 76% vs 26% for 2025-30**) and **16x FY28 P/E — a >50% discount to WMT (33x) and 40% to TJX (26.5x)**. The load-bearing argument is the SOTP: **40x EV/EBITDA on Space + Connectivity (below the 46x space-comp median) implies ZERO value for AI; 50x implies NEGATIVE ~$300bn for AI.** MS's $300 PT rests on a 15-yr DCF = **56x EV/EBITDA on Space+Connectivity ≈ $136/share**, so at $100 the market implies **zero AI value *and* FY28 Space+Connectivity EBITDA ~30% below MSe**. **Sentiment/positioning colour (the genuinely new content):** "no major fundamental changes to the story over the past few weeks" yet **"bears appear to have become gradually more convicted, while bulls appear more resigned to wait for more obvious positive catalysts,"** and investor-conversation frequency has been **"less intense than we would have expected"**; **"most investors significantly discount Grok & Cursor and view the bulk of the AI monetization opportunity as neocloud,"** with many ascribing **zero or negative AI value** on capex intensity, uncertain economics and the **management-time** drain. Risks Jonas concedes: **lockup releases ("supply is undefeated?")** and a print that "may leave many key investor questions unanswered." Also logged: **"SpaceX has erased $1 trillion in market value over the past month"** (@Hedgeye, 07-22); Musk on the TSLA call — "sees more overlap between Tesla, SpaceX… can't comment on potential merger" (@zerohedge, 07-22), and a related Jonas piece **"Starlink and Tesla… Connective Tissue for Robot Swarm"** (07-24); and the [[MEDIATEK]] cross-read that a **second hyperscaler ASIC project in final design is speculated to be xAI/SpaceX** (Jefferies sales, 07-23) — unmodelled on both pages. Page has no Intra-quarter table (newly public issue), so this was filed as a standalone section per the existing convention.
